## 26. EvoMem: Self-Evolving Skills for Time-Aware Long Video Memory Agents

**中文标题**: EvoMem: Self-Evolving Skills for Time-Aware Long Video Memory Agents

**作者**: Yutong Guan, Meixing Shi, Haoran Sun, Bing Sun, Yuxiang Cai et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25889v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25889v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25889v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Although memory-augmented architectures have expanded the ability of large vision-language models (LVLMs) to process long videos, existing retrieval processes still largely rely on global semantic matching and lack state-driven temporal control, often returning fragmented or redundant evidence and failing to maintain a coherent evidence chain. To address this challenge, we propose EvoMem, a time-aware long-video memory agent guided by self-evolving retrieval skills. EvoMem constructs a hierarchical time-indexed memory, where different types of evidence are aligned onto a unified video timeline; during retrieval, temporal cues from acquired evidence are further converted into explicit signals that constrain subsequent memory access. To address recurring procedural errors in long-video question answering, such as unclear retrieval targets, improper memory access paths, or premature stopping, EvoMem introduces retrieval skills to provide process-level guidance, enabling the agent to dynamically adjust retrieval content, memory operations, and target temporal ranges according to the current evidence state, while deciding when to stop. Furthermore, inspired by reinforcement learning, we formulate skill evolution as a reward-guided policy improvement process. Each candidate skill is treated as an external policy patch, and rewards are assigned according to answer-correctness state transitions on the validation set. In this way, effective skills are accepted and low-quality skills are rejected without updating model parameters. Experiments on four long-video question answering benchmarks show that EvoMem significantly outperforms existing baselines, achieving an average accuracy of 60.1% and an average improvement of 8.6 percentage points over the previous best methods, demonstrating its effectiveness for long-video reasoning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Although memory-augmented architectures have expanded the ability of large vision-language models (LVLMs) to process long videos, existing retrieval processes still largely rely on global semantic matching and lack state-driven temporal control, often returning fragmented or redundant evidence and failing to maintain a coherent evidence chain. To address this challenge, we propose EvoMem, a time-aware long-video memory agent guided by self-evolving retrieval skills. EvoMem constructs a hierarchical time-indexed memory, where different types of evidence are aligned onto a unified video timeline; during retrieval, temporal cues from acquired evidence are further converted into explicit signals that constrain subsequent memory access. To address recurring procedural errors in long-video question answering, such as unclear retrieval targets, improper memory access paths, or premature stopping, EvoMem introduces retrieval skills to provide process-level guidance, enabling the agent to dynamically adjust retrieval content, memory operations, and target temporal ranges according to the current evidence state, while deciding when to stop. Furthermore, inspired by reinforcement learning, we formulate skill evolution as a reward-guided policy improvement process. Each candidate skill is treated as an external policy patch, and rewards are assigned according to answer-correctness state transitions on the validation set. In this way, effective skills are accepted and low-quality skills are rejected without updating model parameters. Experiments on four long-video question answering benchmarks show that EvoMem significantly outperforms existing baselines, achieving an average accuracy of 60.1% and an average improvement of 8.6 percentage points over the previous best methods, demonstrating its effectiveness for long-video reasoning.

</details>

## 27. EgoWitness: Benchmarking Instance-Level Spatial Memory and Reasoning in Egocentric Video Streams

**中文标题**: EgoWitness: Benchmarking Instance-Level Spatial Memory and Reasoning in Egocentric Video Streams

**作者**: Zhengru Fang, Zehao Liu, Mingyu Tsoi, Yaoyu Zhu, Junlong Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25849v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25849v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25849v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recalling where and when an object was last seen and who saw it requires recognizing it across days and people. Existing long-video benchmarks focus on events, object categories, or relative locations, leaving instance-level spatial memory largely untested in multimodal models. To address this gap, we present EgoWitness, a benchmark for instance-level spatial memory and reasoning in egocentric video. Its 8,453 human-verified questions span approximately 324 hours of week-long and short-horizon recordings and test seven abilities, from recognizing an object or room to recalling who saw it and reasoning about room visits. Over one million reviewed masks of 34 registered landmarks and 21 movable-object labels are linked through room timelines. Among twelve direct vision-language models (VLMs), open-weight models reach at most 46.97% accuracy, and none exceeds 35% on identifying who saw an object last. We therefore propose EgoClue, a spatial-memory agent that retrieves candidate intervals, verifies instance identity, and computes spatial and temporal relations with five tools. Two-stage training with supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) raises its 4B controller from 23.64% to 72.65% accuracy on 1,320 held-out questions. Evidence-trace synthesis matches the best direct VLM at 74.85% on week-long questions and achieves 80.56% in environments excluded from training.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recalling where and when an object was last seen and who saw it requires recognizing it across days and people. Existing long-video benchmarks focus on events, object categories, or relative locations, leaving instance-level spatial memory largely untested in multimodal models. To address this gap, we present EgoWitness, a benchmark for instance-level spatial memory and reasoning in egocentric video. Its 8,453 human-verified questions span approximately 324 hours of week-long and short-horizon recordings and test seven abilities, from recognizing an object or room to recalling who saw it and reasoning about room visits. Over one million reviewed masks of 34 registered landmarks and 21 movable-object labels are linked through room timelines. Among twelve direct vision-language models (VLMs), open-weight models reach at most 46.97% accuracy, and none exceeds 35% on identifying who saw an object last. We therefore propose EgoClue, a spatial-memory agent that retrieves candidate intervals, verifies instance identity, and computes spatial and temporal relations with five tools. Two-stage training with supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) raises its 4B controller from 23.64% to 72.65% accuracy on 1,320 held-out questions. Evidence-trace synthesis matches the best direct VLM at 74.85% on week-long questions and achieves 80.56% in environments excluded from training.

</details>

## 28. Interpretable Prototype-Conditioned Adaptation for Weather Forecasting

**中文标题**: Interpretable Prototype-Conditioned Adaptation for Weather Forecasting

**作者**: Zhixuan Li, Hao Jiang, Chen Hui, Kai Geng, Weisi Lin

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25848v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25848v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25848v1)

**项目主页**: https://zhixuanli.github.io/projects/li2026protocast

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Data-driven weather forecasting requires distinguishing local states and their temporal evolution. However, learned patterns remain implicit in neural forecasters, making their influence difficult to interpret. Failing to distinguish local evolution can lead to similar forecasts for locations that develop differently, increasing prediction error. This motivates explicit references for distinguishing and interpreting local patterns. Prototypes provide explicit pattern references, but sample- or task-level conditioning leaves open how to adapt to distinct locations and variable groups. To deal with this problem, we design ProtoCast, which uses group-specific prototype banks to adapt local features while accounting for cross-variable dependencies. Specifically, (1) we combine each group's historical features and temporal change with forecast context from cross-variable attention for evolution-aware prototype matching. (2) Sparse mixtures of prototype-specific low-rank operators tailor corrections to each group and location. A gated attention block then exchanges the corrected features across groups before prediction. Each group's correction decomposes into explicit prototype contributions before this fusion. (3) We jointly train the forecasting network and operators from scratch and refit each group's prototypes before every training epoch. Slot tracking preserves prototype-operator associations, making each prototype's contribution to local corrections more interpretable. Experiments on GlobalForecast and TyphoonTracking show improved forecasting accuracy, while prototype deletion links local feature contributions to changes in prediction error. This work advances the development of more accurate and interpretable data-driven weather forecasting models. Project page: https://zhixuanli.github.io/projects/li2026protocast

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 29. TrajCal: Trajectory-Calibrated Time Schedules for Few-Step Flow Sampling

**中文标题**: TrajCal: Trajectory-Calibrated Time Schedules for Few-Step Flow Sampling

**作者**: Junchi Zhang, Zihan Gu, Jiangcan Jia, Lulu Zhao, Wenbo Su et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25847v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25847v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25847v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Flow matching enables high-quality image and video generation, but its iterative sampling process requires repeated evaluations of large neural networks. Existing few-step methods can distort the output and reduce its agreement with a many-step reference. We propose TrajCal, a training-free calibration framework for few-step sampling. Based on scatter search, a well-established metaheuristic for difficult optimization problems, TrajCal starts with diverse seed schedules, deforms and combines these candidates, and selects one fixed schedule by final output fidelity for reuse on new inputs. For both image and video generation, TrajCal achieves higher reference fidelity than all compared baselines at low evaluation budgets while maintaining competitive visual quality. The search is effective even with randomly generated seeds, while trajectory-based seeds further improve fidelity. For cross-style generalization, photography-calibrated schedules also improve fidelity on unseen image styles.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Flow matching enables high-quality image and video generation, but its iterative sampling process requires repeated evaluations of large neural networks. Existing few-step methods can distort the output and reduce its agreement with a many-step reference. We propose TrajCal, a training-free calibration framework for few-step sampling. Based on scatter search, a well-established metaheuristic for difficult optimization problems, TrajCal starts with diverse seed schedules, deforms and combines these candidates, and selects one fixed schedule by final output fidelity for reuse on new inputs. For both image and video generation, TrajCal achieves higher reference fidelity than all compared baselines at low evaluation budgets while maintaining competitive visual quality. The search is effective even with randomly generated seeds, while trajectory-based seeds further improve fidelity. For cross-style generalization, photography-calibrated schedules also improve fidelity on unseen image styles.

</details>

## 30. ContextRenderer: Dynamic-Context Video Generation for Interactive Game Worlds

**中文标题**: ContextRenderer: Dynamic-Context Video Generation for Interactive Game Worlds

**作者**: Runze Wang, Fei Zha, Bing He, Ke Xu, Zhongyuan Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25838v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25838v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25838v1)

**项目主页**: https://rainzor.github.io/contextrenderer/

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Interactive game world models separate executable world evolution from visual generation, using video models to render engine-provided scene representations into detailed observations. During sustained gameplay, however, existing generative renderers struggle to follow changing conditions while preserving visual continuity across diverse proxies and target styles. We therefore introduce ContextRenderer, an autoregressive video diffusion framework designed for both dynamic conditioning and cross-proxy generalization. It couples Dynamic-Context Learning (DCL) for chunk-level interaction supervision with Recurrent History Compression (RHC) to follow condition updates while preserving visual continuity within a bounded token budget. Across proxy representations, Cross-Representation Flow Alignment (CRFA) connects proxy reconstruction with appearance and interaction variation through a shared high-fidelity reconstruction. WorldPrism, our interaction-centric dataset constructed from agent-built worlds, AAA gameplay, and open-source videos, supplies the corresponding renderings and chunk-level annotations for these learning tasks. Experiments on GWM-Bench and Solar-Bench show improved reconstruction and visual quality, while qualitative results demonstrate minute-long interactive rendering under changing prompts and subject references. Project page: https://rainzor.github.io/contextrenderer/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Interactive game world models separate executable world evolution from visual generation, using video models to render engine-provided scene representations into detailed observations. ## 31. What Places an Action Boundary? Structure-Aware Attribution for Temporal Action Localization

**中文标题**: What Places an Action Boundary? Structure-Aware Attribution for Temporal Action Localization

**作者**: Ruoshan Lan, Zongru Shao, Chieh Tsai, Mihai Surdeanu

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25815v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25815v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25815v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Temporal action localization (TAL) detectors must identify actions and estimate their temporal boundaries, but localization remains a major source of detection errors. While understanding what determines boundary placement requires faithful attribution of temporal evidence, existing attribution methods have not sufficiently addressed the temporal structure of TAL or the need to tailor attribution to boundary localization. We introduce a structure-aware attribution framework that averages contributions across random evidence-restoration orders following the Shapley-Shubik principle. We make it tractable by using the detector's receptive field to limit which time steps are ordered individually, and by approximating each order with a soft gradient path. For boundary predictions, we further introduce cell-structured restoration orders and boundary-anchored references that selectively remove adjacent evidence. We also adapt faithfulness evaluation to assess the joint effects of contiguous temporal evidence and the direction of boundary displacement. Experiments on THUMOS14 and EPIC-KITCHENS-100 with TriDet and ActionFormer demonstrate improved attribution faithfulness over existing methods. Attribution analysis and controlled interventions further reveal that class-score changes do not reliably track boundary displacement, providing input-level evidence that classification relies on content less informative about boundary placement. Code and evaluation artifacts will be publicly released.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Temporal action localization (TAL) detectors must identify actions and estimate their temporal boundaries, but localization remains a major source of detection errors. While understanding what determines boundary placement requires faithful attribution of temporal evidence, existing attribution methods have not sufficiently addressed the temporal structure of TAL or the need to tailor attribution to boundary localization. We introduce a structure-aware attribution framework that averages contributions across random evidence-restoration orders following the Shapley-Shubik principle. We make it tractable by using the detector's receptive field to limit which time steps are ordered individually, and by approximating each order with a soft gradient path. For boundary predictions, we further introduce cell-structured restoration orders and boundary-anchored references that selectively remove adjacent evidence. We also adapt faithfulness evaluation to assess the joint effects of contiguous temporal evidence and the direction of boundary displacement. Experiments on THUMOS14 and EPIC-KITCHENS-100 with TriDet and ActionFormer demonstrate improved attribution faithfulness over existing methods. Attribution analysis and controlled interventions further reveal that class-score changes do not reliably track boundary displacement, providing input-level evidence that classification relies on content less informative about boundary placement. Code and evaluation artifacts will be publicly released.

</details>

## 32. SkillLens: Action-Guided Dense Visual Dynamics for Proficiency Estimation

**中文标题**: SkillLens: Action-Guided Dense Visual Dynamics for Proficiency Estimation

**作者**: Deokhyun Ahn, Hyukjin Kim, Jae-Ho Han, Bumsub Ham, Heeseung Choi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25712v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25712v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25712v1)

**项目主页**: https://skill-lens.net/

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Human proficiency estimation is challenging because skill is a high-level and often subjective property that is not captured by action identity alone. Existing approaches leverage multimodal observations such as ego-exo video, gaze, pose, and inertial signals, but the gap between sensor-level evidence and abstract proficiency labels remains difficult to bridge. We present SkillLens, a proficiency estimation framework that uses atomic action descriptions as temporal guidance, rather than direct semantic supervision, to localize proficiency-relevant windows for dense visual-dynamics extraction. Each window is summarized into an action-dynamics feature and scored by a high-low prototype margin against higher- and lower-proficiency executions from the same activity-action category. Fusing these ego/exo PDM cues with a global ego-exo-gaze baseline through CORAL ordinal calibration, our method achieves 0.542 accuracy on Ego-Exo4D. Our project page is available at https://skill-lens.net/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Human proficiency estimation is challenging because skill is a high-level and often subjective property that is not captured by action identity alone. Existing approaches leverage multimodal observations such as ego-exo video, gaze, pose, and inertial signals, but the gap between sensor-level evidence and abstract proficiency labels remains difficult to bridge. We present SkillLens, a proficiency estimation framework that uses atomic action descriptions as temporal guidance, rather than direct semantic supervision, to localize proficiency-relevant windows for dense visual-dynamics extraction. Each window is summarized into an action-dynamics feature and scored by a high-low prototype margin against higher- and lower-proficiency executions from the same activity-action category. Fusing these ego/exo PDM cues with a global ego-exo-gaze baseline through CORAL ordinal calibration, our method achieves 0.542 accuracy on Ego-Exo4D. Our project page is available at https://skill-lens.net/

</details>

## 33. D3-Cache: Accelerating Diffusion Models with Dynamic Mode Decomposition

**中文标题**: D3-Cache: Accelerating Diffusion Models with Dynamic Mode Decomposition

**作者**: Yanbo Li, An Xiang, Xiaoxin Hu, Feng Wang, Cheng-zhong Xu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25689v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25689v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25689v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers generate high-quality images and videos, but every sampling step requires a full network evaluation. Training-free caching reduces this cost by reusing or forecasting network outputs across steps, but common approaches use per-model calibrated skip criteria or maintain per-layer prediction histories. We present D3-Cache, which applies Dynamic Mode Decomposition (DMD) to Diffusion sampling: it fits DMD to a short history of exact velocities and forecasts only the velocity that the scheduler consumes. A horizon-aware gate decides when to skip: whenever the network is evaluated, its output is compared with the forecast for that step, and the larger the error, the sooner the network is evaluated again, up to a cap on consecutive skipped steps. D3-Cache requires no training or per-model offline calibration curve and adds negligible memory. It shares one history length and risk tolerance across the four evaluated models. On four state-of-the-art image and video models, D3-Cache achieves speedups of 2.6-3.8x with high fidelity to the base model output.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion Transformers generate high-quality images and videos, but every sampling step requires a full network evaluation. Training-free caching reduces this cost by reusing or forecasting network outputs across steps, but common approaches use per-model calibrated skip criteria or maintain per-layer prediction histories. We present D3-Cache, which applies Dynamic Mode Decomposition (DMD) to Diffusion sampling: it fits DMD to a short history of exact velocities and forecasts only the velocity that the scheduler consumes. A horizon-aware gate decides when to skip: whenever the network is evaluated, its output is compared with the forecast for that step, and the larger the error, the sooner the network is evaluated again, up to a cap on consecutive skipped steps. D3-Cache requires no training or per-model offline calibration curve and adds negligible memory. It shares one history length and risk tolerance across the four evaluated models. On four state-of-the-art image and video models, D3-Cache achieves speedups of 2.6-3.8x with high fidelity to the base model output.

</details>

## 34. MambaLIFT: Reference-Based Mechanism-Aware Attribution for VMamba

**中文标题**: MambaLIFT: Reference-Based Mechanism-Aware Attribution for VMamba

**作者**: Jintu Kakati, Niladri B. Puhan

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25644v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25644v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25644v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CV, eess.IV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Attributing predictions of visual state-space models remains challenging because input-dependent selective dynamics couple feature selection, recurrent state propagation, and shared computational pathways. We introduce MambaLIFT, a reference-based, multiplier-first attribution framework for VMamba that propagates contributions through its computational graph using interaction-aware rules for selective-scan injection, recurrent memory, LayerNorm, and shared pathways. MambaLIFT decomposes the reference-relative class-logit difference, \(f_c(x)-f_c(x^0)\), by spatial position, VSS block, scan direction, and computational mechanism. Experiments on ImageNet-1K evaluate VMamba-T and VMamba-B, with hyperparameters selected on a calibration set and faithfulness assessed on a disjoint held-out evaluation set. MambaLIFT achieves numerical conservation to floating-point precision across audited attribution interfaces in both configurations. Under mean-colour pixel flipping, it achieves significantly higher mean faithfulness scores than MambaLRP on both configurations and the highest scores among the evaluated methods, including Integrated Gradients with matched references. Under blurred replacement, MambaLIFT is non-inferior to MambaLRP on VMamba-T and equivalent on VMamba-B, indicating that its advantage depends on the replacement protocol. Ablations on VMamba-T show that interaction allocation, temporal propagation, and pathway composition affect attribution maps. Mechanism-level analyses further show that exact relevance conservation does not imply additive effects under finite interventions: the mechanism decomposition accounts for the reference-relative logit difference but does not estimate intervention effects or establish causal validity.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Attributing predictions of visual state-space models remains challenging because input-dependent selective dynamics couple feature selection, recurrent state propagation, and shared computational pathways. We introduce MambaLIFT, a reference-based, multiplier-first attribution framework for VMamba that propagates contributions through its computational graph using interaction-aware rules for selective-scan injection, recurrent memory, LayerNorm, and shared pathways. MambaLIFT decomposes the reference-relative class-logit difference, \(f_c(x)-f_c(x^0)\), by spatial position, VSS block, scan direction, and computational mechanism. Experiments on ImageNet-1K evaluate VMamba-T and VMamba-B, with hyperparameters selected on a calibration set and faithfulness assessed on a disjoint held-out evaluation set. MambaLIFT achieves numerical conservation to floating-point precision across audited attribution interfaces in both configurations. Under mean-colour pixel flipping, it achieves significantly higher mean faithfulness scores than MambaLRP on both configurations and the highest scores among the evaluated methods, including Integrated Gradients with matched references. Under blurred replacement, MambaLIFT is non-inferior to MambaLRP on VMamba-T and equivalent on VMamba-B, indicating that its advantage depends on the replacement protocol. Ablations on VMamba-T show that interaction allocation, temporal propagation, and pathway composition affect attribution maps. Mechanism-level analyses further show that exact relevance conservation does not imply additive effects under finite interventions: the mechanism decomposition accounts for the reference-relative logit difference but does not estimate intervention effects or establish causal validity.

</details>

## 35. What One Sample Cannot See: Why Fewer Denoising Steps Can Score Higher on Physics Benchmarks

**中文标题**: What One Sample Cannot See: Why Fewer Denoising Steps Can Score Higher on Physics Benchmarks

**作者**: Yutao Li, Chen Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25632v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25632v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25632v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

When a storm can head north or south, a forecast down the middle may score better under squared error than either plausible path. The same paradox appears when video generators are tested as world models: fewer denoising steps can raise physics scores even as videos blur or stop moving early. Why? Physics-IQ compares each generated video with one recording, although a scene can have multiple valid futures. An exact decomposition shows its scores can reward reduced variation among generated futures, even when their distribution becomes less accurate. We introduce Kernel Physics-IQ to remove this incentive in expectation, needing only two independent samples per condition. Two-step sampling lowers Kernel Physics-IQ in all five image-to-video models. For CogVideoX, the official score rises by 0.055 while ours falls by 0.131. Changing the sampling schedule reveals further differences official scores miss. In controlled physical systems with known outcome probabilities, our metric favors the true distribution over collapsed predictions. In short, a world model should be judged not only by how closely one video matches what happened, but by how faithfully its predictions represent what could have happened.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

When a storm can head north or south, a forecast down the middle may score better under squared error than either plausible path. The same paradox appears when video generators are tested as world models: fewer denoising steps can raise physics scores even as videos blur or stop moving early. Why? Physics-IQ compares each generated video with one recording, although a scene can have multiple valid futures. An exact decomposition shows its scores can reward reduced variation among generated futures, even when their distribution becomes less accurate. We introduce Kernel Physics-IQ to remove this incentive in expectation, needing only two independent samples per condition. Two-step sampling lowers Kernel Physics-IQ in all five image-to-video models. For CogVideoX, the official score rises by 0.055 while ours falls by 0.131. Changing the sampling schedule reveals further differences official scores miss. In controlled physical systems with known outcome probabilities, our metric favors the true distribution over collapsed predictions. In short, a world model should be judged not only by how closely one video matches what happened, but by how faithfully its predictions represent what could have happened.

</details>

## 36. Physical Laws without Physics Loss? Probing Learned Dynamics in Video World Models through Forced Response

**中文标题**: Physical Laws without Physics Loss? Probing Learned Dynamics in Video World Models through Forced Response

**作者**: Yutao Li, Chen Wang, Wangmeng Zuo, Hui Li

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25626v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25626v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25626v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Do video world models learn physical laws without a physics loss, or merely imitate motion? Overall motion and appearance scores cannot settle it. We investigate this through nonlinear forced response: an excitation shakes the support of a suspended object. We introduce dual visual-numeric conditioning and adapt six image-to-video backbones to predict this motion using only the flow-matching loss. Wan predicts motion accurately and generalizes to unseen excitations, objects, poses, and scenes. Counterfactual tests suggest capabilities beyond replaying training videos. But what laws have they learned? We design three tiers of tests using Taylor expansions of the dynamics and geometry. All six reproduce the linear response, including resonance; five capture higher-order dynamics, including nonlinear softening. Remarkably, softening is barely present in training: only 0.74% of the swing's squared displacement. Extensions to a double pendulum and a rocking block show sustained dynamics are learned more faithfully than brief impacts. Yet learned dynamics may fail to appear when state estimation or propagation goes wrong. We turn these tests and findings into strategies to improve video training and generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Do video world models learn physical laws without a physics loss, or merely imitate motion? Overall motion and appearance scores cannot settle it. We investigate this through nonlinear forced response: an excitation shakes the support of a suspended object. We introduce dual visual-numeric conditioning and adapt six image-to-video backbones to predict this motion using only the flow-matching loss. Wan predicts motion accurately and generalizes to unseen excitations, objects, poses, and scenes. Counterfactual tests suggest capabilities beyond replaying training videos. But what laws have they learned? We design three tiers of tests using Taylor expansions of the dynamics and geometry. All six reproduce the linear response, including resonance; five capture higher-order dynamics, including nonlinear softening. Remarkably, softening is barely present in training: only 0.74% of the swing's squared displacement. Extensions to a double pendulum and a rocking block show sustained dynamics are learned more faithfully than brief impacts. Yet learned dynamics may fail to appear when state estimation or propagation goes wrong. We turn these tests and findings into strategies to improve video training and generation.

</details>

## 37. CaT-Movie: Balancing Global Planning and Local Rendering for Long Multi-Shot Video Generation

**中文标题**: CaT-Movie: Balancing Global Planning and Local Rendering for Long Multi-Shot Video Generation

**作者**: Jinpeng Lu, Haoyuan Shi, Yuanshen Guan, Jiyuan Xia, Bin Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25620v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25620v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25620v1)

**项目主页**: https://jinplu.github.io/CaT-Movie/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Current multi-shot video generators render only limited durations per pass, requiring long movies to be stitched from local segments across which narrative progression and visual identity drift. To synthesize long-horizon movies with Coherence across Time, we present CaT-Movie, a multi-shot generation system consisting of a global story planner and a local window renderer. The planner expands user requests via a large language model to preserve, infer, and complete story details, while deterministic code maintains the resulting storyboard across shots to compile self-contained shot prompts and index reference frames from generated history. To execute this guidance, the renderer applies reference-routed sparse self-attention and shot-wise cross-attention to route identities from the indexed frames while isolating per-shot textual conditions. Given the scarcity of high-quality open-source multi-shot data, we construct a dataset of over 350K film shots annotated with semantic state transitions, entity grounding, and multi-granularity requests. We further build CaT-MBench, a benchmark on held-out films with the same annotations. It gives every baseline its native format and counts cross-shot consistency only when a cut opens a new camera setup, preventing repeated framing from passing as consistency. On CaT-MBench, CaT-Movie attains the highest average score and the most consistent characters among all methods. The project page is available at https://jinplu.github.io/CaT-Movie/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Current multi-shot video generators render only limited durations per pass, requiring long movies to be stitched from local segments across which narrative progression and visual identity drift. To synthesize long-horizon movies with Coherence across Time, we present CaT-Movie, a multi-shot generation system consisting of a global story planner and a local window renderer. The planner expands user requests via a large language model to preserve, infer, and complete story details, while deterministic code maintains the resulting storyboard across shots to compile self-contained shot prompts and index reference frames from generated history. To execute this guidance, the renderer applies reference-routed sparse self-attention and shot-wise cross-attention to route identities from the indexed frames while isolating per-shot textual conditions. Given the scarcity of high-quality open-source multi-shot data, we construct a dataset of over 350K film shots annotated with semantic state transitions, entity grounding, and multi-granularity requests. We further build CaT-MBench, a benchmark on held-out films with the same annotations. It gives every baseline its native format and counts cross-shot consistency only when a cut opens a new camera setup, preventing repeated framing from passing as consistency. On CaT-MBench, CaT-Movie attains the highest average score and the most consistent characters among all methods. The project page is available at https://jinplu.github.io/CaT-Movie/.

</details>

## 38. LoopedWAM: Looped World Action Models for Elastic Inference

**中文标题**: LoopedWAM: Looped World Action Models for Elastic Inference

**作者**: Anh Dao, Xinxin Liu, Hang Yu, Jeffrey A Chan Santiago, Yuzhang Shang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25595v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25595v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25595v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) couple generative video modeling with robotic action prediction but typically require large video and action transformers, limiting deployment efficiency. We present LoopedWAM, a compact WAM that recurrently applies a shared six-block core in each expert, executing 30 transformer layers while storing only 12 blocks per expert. To account for their asymmetric inference costs, the video and action experts can operate at different depths: visual features are computed once per query, whereas action computation repeats at every sampling step. We further propose elastic self-distillation, which trains a single elastic checkpoint, LoopedWAM-E, across action-depth and sampling-step budgets. With only 0.58B policy parameters and no robot pretraining, LoopedWAM achieves 96.1% success on LIBERO, 91.9% on RoboTwin 2.0, and 52.2% on RoboCasaGR1, while using $7.4\times$ less peak GPU memory than Fast-WAM under matched inference measurements. On LIBERO, LoopedWAM-E achieves 96.0% success in 27.6 ms or 95.2% in 24.7 ms on an H100, providing multiple accuracy-latency operating points from a single checkpoint; with two sampling steps and four action loops, it stays within 0.5 points of LoopedWAM on all three benchmarks. Ablations demonstrate that weight-shared depth preserves competitive performance, while aggressive action-depth reduction requires explicit budget-aware training. On a real AgileX PiPER arm, LoopedWAM succeeds in 71.3% of 80 trials across four tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World action models (WAMs) couple generative video modeling with robotic action prediction but typically require large video and action transformers, limiting deployment efficiency. We present LoopedWAM, a compact WAM that recurrently applies a shared six-block core in each expert, executing 30 transformer layers while storing only 12 blocks per expert. To account for their asymmetric inference costs, the video and action experts can operate at different depths: visual features are computed once per query, whereas action computation repeats at every sampling step. We further propose elastic self-distillation, which trains a single elastic checkpoint, LoopedWAM-E, across action-depth and sampling-step budgets. With only 0.58B policy parameters and no robot pretraining, LoopedWAM achieves 96.1% success on LIBERO, 91.9% on RoboTwin 2.0, and 52.2% on RoboCasaGR1, while using $7.4\times$ less peak GPU memory than Fast-WAM under matched inference measurements. On LIBERO, LoopedWAM-E achieves 96.0% success in 27.6 ms or 95.2% in 24.7 ms on an H100, providing multiple accuracy-latency operating points from a single checkpoint; with two sampling steps and four action loops, it stays within 0.5 points of LoopedWAM on all three benchmarks. Ablations demonstrate that weight-shared depth preserves competitive performance, while aggressive action-depth reduction requires explicit budget-aware training. On a real AgileX PiPER arm, LoopedWAM succeeds in 71.3% of 80 trials across four tasks.

</details>

## 39. VidToC: Rethinking Video Token Compression for Multimodal Large Language Models

**中文标题**: VidToC: Rethinking Video Token Compression for Multimodal Large Language Models

**作者**: Zihan Xiao, Boshen Xu, Pei Fu, Jianzhong Ju, Zhenbo Luo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25574v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25574v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25574v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Extending multimodal large language models (MLLMs) from images to videos faces substantial memory and latency challenges due to the massive number of visual tokens. These challenges have driven the development of numerous visual token compression methods and more efficient LLM architectures. However, existing compression methods are often evaluated with different training setups, tasks, and models, making fair comparison difficult. Meanwhile, the growing use of hybrid Mamba-Transformer architectures raises questions about whether compression strategies transfer across backbones. We present VidToC, a controlled evaluation framework for representative compression algorithms, LLM backbones, and time encoding strategies, covering training-free and fine-tuned settings on three complementary video understanding tasks. Our results reveal task-dependent compression behavior and characterize the effects of compression placement, time encoding, and backbone choice. These findings provide empirical guidance for designing and selecting video token compression strategies across tasks and training settings.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Extending multimodal large language models (MLLMs) from images to videos faces substantial memory and latency challenges due to the massive number of visual tokens. These challenges have driven the development of numerous visual token compression methods and more efficient LLM architectures. However, existing compression methods are often evaluated with different training setups, tasks, and models, making fair comparison difficult. Meanwhile, the growing use of hybrid Mamba-Transformer architectures raises questions about whether compression strategies transfer across backbones. We present VidToC, a controlled evaluation framework for representative compression algorithms, LLM backbones, and time encoding strategies, covering training-free and fine-tuned settings on three complementary video understanding tasks. Our results reveal task-dependent compression behavior and characterize the effects of compression placement, time encoding, and backbone choice. These findings provide empirical guidance for designing and selecting video token compression strategies across tasks and training settings.

</details>

## 40. Online Mistake Detection in Instruction-Conditioned Procedural Video

**中文标题**: Online Mistake Detection in Instruction-Conditioned Procedural Video

**作者**: Amit Feldman, Ilan Shimshoni, Rotem Dror

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25567v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25567v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25567v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

An assistant monitoring a person performing a procedure is most useful when it can detect mistakes while there is still time to recover. This requires judging actions against task instructions as the video unfolds, identifying the attempted step, locating it in time, and assessing its execution. Existing online detectors do not use the instruction text, while instruction-conditioned methods operate offline. In this paper, we study online, instruction-conditioned mistake detection in egocentric videos. Using only observed video, task instructions, and prior predictions, our model identifies and localizes completed action attempts and classifies them as correct, modified, slip, corrections, or additions; omitted steps are determined after the video ends. We compare a modular pipeline, with separate step identification, temporal localization, and execution verification, to a joint model that predicts all outputs in a single step. To support supervised learning from mistakes, we repartition EgoPER to include erroneous videos in all splits. The approaches achieve similar step-segmentation F1@0.5 (93.6% and 92.1%), while the joint model achieves higher binary mistake-detection F1@0.5 (84.1% vs. 80.7%), with its largest gain on slips (56.4% vs. 37.5%). Component-level analysis suggests that error propagation between stages contributes to the modular approach's lower detection score. On test videos containing mistakes, both designs outperform released AEM and ESTANet checkpoints under our evaluation protocol.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

An assistant monitoring a person performing a procedure is most useful when it can detect mistakes while there is still time to recover. This requires judging actions against task instructions as the video unfolds, identifying the attempted step, locating it in time, and assessing its execution. Existing online detectors do not use the instruction text, while instruction-conditioned methods operate offline. In this paper, we study online, instruction-conditioned mistake detection in egocentric videos. Using only observed video, task instructions, and prior predictions, our model identifies and localizes completed action attempts and classifies them as correct, modified, slip, corrections, or additions; omitted steps are determined after the video ends. We compare a modular pipeline, with separate step identification, temporal localization, and execution verification, to a joint model that predicts all outputs in a single step. To support supervised learning from mistakes, we repartition EgoPER to include erroneous videos in all splits. The approaches achieve similar step-segmentation F1@0.5 (93.6% and 92.1%), while the joint model achieves higher binary mistake-detection F1@0.5 (84.1% vs. 80.7%), with its largest gain on slips (56.4% vs. 37.5%). Component-level analysis suggests that error propagation between stages contributes to the modular approach's lower detection score. On test videos containing mistakes, both designs outperform released AEM and ESTANet checkpoints under our evaluation protocol.

</details>

## 41. TwoPass: Draft-Guided Few-Step Video Prediction for Robotic Manipulation

**中文标题**: TwoPass: Draft-Guided Few-Step Video Prediction for Robotic Manipulation

**作者**: Yijun Liu, Shijia Ge, Tongtong Feng, Xin Wang, Yuan Meng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25553v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25553v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25553v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/PlantPotatoOnMoon/TwoPass.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Action-conditioned video prediction lets robots anticipate the visual consequences of their actions for planning and policy evaluation. Existing predictors denoise each future from noise without using their own estimate of it, which limits the quality of the predicted future, in particular of the manipulated objects, and requires many network calls. We present TwoPass, a self-conditioned training-and-inference framework that keeps the standard flow-matching objective. Self-Generated Draft Training supplies a detached clean-video estimate from the shared network as conditioning at the same noisy state. A Refinement-Only Late Adapter applies a dedicated residual transformation when a draft is present. At inference, Draft-Reuse Iterative Refinement obtains both a solver update and the next draft from each velocity prediction, requiring as few as 4 function evaluations (NFE) per video chunk. Training samples discrete solver-query noise levels and updates the pretrained backbone. Extensive experiments on Bridge and Language Table show that TwoPass achieves state-of-the-art results with 4 NFE, far fewer than the 50-59 calls of existing predictors. Code: https://github.com/PlantPotatoOnMoon/TwoPass.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Action-conditioned video prediction lets robots anticipate the visual consequences of their actions for planning and policy evaluation. Existing predictors denoise each future from noise without using their own estimate of it, which limits the quality of the predicted future, in particular of the manipulated objects, and requires many network calls. We present TwoPass, a self-conditioned training-and-inference framework that keeps the standard flow-matching objective. Self-Generated Draft Training supplies a detached clean-video estimate from the shared network as conditioning at the same noisy state. A Refinement-Only Late Adapter applies a dedicated residual transformation when a draft is present. At inference, Draft-Reuse Iterative Refinement obtains both a solver update and the next draft from each velocity prediction, requiring as few as 4 function evaluations (NFE) per video chunk. Training samples discrete solver-query noise levels and updates the pretrained backbone. Extensive experiments on Bridge and Language Table show that TwoPass achieves state-of-the-art results with 4 NFE, far fewer than the 50-59 calls of existing predictors. Code: https://github.com/PlantPotatoOnMoon/TwoPass.

</details>

---

## 42. TuringPAI: A Foundation for On-Device Physical AI

**中文标题**: TuringPAI: A Foundation for On-Device Physical AI

**作者**: Qiman Wu, Hanlin Chen, Yuhua Wei, Mingyuan Wang, Shaman Tang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25474v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25474v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25474v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Physical AI requires models that understand spatial relationships and dynamic environments and translate observations into actions under limited onboard compute. We present TuringPAI, a family of models for on-device Physical AI comprising TuringPAI-VL for shared vision-language understanding, TuringPAI-Drive for autonomous driving, and TuringPAI-Embodied for robotic manipulation. TuringPAI-VL is a 0.65B model built on HybridViT-MLQ, which combines convolutional local aggregation with Transformer-based global modeling. Multi-scale Latent Queries integrate spatial features and observation history within the same visual Transformer, providing a compact, structured interface to the language model. Training combines general vision-language data with geometric, cross-view, and temporal supervision. TuringPAI-VL supports general multimodal understanding and improves quantitative geometric perception over Qwen2-VL-2B, while providing a shared initialization for the two downstream VLA models. For autonomous driving, TuringPAI-Drive maintains all evaluated ADE/FDE metrics within 1.2\% of a full-token HybridViT baseline using the MLQ interface and achieves comparable aggregate scores in an in-house simulation evaluation. Using XPeng Native Runtime (XNRT) on a single NVIDIA Orin-X SoC, TuringPAI-Drive generates trajectories at 12\,Hz in on-vehicle operation. For robotic manipulation, TuringPAI-Embodied achieves a 96.8\% average success rate across the four LIBERO suites. These results demonstrate a shared vision-language foundation for driving and robotic manipulation under practical on-device constraints.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Physical AI requires models that understand spatial relationships and dynamic environments and translate observations into actions under limited onboard compute. We present TuringPAI, a family of models for on-device Physical AI comprising TuringPAI-VL for shared vision-language understanding, TuringPAI-Drive for autonomous driving, and TuringPAI-Embodied for robotic manipulation. TuringPAI-VL is a 0.65B model built on HybridViT-MLQ, which combines convolutional local aggregation with Transformer-based global modeling. Multi-scale Latent Queries integrate spatial features and observation history within the same visual Transformer, providing a compact, structured interface to the language model. Training combines general vision-language data with geometric, cross-view, and temporal supervision. TuringPAI-VL supports general multimodal understanding and improves quantitative geometric perception over Qwen2-VL-2B, while providing a shared initialization for the two downstream VLA models. For autonomous driving, TuringPAI-Drive maintains all evaluated ADE/FDE metrics within 1.2\% of a full-token HybridViT baseline using the MLQ interface and achieves comparable aggregate scores in an in-house simulation evaluation. Using XPeng Native Runtime (XNRT) on a single NVIDIA Orin-X SoC, TuringPAI-Drive generates trajectories at 12\,Hz in on-vehicle operation. For robotic manipulation, TuringPAI-Embodied achieves a 96.8\% average success rate across the four LIBERO suites. These results demonstrate a shared vision-language foundation for driving and robotic manipulation under practical on-device constraints.

</details>

## 43. ActMem: From Latent Memory Reasoning to Grounded Robotic Action

**中文标题**: ActMem: From Latent Memory Reasoning to Grounded Robotic Action

**作者**: Linfeng Dong, Jianwei Niu, Jiuyi Zhao, Ruibing Feng, Xinghao Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25461v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25461v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25461v1)

**项目主页**: https://huggingface.co/donk111/ActMem.

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/LigphiDonk/ActMem

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have shown promising results in real-world long-horizon manipulation, where memory plays a crucial role.   However, existing memory-augmented VLAs either retrieve past observations without reasoning about what they imply, or reason over the full   history in a shared context where it crowds out current perception. Cognitive science treats them as distinct systems: memory reconstructs past   experience to decide what to do, while perception guides how to act. We therefore propose ActMem, an in-context memory framework that   decouples memory reasoning from perceptual grounding within a single vision-language backbone. Built on a vision-language model with native   video understanding, ActMem infers the current subtask from a timestamped interaction history and keeps the hidden states formed during this   inference as a Latent Memory Chain-of-Thought (Memory CoT), which conditions a flow-matching action expert. Perception Attention further lets   the expert attend directly to the current view. To keep inference fast under long histories, Efficient Memory CoT Inference reuses computation   across consecutive decisions without changing the output. ActMem achieves 71.1% task success and 80.8% completion success on RoboMemArena,   surpassing the strongest baseline by 7.5 and 8.7 percentage points, and 73.8% average success on MIKASA-Robo, the best among all compared   methods. In the real world, it reaches 68.8% average success on exogenous, endogenous, and traceless memory tasks, 10.0 percentage points above   the strongest memory-augmented VLA. Code and checkpoints will be released at https://github.com/LigphiDonk/ActMem and   https://huggingface.co/donk111/ActMem.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have shown promising results in real-world long-horizon manipulation, where memory plays a crucial role.   However, existing memory-augmented VLAs either retrieve past observations without reasoning about what they imply, or reason over the full   history in a shared context where it crowds out current perception. Cognitive science treats them as distinct systems: memory reconstructs past   experience to decide what to do, while perception guides how to act. We therefore propose ActMem, an in-context memory framework that   decouples memory reasoning from perceptual grounding within a single vision-language backbone. Built on a vision-language model with native   video understanding, ActMem infers the current subtask from a timestamped interaction history and keeps the hidden states formed during this   inference as a Latent Memory Chain-of-Thought (Memory CoT), which conditions a flow-matching action expert. Perception Attention further lets   the expert attend directly to the current view. To keep inference fast under long histories, Efficient Memory CoT Inference reuses computation   across consecutive decisions without changing the output. ActMem achieves 71.1% task success and 80.8% completion success on RoboMemArena,   surpassing the strongest baseline by 7.5 and 8.7 percentage points, and 73.8% average success on MIKASA-Robo, the best among all compared   methods. In the real world, it reaches 68.8% average success on exogenous, endogenous, and traceless memory tasks, 10.0 percentage points above   the strongest memory-augmented VLA. Code and checkpoints will be released at https://github.com/LigphiDonk/ActMem and   https://huggingface.co/donk111/ActMem.

</details>

## 44. Stereo Visual Odometry with In-the-Loop Geometric Feed-Forward Models Towards Dense Metric Mapping

**中文标题**: Stereo Visual Odometry with In-the-Loop Geometric Feed-Forward Models Towards Dense Metric Mapping

**作者**: Fahira Afzal Maken, Sagun Man Singh Shrestha, Edward Vanderfeen, Lucas Carvalho de Lima, Rongxin Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25445v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25445v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25445v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present a stereo visual odometry (SVO) framework that integrates geometric feed-forward models (GFMs) for dense depth prediction with factor-graph-based visual odometry to achieve accurate localisation and dense metric mapping. Our key idea is to exploit sparse but reliable metric geometry estimated from stereo and temporal multi-view observations to calibrate dense GFM predictions, while retaining the robustness and efficiency of model-based SVO. Rather than directly conditioning the GFM on potentially noisy stereo depth, optimised 3D landmarks are used to jointly estimate a scale-shift transformation over all keyframes within each local submap, yielding metrically consistent dense depth predictions. To balance accuracy and computational efficiency, we introduce a hierarchical factor-graph architecture. At the local level, sliding-window optimisation estimates high-frequency camera poses and multi-view-refined 3D landmarks. Dense predictions are generated only for selected keyframes and registered within each submap using SE(3) Generalised ICP. Consecutive submaps are further aligned in Sim(3) using their shared geometry, allowing residual scale inconsistencies to be corrected. These intra- and inter-submap constraints are incorporated into a global factor graph to produce a consistent trajectory and dense metric reconstruction. Extensive experiments on public and in-house datasets demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves competitive odometry accuracy against geometric and learning-based baselines while providing accurate dense metric reconstruction and improved robustness to failures in learned depth prediction.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present a stereo visual odometry (SVO) framework that integrates geometric feed-forward models (GFMs) for dense depth prediction with factor-graph-based visual odometry to achieve accurate localisation and dense metric mapping. Our key idea is to exploit sparse but reliable metric geometry estimated from stereo and temporal multi-view observations to calibrate dense GFM predictions, while retaining the robustness and efficiency of model-based SVO. Rather than directly conditioning the GFM on potentially noisy stereo depth, optimised 3D landmarks are used to jointly estimate a scale-shift transformation over all keyframes within each local submap, yielding metrically consistent dense depth predictions. To balance accuracy and computational efficiency, we introduce a hierarchical factor-graph architecture. At the local level, sliding-window optimisation estimates high-frequency camera poses and multi-view-refined 3D landmarks. Dense predictions are generated only for selected keyframes and registered within each submap using SE(3) Generalised ICP. Consecutive submaps are further aligned in Sim(3) using their shared geometry, allowing residual scale inconsistencies to be corrected. These intra- and inter-submap constraints are incorporated into a global factor graph to produce a consistent trajectory and dense metric reconstruction. Extensive experiments on public and in-house datasets demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves competitive odometry accuracy against geometric and learning-based baselines while providing accurate dense metric reconstruction and improved robustness to failures in learned depth prediction.

</details>

## 45. VisualTalk-Bench: Evaluating Question-Driven Avatar-Led Video Explanations

**中文标题**: VisualTalk-Bench: Evaluating Question-Driven Avatar-Led Video Explanations

**作者**: Xin Zhang, Tujie Xu, Zhiyi Chen, Xiaodong Zhang, Sicheng Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25444v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25444v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25444v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Advances in video generation and avatar synthesis enable new ways to answer user questions through visual explanations. This task requires a system to formulate a correct answer, translate relevant information into visual material, and deliver the explanation through an avatar's speech and actions. Existing benchmarks rarely assess whether avatars point to the visual content being discussed. We introduce VisualTalk-Bench for question-driven avatar-led video explanations in finance and Chinese and English education. It includes 800 curated reference videos and 100 independent evaluation requests, assessing Content Factuality, Speech-Visual Consistency, Visual Quality, Motion Smoothness, and Avatar-Content Pointing. Yet end-to-end video generators often struggle to align gestures with specific visual targets. To explore this challenge, we develop Avatar-O Agent, a modular reference implementation with explicit interaction planning. Experiments across six agents show that factual precision and visual quality do not guarantee accurate pointing. A 26-participant study comparing four agents finds that Avatar-O Agent receives the highest average ratings for visual guidance, perceived pointing accuracy, and explanation effectiveness. We will release the data and code.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Advances in video generation and avatar synthesis enable new ways to answer user questions through visual explanations. This task requires a system to formulate a correct answer, translate relevant information into visual material, and deliver the explanation through an avatar's speech and actions. Existing benchmarks rarely assess whether avatars point to the visual content being discussed. We introduce VisualTalk-Bench for question-driven avatar-led video explanations in finance and Chinese and English education. It includes 800 curated reference videos and 100 independent evaluation requests, assessing Content Factuality, Speech-Visual Consistency, Visual Quality, Motion Smoothness, and Avatar-Content Pointing. Yet end-to-end video generators often struggle to align gestures with specific visual targets. To explore this challenge, we develop Avatar-O Agent, a modular reference implementation with explicit interaction planning. Experiments across six agents show that factual precision and visual quality do not guarantee accurate pointing. A 26-participant study comparing four agents finds that Avatar-O Agent receives the highest average ratings for visual guidance, perceived pointing accuracy, and explanation effectiveness. We will release the data and code.

</details>

## 46. Zero to Four Views: Unified Panorama Generation and Preference RL for Unobserved Regions

**中文标题**: Zero to Four Views: Unified Panorama Generation and Preference RL for Unobserved Regions

**作者**: Rui Hong, Xugang Ye, Jana Košecká, Kai Fan

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25389v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25389v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25389v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Panoramas can serve as shared scene backgrounds for multi-shot video generation, but most existing panorama generators take text alone or a single input image. We present a single model that generates a full equirectangular panorama from a text caption and K in {0,...,4} perspective conditioning views taken by a four-camera rig at one viewpoint. We fine-tune a pretrained text-to-image rectified-flow transformer in two stages. In the first stage, each conditioning view is appended to the token sequence together with the ID of its camera slot, and the number of views varies during training, so one set of weights serves every input setting. In the second stage, we improve the region not covered by any conditioning view. Supervised training computes its loss on noisy latents of the ground truth and never scores a panorama the model has generated and decoded, so we propose Uncovered-Region GRPO (UR-GRPO), a reinforcement-learning recipe that rewards the candidates a vision-language model prefers in pairwise comparisons on crops around this region. Because the slot ID alone does not tell the model which way the rig points, we also offer an optional variant that writes each view's camera direction into its position encoding, which lets the user decide where each view appears in the output. With one conditioning view, our model outperforms the previous state of the art, 360Anything, trained on the same data and evaluated with the same code, on Laval Indoor and gives mixed results on an indoor subset of SUN360; the second stage improves the results for every number of conditioning views.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Panoramas can serve as shared scene backgrounds for multi-shot video generation, but most existing panorama generators take text alone or a single input image. We present a single model that generates a full equirectangular panorama from a text caption and K in {0,...,4} perspective conditioning views taken by a four-camera rig at one viewpoint. We fine-tune a pretrained text-to-image rectified-flow transformer in two stages. In the first stage, each conditioning view is appended to the token sequence together with the ID of its camera slot, and the number of views varies during training, so one set of weights serves every input setting. In the second stage, we improve the region not covered by any conditioning view. Supervised training computes its loss on noisy latents of the ground truth and never scores a panorama the model has generated and decoded, so we propose Uncovered-Region GRPO (UR-GRPO), a reinforcement-learning recipe that rewards the candidates a vision-language model prefers in pairwise comparisons on crops around this region. Because the slot ID alone does not tell the model which way the rig points, we also offer an optional variant that writes each view's camera direction into its position encoding, which lets the user decide where each view appears in the output. With one conditioning view, our model outperforms the previous state of the art, 360Anything, trained on the same data and evaluated with the same code, on Laval Indoor and gives mixed results on an indoor subset of SUN360; the second stage improves the results for every number of conditioning views.

</details>

## 47. SceneAnything: Agentic Reconstruction of Large-Scale Mesh Scenes from a Single Video

**中文标题**: SceneAnything: Agentic Reconstruction of Large-Scale Mesh Scenes from a Single Video

**作者**: Bowen Xue, Saeed Hadadan, Fabrice Rousselle, Zahra Montazeri, Ravi Ramamoorthi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25340v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25340v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25340v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reconstructing an editable 3D scene from video requires matching visible structure while plausibly completing unseen surfaces. Agents can write scene-construction programs, but direct reconstruction attempts often produce errors in layout, scale, and lighting. We introduce SceneAnything, an automated system for reconstructing mesh scenes from real and generated videos, enabling text-to-scene construction when paired with video generation. A software harness coordinates agents, pretrained vision models, and graphics tools through an explicit reconstruction protocol. Shared camera, depth, and segmentation estimates ground component construction: a scene-owner agent plans the environment and briefs authors, who write parameterized Blender programs. A visibility- and fit-based level-of-detail rule selects the construction level, while unseen regions are completed and marked as inferred. Assembled scenes use fitted lighting and supported motion, and revisions undergo programmatic checks and independent visual comparisons. Accepted programs and lessons are reused across scenes. We evaluate on 100 scenes; subset comparisons show improved original-view fidelity over direct-agent reconstruction and higher novel-view content similarity than the compared video-to-3D methods. We will release the harness, agent instructions, reusable libraries, and scenes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reconstructing an editable 3D scene from video requires matching visible structure while plausibly completing unseen surfaces. Agents can write scene-construction programs, but direct reconstruction attempts often produce errors in layout, scale, and lighting. We introduce SceneAnything, an automated system for reconstructing mesh scenes from real and generated videos, enabling text-to-scene construction when paired with video generation. A software harness coordinates agents, pretrained vision models, and graphics tools through an explicit reconstruction protocol. Shared camera, depth, and segmentation estimates ground component construction: a scene-owner agent plans the environment and briefs authors, who write parameterized Blender programs. A visibility- and fit-based level-of-detail rule selects the construction level, while unseen regions are completed and marked as inferred. Assembled scenes use fitted lighting and supported motion, and revisions undergo programmatic checks and independent visual comparisons. Accepted programs and lessons are reused across scenes. We evaluate on 100 scenes; subset comparisons show improved original-view fidelity over direct-agent reconstruction and higher novel-view content similarity than the compared video-to-3D methods. We will release the harness, agent instructions, reusable libraries, and scenes.

</details>

## 48. VideoRNG: Relightable Neural Gaussians from Generative Video Models

**中文标题**: VideoRNG: Relightable Neural Gaussians from Generative Video Models

**作者**: Bowen Xue, Saeed Hadadan, Fabrice Rousselle, Ravi Ramamoorthi, Zahra Montazeri et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25338v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25338v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25338v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Generating realistic 3D assets remains constrained by classical representations: surface meshes and compact physically based materials struggle to express objects whose appearance arises from fine geometry or complex light transport, while existing Gaussian-splatting generators typically bake fixed illumination into appearance. We introduce VideoRNG, a two-stage method that generates complete, relightable 3D assets from a text prompt or a single image without prescribing a mesh or analytic material model. A video generator fine-tuned from a pretrained video diffusion backbone first produces a structured RGBA turntable sequence under a known pose, camera, and illumination. A feed-forward large reconstruction model initialized from a pretrained video diffusion backbone then maps the video-VAE latent frames of selected turntable frames directly to millions of anisotropic Gaussians with learned neural appearance features. A shared directional decoder and stochastic Gaussian ray tracer turn this representation, which we call ray-traced Relightable Neural Gaussians (rtRNG), into radiance under novel viewpoints and illumination. To preserve high-frequency appearance while learning geometry, we introduce depth-anchored appearance training, and we make million-Gaussian reconstruction practical through foreground token selection and cost-aware distributed sampling. VideoRNG generates complex assets such as fur, feathers, and spider webs that can be interactively rendered, relit, and integrated into scenes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Generating realistic 3D assets remains constrained by classical representations: surface meshes and compact physically based materials struggle to express objects whose appearance arises from fine geometry or complex light transport, while existing Gaussian-splatting generators typically bake fixed illumination into appearance. We introduce VideoRNG, a two-stage method that generates complete, relightable 3D assets from a text prompt or a single image without prescribing a mesh or analytic material model. A video generator fine-tuned from a pretrained video diffusion backbone first produces a structured RGBA turntable sequence under a known pose, camera, and illumination. A feed-forward large reconstruction model initialized from a pretrained video diffusion backbone then maps the video-VAE latent frames of selected turntable frames directly to millions of anisotropic Gaussians with learned neural appearance features. A shared directional decoder and stochastic Gaussian ray tracer turn this representation, which we call ray-traced Relightable Neural Gaussians (rtRNG), into radiance under novel viewpoints and illumination. To preserve high-frequency appearance while learning geometry, we introduce depth-anchored appearance training, and we make million-Gaussian reconstruction practical through foreground token selection and cost-aware distributed sampling. VideoRNG generates complex assets such as fur, feathers, and spider webs that can be interactively rendered, relit, and integrated into scenes.

</details>

## 49. PGX: Physically Grounded Execution for Long-Horizon Humanoid Loco-Manipulation

**中文标题**: PGX: Physically Grounded Execution for Long-Horizon Humanoid Loco-Manipulation

**作者**: Kai Lu, Chenyang Ma, Ioannis Havoutis

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26596v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26596v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26596v1)

**项目主页**: https://kl-research.github.io/pgx

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic vision-language-action (VLA) models can execute diverse individual manipulation skills, yet they still struggle with long-horizon loco-manipulation tasks, where temporal structure, skills, and precision are tightly coupled within a monolithic model. We propose Physically Grounded Execution (PGX), a framework that offloads temporal and precision control to two classical training-free components, allowing the VLA to focus on stage-specific skill dynamics. PGX decomposes long-horizon loco-manipulation into three layers: (i) a predicate-based state machine governs task progression, using physical predicates to gate phase transitions, trigger recovery, and coordinate navigation and waiting. Shared predicate semantics connect offline demonstration segmentation, online execution, and stage-wise evaluation; (ii) VLA skills operate within active manipulation phases, with flat or stage-trained models concentrating on task-relevant actions; and (iii) world-coordinate locking (WCL) maintains spatial consistency through base station keeping, object-relative positioning, and world-anchored contact targets. We demonstrate the effectiveness of PGX with extensive experiments conducted on three simulated tasks and one real-world task using a Unitree G1 humanoid robot for long-horizon loco-manipulation, showing improvements in stage progression and task success rates. Project page is available at: https://kl-research.github.io/pgx

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robotic vision-language-action (VLA) models can execute diverse individual manipulation skills, yet they still struggle with long-horizon loco-manipulation tasks, where temporal structure, skills, and precision are tightly coupled within a monolithic model. We propose Physically Grounded Execution (PGX), a framework that offloads temporal and precision control to two classical training-free components, allowing the VLA to focus on stage-specific skill dynamics. PGX decomposes long-horizon loco-manipulation into three layers: (i) a predicate-based state machine governs task progression, using physical predicates to gate phase transitions, trigger recovery, and coordinate navigation and waiting. Shared predicate semantics connect offline demonstration segmentation, online execution, and stage-wise evaluation; (ii) VLA skills operate within active manipulation phases, with flat or stage-trained models concentrating on task-relevant actions; and (iii) world-coordinate locking (WCL) maintains spatial consistency through base station keeping, object-relative positioning, and world-anchored contact targets. We demonstrate the effectiveness of PGX with extensive experiments conducted on three simulated tasks and one real-world task using a Unitree G1 humanoid robot for long-horizon loco-manipulation, showing improvements in stage progression and task success rates. Project page is available at: https://kl-research.github.io/pgx

</details>
