## 50. Actor-Predictor Architecture for Reinforcement Learning with Formal Specification Objectives

**中文标题**: Actor-Predictor Architecture for Reinforcement Learning with Formal Specification Objectives

**作者**: Josef Brozovich, Qining Zhang, Lei Ying

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26313v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26313v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26313v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

One fundamental challenge in reinforcement learning (RL) is enforcing temporal safety constraints, such as satisfying formal specifications. Existing methods often transform logic formulas into automata for state augmentation and reward shaping. However, this approach assumes that automaton construction is both feasible and tractable, which limits its applicability to small specifications and linear temporal logic (LTL). This paper proposes Actor-Predictor, a framework that learns a surrogate representation of logic progress without explicitly constructing an automaton. The predictor continuously estimates whether a partial trajectory will satisfy the formal specification in a later step, and its learned features are provided as auxiliary inputs to the actor, which is then optimized using GRPO and RWR. We show theoretically that, under sufficient predictive accuracy, the learned representation is equivalent to the automaton state. Experiments on grid world and robotic tasks in Isaac Lab demonstrate that Actor-Predictor outperforms automaton-based baselines on LTL-constrained tasks. It also performs well on non-regular formal specifications, such as $(a^n b^n)$, for which finite-automaton-based approaches are not applicable.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

One fundamental challenge in reinforcement learning (RL) is enforcing temporal safety constraints, such as satisfying formal specifications. Existing methods often transform logic formulas into automata for state augmentation and reward shaping. However, this approach assumes that automaton construction is both feasible and tractable, which limits its applicability to small specifications and linear temporal logic (LTL). This paper proposes Actor-Predictor, a framework that learns a surrogate representation of logic progress without explicitly constructing an automaton. The predictor continuously estimates whether a partial trajectory will satisfy the formal specification in a later step, and its learned features are provided as auxiliary inputs to the actor, which is then optimized using GRPO and RWR. We show theoretically that, under sufficient predictive accuracy, the learned representation is equivalent to the automaton state. Experiments on grid world and robotic tasks in Isaac Lab demonstrate that Actor-Predictor outperforms automaton-based baselines on LTL-constrained tasks. It also performs well on non-regular formal specifications, such as $(a^n b^n)$, for which finite-automaton-based approaches are not applicable.

</details>

## 51. STAGE: Spatio-Temporal Augmented Graph Expansion for Weakly Supervised Cross-Tweet Event Causality Discovery

**中文标题**: STAGE: Spatio-Temporal Augmented Graph Expansion for Weakly Supervised Cross-Tweet Event Causality Discovery

**作者**: Hieu Minh Duong, Todd Gary, Eugene Levin, Long Nguyen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26255v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26255v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26255v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Causal relations in disaster-related social media of- ten span independent posts, making cross-tweet causal discovery difficult to supervise and model at scale. We propose STAGE, a framework that separates scalable cross-tweet weak-supervision construction from spatio-temporal relational learning over event occurrences. Starting from local causal seeds, STAGE retrieves semantically related event occurrences across tweets and uses a causal scorer to construct weakly labeled cross-tweet pairs. These pairs supervise a multi-head Graph Attention Network over semantically enriched event representations and a spatio- temporal context graph. We evaluate STAGE on California Wildfire and Hurricane Harvey using human-gold cross-tweet test sets and 15 baselines. Compared with local-only supervision, the constructed cross-tweet supervision improves STAGE AUPRC by 0.340 on Wildfire and 0.107 on Hurricane. STAGE achieves in-domain AUROC of 0.885 on Wildfire and 0.710 on Hurricane. Cross-disaster experiments reveal asymmetric transfer, highlighting sensitivity to dataset-specific spatio-temporal shifts. These results demonstrate the value of scalable cross-tweet supervision combined with relational event modeling for causal learning across fragmented disaster reports.

</details>

<details>

These pairs supervise a multi-head Graph Attention Network over semantically enriched event representations and a spatio- temporal context graph. We evaluate STAGE on California Wildfire and Hurricane Harvey using human-gold cross-tweet test sets and 15 baselines. ## 52. SenseAlign: Learning Decision-Aligned Predictive Representations for Active Urban Sensing with UAVs

**中文标题**: SenseAlign: Learning Decision-Aligned Predictive Representations for Active Urban Sensing with UAVs

**作者**: Weijiang Xiong, Nikolas Geroliminis

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26109v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26109v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26109v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Active urban sensing orchestrates a mobile sensor fleet to proactively inspect a dynamic road network and gather critical information for system management. This problem simultaneously requires understanding the complex spatio-temporal evolution and making decisions with partial observations. In this work, we focus on active sensing in a large-scale urban road network with utilities grounded in dynamic traffic states, while prior work has rarely considered these factors jointly. We propose \emph{SenseAlign}, a Reinforcement Learning-based (RL) framework that learns decision-aligned predictive representations for coordinating an urban sensing drone fleet. SenseAlign first trains a privileged teacher agent that exploits future ground-truth traffic for optimal sensing choices. The student agent has an auxiliary branch that learns to predict future traffic with partial observations, in addition to the RL-trained actor and critic branches. During student learning, our framework matches the action distributions of the teacher under ground-truth and student-predicted future traffic, while keeping the teacher's observations unchanged. Such a design shapes the latent space for decision-making instead of prediction alone. At inference time, the student agent has no overhead as all this learning is done in training. We conduct comprehensive experiments with the real road network of central Barcelona containing 1570 road segments, and demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms the baselines on multiple utility formulations. The codebase will be made publicly available upon paper acceptance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Active urban sensing orchestrates a mobile sensor fleet to proactively inspect a dynamic road network and gather critical information for system management. We propose \emph{SenseAlign}, a Reinforcement Learning-based (RL) framework that learns decision-aligned predictive representations for coordinating an urban sensing drone fleet. SenseAlign first trains a privileged teacher agent that exploits future ground-truth traffic for optimal sensing choices. The student agent has an auxiliary branch that learns to predict future traffic with partial observations, in addition to the RL-trained actor and critic branches. During student learning, our framework matches the action distributions of the teacher under ground-truth and student-predicted future traffic, while keeping the teacher's observations unchanged. Such a design shapes the latent space for decision-making instead of prediction alone. At inference time, the student agent has no overhead as all this learning is done in training. We conduct comprehensive experiments with the real road network of central Barcelona containing 1570 road segments, and demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms the baselines on multiple utility formulations. The codebase will be made publicly available upon paper acceptance.

</details>

## 53. ForeSeek: Foresight-Driven Reasoning for Long-Form Video Understanding

**中文标题**: ForeSeek: Foresight-Driven Reasoning for Long-Form Video Understanding

**作者**: Yuchen Sun, Siyuan Cheng, Shi-Zhe Chen, Yixin Qin, Jinwen Luo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26024v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26024v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26024v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/harry0021/ForeSeek

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-form video understanding, as a fundamental yet crucial vision task, is being revolutionized by multimodal large language model (MLLM)-based video agents. However, the retrieval-driven nature inherent to these agents creates a significant bottleneck, forcing them to reactively fetch clips based on local saliency without a global temporal plan. To address this, we present ForeSeek, a foresight-driven video agent that transforms long-video reasoning from reactive retrieval into predictive exploration. Rather than blindly probing the timeline, ForeSeek forms visually grounded expectations over unseen temporal regions, verifies them through targeted observations, and continuously refines its search based on emerging evidence, where failed expectations serve as informative signals for principled backtracking. Furthermore, we develop a two-stage training framework that equips MLLMs with foresight-guided reasoning and efficient decision making. Extensive experiments on five long-video benchmarks demonstrate that ForeSeek consistently improves the efficiency--accuracy trade-off of long-video reasoning, achieving competitive or superior accuracy while observing $2.5$--$4.4\times$ fewer frames than the strongest search-based agent. Code is available at https://github.com/harry0021/ForeSeek

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Rather than blindly probing the timeline, ForeSeek forms visually grounded expectations over unseen temporal regions, verifies them through targeted observations, and continuously refines its search based on emerging evidence, where failed expectations serve as informative signals for principled backtracking. Furthermore, we develop a two-stage training framework that equips MLLMs with foresight-guided reasoning and efficient decision making. Extensive experiments on five long-video benchmarks demonstrate that ForeSeek consistently improves the efficiency--accuracy trade-off of long-video reasoning, achieving competitive or superior accuracy while observing $2.5$--$4.4\times$ fewer frames than the strongest search-based agent. ## 54. Generalist Meets Specialists: An Evolving Multimodal Agent with Persistent Tool Orchestration for Traffic Scenario Understanding

**中文标题**: Generalist Meets Specialists: An Evolving Multimodal Agent with Persistent Tool Orchestration for Traffic Scenario Understanding

**作者**: Linjia Kang, Binhan Jiang, Juan Yang, Zhiqin Song, Xiangsen Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25868v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25868v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25868v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Traffic scenario understanding requires combining fine-grained visual evidence with spatial and temporal reasoning. Tool-augmented vision-language models (VLMs) can use specialized perception tools, but effective collaboration faces two challenges: terminal rewards provide limited guidance for intermediate decisions and error recovery, while repeated planning adds unnecessary cost across similar tasks. Echoing the fast-slow dichotomy of human cognition, we propose StarTV-Agent, which combines policy learning with experience evolution to support adaptive reasoning when needed and efficient reuse of prior experience when possible. During training, Role-Sensitive Process Optimization (RSPO) combines process advantages with terminal advantages, while Weakness-Driven Demonstration Learning (WDL) provides validated teacher demonstrations targeting the student's weak questions. During inference, Lightweight Adaptive Workflow Reuse (LAWR) enables a fast path by selecting suitable workflows and binding task-specific arguments and resource references, while a slow ReAct-based path handles tasks without suitable workflows. We further design label-free feedback based on execution completion, workflow adoption, and cost savings to guide workflow reuse and memory updates. Across three challenging benchmarks, StarTV-Agent achieves up to 8.0% relative performance improvement, while reducing token consumption by up to 52.1%.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Traffic scenario understanding requires combining fine-grained visual evidence with spatial and temporal reasoning. Tool-augmented vision-language models (VLMs) can use specialized perception tools, but effective collaboration faces two challenges: terminal rewards provide limited guidance for intermediate decisions and error recovery, while repeated planning adds unnecessary cost across similar tasks. Echoing the fast-slow dichotomy of human cognition, we propose StarTV-Agent, which combines policy learning with experience evolution to support adaptive reasoning when needed and efficient reuse of prior experience when possible. During training, Role-Sensitive Process Optimization (RSPO) combines process advantages with terminal advantages, while Weakness-Driven Demonstration Learning (WDL) provides validated teacher demonstrations targeting the student's weak questions. During inference, Lightweight Adaptive Workflow Reuse (LAWR) enables a fast path by selecting suitable workflows and binding task-specific arguments and resource references, while a slow ReAct-based path handles tasks without suitable workflows. We further design label-free feedback based on execution completion, workflow adoption, and cost savings to guide workflow reuse and memory updates. Across three challenging benchmarks, StarTV-Agent achieves up to 8.0% relative performance improvement, while reducing token consumption by up to 52.1%.

</details>

## 55. STARK: Moving towards Personalized Agents That Truly Learn and Remember

**中文标题**: STARK: Moving towards Personalized Agents That Truly Learn and Remember

**作者**: Aryan Jain, Abhishek Jha, Anish Nediyanchath, Promod Yenigalla

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25705v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25705v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25705v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI, cs.IR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-term memory is essential for personalized agentic systems that interact with users over long time periods, yet current architectures only capture what happened while leaving the reasoning of why it happened to be derived later by underlying LLMs.%, and silo knowledge within individual users. These events can, however, convey not only what a user experienced but also how that experience shaped their behavioral patterns. We introduce STARK, a dual-graph memory architecture which treats these events not just as nodes but also as edges between behavioral insights. STARK maintains an Episodic Graph which records interactions as temporally, causally, and semantically linked episodes; and an Insight Graph which represents LLM-extracted behavioral signals scored by how many events support them, connected by the very events that led to them. At inference, behavioral knowledge is retrieved directly from insight nodes grounded by event-as-edges, eliminating repeated episodic traversal. The event-as-edge structure also naturally enables projecting the knowledge graph into a relational embedding space where cross-user transfer becomes possible under shared contextual circumstances, allowing the system to infer missing insights for cold-start users. Evaluations show that STARK improves by 1.48 judge score on LongMemEval information extraction and 0.93 on LoCoMo open domain questions over state of the art. On the public LongLaMP abstract-generation benchmark, cross-user transfer improves open-ended judge scores by 0.7 points and ROUGE-L by 3 at 5% graph sparsity.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

On the public LongLaMP abstract-generation benchmark, cross-user transfer improves open-ended judge scores by 0.7 points and ROUGE-L by 3 at 5% graph sparsity.

</details>

## 56. LATER: Learning Action-Conditioned Temporal Evidence Retrieval for Predictive World Models

**中文标题**: LATER: Learning Action-Conditioned Temporal Evidence Retrieval for Predictive World Models

**作者**: Zihan Zhou, Wenxi Xu, Qilin Wang, Hao Tang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25671v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25671v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25671v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Early interactions can reveal physical properties that later observations no longer show. These properties can still gov- ern the effects of future actions. Inspired by the distinction between retention and selective readout in working mem- ory and cognitive science, we introduce LATER, an action- conditioned world model built on CF-JEPA. LATER stores completed real transitions in partitioned memories that remain fixed during candidate rollouts. A response bridge queries these memories using the current state and candidate action, while multi-cut auxiliary supervision trains the memory writ- ers. On distracted Two-Stage PushT, LATER raises terminal success from 15.5% to 25.0% over reconstructed CF-JEPA. In a separate comparison across six models and two scratch- training seeds, LATER achieves the lowest mean physical readout error in both clean and distracted views, reducing along-push mean absolute error (MAE) under distractors by 20.2% relative to CF-JEPA. Controlled history interventions show that later predictions depend on earlier physical evi- dence even when query observations and actions are fixed, with larger effects during pushing than waiting.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

LATER stores completed real transitions in partitioned memories that remain fixed during candidate rollouts. In a separate comparison across six models and two scratch- training seeds, LATER achieves the lowest mean physical readout error in both clean and distracted views, reducing along-push mean absolute error (MAE) under distractors by 20.2% relative to CF-JEPA. Controlled history interventions show that later predictions depend on earlier physical evi- dence even when query observations and actions are fixed, with larger effects during pushing than waiting.

</details>

## 57. Splice-Core: Low-Order-Bit-Splicing GEMM for Highly Energy-Efficient AI Inference Accelerators

**中文标题**: Splice-Core: Low-Order-Bit-Splicing GEMM for Highly Energy-Efficient AI Inference Accelerators

**作者**: Prabhu Vellaisamy, Hsien-Fu Hung, Andrew Kim, Shawn Blanton, John Paul Shen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25617v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25617v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25617v1)

**类别**: cs.AR, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Splice-Core is a temporal-unary/binary (tub) hybrid general matrix multiplication (GEMM) unit that minimizes low-order accumulation latency, while retaining value-dependent execution for high-order activation bits. We present two novel processing element (PE) variants: (i) Splice-BP utilizing bit planes, and (ii) Splice-LUT, which selects a shared weight multiple in a single cycle. Compared to a binary systolic array, Splice-BP/Splice-LUT achieve 77.4%/76.4% geometric-mean tile energy reductions, respectively, across 26 INT4 convolutional neural network (CNN) and large language model (LLM) workloads in 45nm. Splice-LUT further increases geometric-mean tile throughput by 44.4% and reduces geometric-mean energy-delay product (EDP) by 6.13x across the 26 configurations. Across the evaluated W8A8 convolution and expert-projection workloads, Splice-BP and Splice-LUT reduce tile energy by 77.3-88.1%. Post-place-and-route INT4 yields 14%/9% smaller area for Splice-BP/Splice-LUT, compared to the systolic array.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Splice-Core is a temporal-unary/binary (tub) hybrid general matrix multiplication (GEMM) unit that minimizes low-order accumulation latency, while retaining value-dependent execution for high-order activation bits. We present two novel processing element (PE) variants: (i) Splice-BP utilizing bit planes, and (ii) Splice-LUT, which selects a shared weight multiple in a single cycle. Compared to a binary systolic array, Splice-BP/Splice-LUT achieve 77.4%/76.4% geometric-mean tile energy reductions, respectively, across 26 INT4 convolutional neural network (CNN) and large language model (LLM) workloads in 45nm. Splice-LUT further increases geometric-mean tile throughput by 44.4% and reduces geometric-mean energy-delay product (EDP) by 6.13x across the 26 configurations. Across the evaluated W8A8 convolution and expert-projection workloads, Splice-BP and Splice-LUT reduce tile energy by 77.3-88.1%. Post-place-and-route INT4 yields 14%/9% smaller area for Splice-BP/Splice-LUT, compared to the systolic array.

</details>

## 58. Joint Language Modeling for In-the-Wild Speech-Scene Generation with Distinct Token Representations

**中文标题**: Joint Language Modeling for In-the-Wild Speech-Scene Generation with Distinct Token Representations

**作者**: Semin Kim, Ju Yeon Kang, Sunghwan Ahn, Seonuk Kim, Nam Soo Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25503v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25503v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25503v1)

**类别**: eess.AS, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Speech-scene generation aims to synthesize intelligible target speech together with diverse environmental sounds as a coherent acoustic scene. The task is considered challenging in two respects. Speech and environmental audio differ substantially in their semantic and acoustic characteristics, yet existing approaches typically model the complete scene using a single mixed acoustic representation. At the same time, the two sounds are closely related in real-world speech scenes, whereas conventional approaches usually rely on simulated scenes mixed from independently recorded sources. We propose a large language model (LLM)-based framework for joint modeling of distinct speech and environment token streams. A source-aware neural audio codec extracts temporally aligned speech and environment tokens from mixed audio, and a pretrained LLM generates both token streams in an interleaved fashion conditioned on the natural-language scene description and speech content. This formulation preserves source-specific representations while modeling the token streams within a shared generative context. Since both streams are extracted directly from mixed recordings, the model can also be adapted to in-the-wild speech scenes without isolated sources. Our experimental results demonstrate that the proposed framework improves speech intelligibility, scene relevance, and audio quality over existing speech-scene generation techniques, with consistent gains from real-world adaptation. We further investigate the effects of distinct token representations and interleaved generation, and analyze whether the environmental sound remains stable while speech is active and how the two streams interact.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Speech and environmental audio differ substantially in their semantic and acoustic characteristics, yet existing approaches typically model the complete scene using a single mixed acoustic representation. We further investigate the effects of distinct token representations and interleaved generation, and analyze whether the environmental sound remains stable while speech is active and how the two streams interact.

</details>

## 59. PSM-WSM: Predictively Sufficient Multimodal World Social Modeling

**中文标题**: PSM-WSM: Predictively Sufficient Multimodal World Social Modeling

**作者**: Chunlei Meng, Zhenyu Yu, Pengbin Feng, Jacqueline J. Pang, Jiabin Luo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25835v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25835v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25835v1)

**类别**: cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning latent states that support future evolution remains an open problem for multimodal interactive systems. Existing approaches typically optimize representations for current semantic understanding or direct prediction, without explicitly defining what information a latent state should preserve to remain predictive of future dynamics. In this work, we investigate this problem through the principle of predictive sufficiency, which defines a predictive state as a latent variable that retains future-relevant information from historical multimodal observations while reducing redundant dependence on the observed history. Based on this principle, we propose Predictively Sufficient Multimodal Social World Modeling (PSM-WM), a framework that jointly learns predictive latent states and their stochastic temporal evolution through probabilistic state inference, event-conditioned transitions, and multi-step consistency optimization. Beyond forecasting performance, we introduce state-centric evaluations to measure whether learned representations satisfy predictive state properties, including predictive closure, residual history gain, and future semantic alignment. Experiments on Hi-EF, IEMOCAP, MELD, and Ego4D show that PSM-WM improves future multimodal interaction modeling and learns latent states with stronger predictive properties and robustness. These results indicate that predictive sufficiency provides an effective principle for constructing future-oriented multimodal states and highlight the importance of state construction in multimodal world modeling.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Learning latent states that support future evolution remains an open problem for multimodal interactive systems. Existing approaches typically optimize representations for current semantic understanding or direct prediction, without explicitly defining what information a latent state should preserve to remain predictive of future dynamics. In this work, we investigate this problem through the principle of predictive sufficiency, which defines a predictive state as a latent variable that retains future-relevant information from historical multimodal observations while reducing redundant dependence on the observed history. Based on this principle, we propose Predictively Sufficient Multimodal Social World Modeling (PSM-WM), a framework that jointly learns predictive latent states and their stochastic temporal evolution through probabilistic state inference, event-conditioned transitions, and multi-step consistency optimization. Beyond forecasting performance, we introduce state-centric evaluations to measure whether learned representations satisfy predictive state properties, including predictive closure, residual history gain, and future semantic alignment. Experiments on Hi-EF, IEMOCAP, MELD, and Ego4D show that PSM-WM improves future multimodal interaction modeling and learns latent states with stronger predictive properties and robustness. These results indicate that predictive sufficiency provides an effective principle for constructing future-oriented multimodal states and highlight the importance of state construction in multimodal world modeling.

</details>

## 60. Sonic Palette: Predicting Dynamic Color Themes from Music

**中文标题**: Sonic Palette: Predicting Dynamic Color Themes from Music

**作者**: Wen-Li Wei, Kenneth Yang, Jen-Chun Lin

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25781v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25781v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25781v1)

**类别**: cs.MM, cs.SD

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present Sonic Palette, a music-driven framework for predicting time-varying color themes from audio. Rather than treating music-to-palette generation as a static problem, our method formulates music--color correspondence as a cross-modal sequence prediction task that models how palette-level visual attributes evolve over time in response to music. Specifically, Sonic Palette uses a music-conditioned autoregressive Transformer to predict frame-wise color themes, each represented by five dominant colors, from input audio features. To support this task, we curate a dataset of paired audio--color sequences derived from 265 full-length official music videos. We further visualize the predicted color themes through a palette-guided recoloring process that preserves image structure while revealing temporally varying color appearance associated with the music. Quantitative results show that Sonic Palette predicts color themes that more closely match the ground truth, while subjective results indicate more natural temporal color transitions and stronger perceived music--color atmosphere alignment, highlighting its potential for music-driven visual design, computational art, and creative visual authoring.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present Sonic Palette, a music-driven framework for predicting time-varying color themes from audio. Rather than treating music-to-palette generation as a static problem, our method formulates music--color correspondence as a cross-modal sequence prediction task that models how palette-level visual attributes evolve over time in response to music. Specifically, Sonic Palette uses a music-conditioned autoregressive Transformer to predict frame-wise color themes, each represented by five dominant colors, from input audio features. To support this task, we curate a dataset of paired audio--color sequences derived from 265 full-length official music videos. We further visualize the predicted color themes through a palette-guided recoloring process that preserves image structure while revealing temporally varying color appearance associated with the music. Quantitative results show that Sonic Palette predicts color themes that more closely match the ground truth, while subjective results indicate more natural temporal color transitions and stronger perceived music--color atmosphere alignment, highlighting its potential for music-driven visual design, computational art, and creative visual authoring.

</details>

## 61. ACE-Brain-0.7: Learning Execution-Grounded Reasoning for Embodied Agents

**中文标题**: ACE-Brain-0.7: Learning Execution-Grounded Reasoning for Embodied Agents

**作者**: Yinhe Wang, Shijun Shi, Feng Pan, Tao Tao, Wei Mao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26519v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26519v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26519v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reliable reasoning for long-horizon embodied tasks requires predictions grounded in the observed execution state. We present ACE-Brain-0.7, a 9B vision--language model that combines spatial grounding, task understanding, and progress estimation. To train the model, we curate 3.7 million question--answer pairs, drawing execution supervision from egocentric human videos, robot episodes, and simulation. These questions span different stages of an execution and connect task-level judgments with observed progress and outcomes. We then fine-tune Qwen3.5-9B on this collection together with additional task, spatial-grounding, and progress-estimation data. Across six public task-reasoning benchmarks, ACE-Brain-0.7 achieves an average score of 76.5, ahead of GPT-6 Astra at 72.7 and Claude Opus 5.5 at 73.5. Our model also leads the evaluated models in spatial grounding, averaging 75.90 across seven benchmarks and reaching 91.25 Acc@0.5 on RefCOCOg. Controlled evaluations further indicate that our task-only model makes more effective use of visual history and more accurately distinguishes execution states. Together, these results show that ACE-Brain-0.7 effectively combines spatial perception with temporal understanding, delivering strong execution-grounded reasoning and comparing favorably with closed-source models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reliable reasoning for long-horizon embodied tasks requires predictions grounded in the observed execution state. We present ACE-Brain-0.7, a 9B vision--language model that combines spatial grounding, task understanding, and progress estimation. To train the model, we curate 3.7 million question--answer pairs, drawing execution supervision from egocentric human videos, robot episodes, and simulation. These questions span different stages of an execution and connect task-level judgments with observed progress and outcomes. We then fine-tune Qwen3.5-9B on this collection together with additional task, spatial-grounding, and progress-estimation data. Across six public task-reasoning benchmarks, ACE-Brain-0.7 achieves an average score of 76.5, ahead of GPT-6 Astra at 72.7 and Claude Opus 5.5 at 73.5. Our model also leads the evaluated models in spatial grounding, averaging 75.90 across seven benchmarks and reaching 91.25 Acc@0.5 on RefCOCOg. Controlled evaluations further indicate that our task-only model makes more effective use of visual history and more accurately distinguishes execution states. Together, these results show that ACE-Brain-0.7 effectively combines spatial perception with temporal understanding, delivering strong execution-grounded reasoning and comparing favorably with closed-source models.

</details>

---

## 62. Reasoning on Call: Efficient Agentic Robot Control for Long-Horizon Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: Reasoning on Call: Efficient Agentic Robot Control for Long-Horizon Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Zilang Cen, Chao Tang, Fangcheng Liu, Weishi Mi, Wei Hao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26341v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26341v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26341v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Large multimodal models can support long-horizon robot manipulation, but frequent model calls incur substantial latency and token costs. The central challenge is determining when agent assistance or intervention is needed and whether errors require correcting robot execution, subtask planning, or both. To address this problem, we propose RoC (Reasoning on Call), an efficient framework that coordinates a low-level policy and a high-level reasoning agent through an online task-state monitor. The monitor combines temporal visual observations with ordered interaction memory to jointly estimate progress within the active subtask and predict its successor. These predictions guide event-triggered agent review for coordinated execution repair and plan revision. Verified outcomes govern memory updates and subtask transitions, allowing routine execution to remain with the policy while reasoning focuses on decisions requiring additional assessment. On RMBench, RoC improves task success rate by 24.0 percentage points and reduces token consumption by 65.3% compared with the strongest agent-based baseline. It also outperforms the strongest policy-based baseline by 15.1 percentage points in task success rate. Real-world experiments further validate its effectiveness across three long-horizon manipulation tasks. Overall, RoC supports efficient agent intervention and coordinated recovery across execution and planning for long-horizon robot manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

These predictions guide event-triggered agent review for coordinated execution repair and plan revision. Verified outcomes govern memory updates and subtask transitions, allowing routine execution to remain with the policy while reasoning focuses on decisions requiring additional assessment. On RMBench, RoC improves task success rate by 24.0 percentage points and reduces token consumption by 65.3% compared with the strongest agent-based baseline. It also outperforms the strongest policy-based baseline by 15.1 percentage points in task success rate. Real-world experiments further validate its effectiveness across three long-horizon manipulation tasks. Overall, RoC supports efficient agent intervention and coordinated recovery across execution and planning for long-horizon robot manipulation.

</details>

## 63. Spatially Conditioned Trajectory Diffusion

**中文标题**: Spatially Conditioned Trajectory Diffusion

**作者**: Yefim Nudelman, Dor Atzmon, Tom Tirer

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26127v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26127v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26127v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multi-Robot Motion Planning (MRMP) requires trajectories that avoid environmental obstacles and collisions between robots in a shared workspace. Diffusion-based planners often address obstacle avoidance through sampling-time guidance, while some conditioned approaches aggregate map geometry into a global representation. We present Spatially Conditioned Trajectory Diffusion (SCTD), a map-conditioned trajectory generator for multi-robot planning. SCTD retains multiple position-aware occupancy tokens accessed through cross-attention throughout a temporal U-Net, with hard endpoint inpainting enforcing start and goal states. One model trained on single-robot demonstrations across procedural environments generates obstacle-aware trajectories without sampling-time obstacle-cost guidance. High-level Conflict-Based Search (CBS) coordinates these trajectories through constrained replanning, retaining inter-agent guidance during sampling and reusing the model across workspace layouts and team sizes. Experiments assess single-robot success and trajectory quality, generalization to unfamiliar layouts with added obstacles, and coordinated multi-robot planning. On 15,000 held-out tasks, SCTD achieves higher success than the evaluated guided diffusion baseline, with the largest gains on hard-detour tasks, while trajectory-quality comparisons show smoother trajectories. Conditioning ablations show higher success with spatial tokens than with a global map embedding, and map-replacement tests demonstrate dependence on the supplied geometry. The resulting multi-robot system achieves higher team success than the compared diffusion baselines, with experiments covering teams of up to 15 robots.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Experiments assess single-robot success and trajectory quality, generalization to unfamiliar layouts with added obstacles, and coordinated multi-robot planning. On 15,000 held-out tasks, SCTD achieves higher success than the evaluated guided diffusion baseline, with the largest gains on hard-detour tasks, while trajectory-quality comparisons show smoother trajectories. Conditioning ablations show higher success with spatial tokens than with a global map embedding, and map-replacement tests demonstrate dependence on the supplied geometry. The resulting multi-robot system achieves higher team success than the compared diffusion baselines, with experiments covering teams of up to 15 robots.

</details>

## 64. Reference-Twist-Based Velocity-Domain Spatiotemporal Calibration for mmWave Radar, Event Camera, and IMU

**中文标题**: Reference-Twist-Based Velocity-Domain Spatiotemporal Calibration for mmWave Radar, Event Camera, and IMU

**作者**: Zhenghao Zou, Yang Lyu, Xiaohu Guo, Chunhui Zhao, Quan Pan

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26083v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26083v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26083v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multi-sensor navigation requires a common spatial and temporal basis, but geometric calibration relies on targets, features, point clouds, or pose associations that are difficult to establish across mmWave radars, event cameras, and inertial measurement units (IMUs). These sensors nevertheless share rigid-body motion. This paper formulates reference-conditioned velocity-domain spatiotemporal calibration for a rigid suite of two mmWave radars, one event camera, and two IMUs. Given a trusted reference body twist, radar Doppler, event normal flow, and inertial measurements are regressed against the same motion to estimate relative rotations, lever arms, time offsets, and sensor-specific bias and scale without artificial targets, structure from motion, or cross-modal point-cloud registration. The reference is treated as a conditioning input rather than a sensor to be calibrated. We compare joint and reference- conditioned estimation, derive conditional identifiability and CRLB-level covariance and bias bounds, and state the operating conditions under which conditioning is informative. Indoor and forest recordings with axis-separated motion evaluate initial- ization, temporal alignment, motion degeneracy, reference-input sensitivity, and residual consistency. Because the reference does not add physical observability, the estimates remain conditional on reference accuracy, synchronization, motion excitation, and projection diversity.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multi-sensor navigation requires a common spatial and temporal basis, but geometric calibration relies on targets, features, point clouds, or pose associations that are difficult to establish across mmWave radars, event cameras, and inertial measurement units (IMUs). These sensors nevertheless share rigid-body motion. This paper formulates reference-conditioned velocity-domain spatiotemporal calibration for a rigid suite of two mmWave radars, one event camera, and two IMUs. Given a trusted reference body twist, radar Doppler, event normal flow, and inertial measurements are regressed against the same motion to estimate relative rotations, lever arms, time offsets, and sensor-specific bias and scale without artificial targets, structure from motion, or cross-modal point-cloud registration. The reference is treated as a conditioning input rather than a sensor to be calibrated. We compare joint and reference- conditioned estimation, derive conditional identifiability and CRLB-level covariance and bias bounds, and state the operating conditions under which conditioning is informative. Indoor and forest recordings with axis-separated motion evaluate initial- ization, temporal alignment, motion degeneracy, reference-input sensitivity, and residual consistency. Because the reference does not add physical observability, the estimates remain conditional on reference accuracy, synchronization, motion excitation, and projection diversity.

</details>

## 65. GNetSS: Towards Learning GNSS Corrections without Ground Truth

**中文标题**: GNetSS: Towards Learning GNSS Corrections without Ground Truth

**作者**: JiaXu Chee, Simon Denman, Fahira Afzal Maken, Qingqing Cheng, Amir Khodabandeh et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25665v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25665v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25665v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We present GNetSS, a self-supervised GNSS position correction framework that improves the robustness of single point positioning (SPP) without requiring ground-truth locations for training. GNetSS combines a transformer-based network with a deterministic differentiable positioning solver. The network predicts satellite-wise pseudorange corrections by modelling inter-satellite geometry and temporal context, while the solver converts these corrected measurements into receiver position corrections under GNSS geometry and optional motion constraints. Unlike supervised GNSS correction methods that rely on ground-truth, GNetSS derives its training signal directly from GNSS measurement consistency. Experimental results show that GNetSS improves robustness across open-sky, urban, and forested environments. The method is particularly effective at suppressing large-error outliers, reducing the maximum 3D positioning error by over 50% compared with SPP. These results demonstrate the potential of self-supervised pseudorange correction for scalable and robust GNSS positioning in real-world autonomy applications. Code and the in-house dataset will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Code and the in-house dataset will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

## 66. GraF-VTA: Neuro-Symbolic Trajectory Generation from Vision and Temporal Logic via Graph-Based Fusion

**中文标题**: GraF-VTA: Neuro-Symbolic Trajectory Generation from Vision and Temporal Logic via Graph-Based Fusion

**作者**: Zezhi Liu, Zhiwei Zheng, Hanqian Luo, Deyun Qin, Shizhen Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25589v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25589v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25589v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic planning from visual observations and signal temporal logic (STL) requires grounding task requirements in scene evidence while adapting plans to execution feedback. We present GraF-VTA, a graph-based framework that extends Vision-TL-Action with structured task representations, causal observation history, and closed-loop trajectory generation. Bidirectional cross-attention fuses STL syntax tokens with visual features, while an optional frozen automaton embedding supplies complementary untimed task structure. A temporal pyramid preserves detailed current observations and compresses older frames to provide recent execution context. Conditioned on these features and robot state, a flow-matching model generates plans whose action prefixes are executed before replanning from refreshed observations. Across five simulation environments, the single-graph configuration achieves higher open-loop Success@$K$ than TeLoGraF and Vision-TL-Action on four environments at the reported budgets $K\geq16$. On Panda, matched-update ablations increase Success@256 from 53.91\% without observation history to 62.50\% with pyramid memory. In closed-loop Dubins evaluation, it reaches 64.06\% Success@1024, compared with 12.50\% for adapted S-MSP and 27.34\% for adapted Logic-VLA. These results support combining structured visual conditioning with observation history and replanning to improve coverage of STL-satisfying trajectories.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We present GraF-VTA, a graph-based framework that extends Vision-TL-Action with structured task representations, causal observation history, and closed-loop trajectory generation. Bidirectional cross-attention fuses STL syntax tokens with visual features, while an optional frozen automaton embedding supplies complementary untimed task structure. Across five simulation environments, the single-graph configuration achieves higher open-loop Success@$K$ than TeLoGraF and Vision-TL-Action on four environments at the reported budgets $K\geq16$. In closed-loop Dubins evaluation, it reaches 64.06\% Success@1024, compared with 12.50\% for adapted S-MSP and 27.34\% for adapted Logic-VLA. These results support combining structured visual conditioning with observation history and replanning to improve coverage of STL-satisfying trajectories.

</details>

## 67. Communication-Aware UAV Spatio-Temporal Relay Cooperation and Planning in Complex Urban Environments

**中文标题**: Communication-Aware UAV Spatio-Temporal Relay Cooperation and Planning in Complex Urban Environments

**作者**: Haoyu Zhang, Yingjie He, Zehao Zhuo, Xiqun Chen, Zhenyu Gao

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25558v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25558v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25558v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) are increasingly deployed in low-altitude urban airspace for delivery, emergency response, and other applications. However, UAV mobility across spatially heterogeneous urban communication environments can lead to time-varying communication outages, disrupting mission execution and compromising flight safety. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a Spatio-Temporal Relay Cooperation and Planning (ST-RCP) framework that leverages delivery UAVs as opportunistic communication relays for disconnected UAVs without requiring additional communication infrastructure. The framework predicts communication blind-spot events over a finite planning horizon, constructs a helper-event compatibility graph, and jointly optimizes relay selection, obstacle-aware trajectory replanning, and communication-aware scheduling. Multiple planners are evaluated to investigate the trade-offs among communication coverage, computational cost, and energy consumption. Experiments based on real-world traffic scenarios compare the performance of ST-RCP against baseline methods. In the peak scenario, ST-RCP improves descent-phase communication coverage by 29.93% compared with the base-station baseline and by 27.38%, 22.02%, and 15.20% compared with configurations using one, two, and three dedicated relay UAVs, respectively. Process flexibility analysis demonstrates that predictive task-aware relaying expands the pool of feasible relay resources and improves communication continuity while maintaining the feasibility of delivery missions. These findings demonstrate the potential of trajectory-enabled UAV coordination to enhance the safety and resilience of urban aerial operations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) are increasingly deployed in low-altitude urban airspace for delivery, emergency response, and other applications. However, UAV mobility across spatially heterogeneous urban communication environments can lead to time-varying communication outages, disrupting mission execution and compromising flight safety. To address this challenge, this paper proposes a Spatio-Temporal Relay Cooperation and Planning (ST-RCP) framework that leverages delivery UAVs as opportunistic communication relays for disconnected UAVs without requiring additional communication infrastructure. The framework predicts communication blind-spot events over a finite planning horizon, constructs a helper-event compatibility graph, and jointly optimizes relay selection, obstacle-aware trajectory replanning, and communication-aware scheduling. In the peak scenario, ST-RCP improves descent-phase communication coverage by 29.93% compared with the base-station baseline and by 27.38%, 22.02%, and 15.20% compared with configurations using one, two, and three dedicated relay UAVs, respectively. Process flexibility analysis demonstrates that predictive task-aware relaying expands the pool of feasible relay resources and improves communication continuity while maintaining the feasibility of delivery missions. These findings demonstrate the potential of trajectory-enabled UAV coordination to enhance the safety and resilience of urban aerial operations.

</details>

## 68. RefreshGrad: An Online Adaptive Data-sampling Method for Diffusion-based Visuomotor Learning

**中文标题**: RefreshGrad: An Online Adaptive Data-sampling Method for Diffusion-based Visuomotor Learning

**作者**: Dijia Zhan, Jinyi Li, Xuemiao Xu, Yan Zhou, Yu Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25398v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25398v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25398v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Training vision-language-action (VLA) policies from demonstrations typically samples trajectory segments uniformly or relies on static offline data curation before training. However, we find that demonstration data vary sharply in supervision value across temporal windows, and that each window's value to the policy evolves substantially during training rather than being fixed or captured by raw loss alone. Motivated by this finding, we propose RefreshGrad, an online adaptive data-sampling framework that periodically scores temporal windows from the evolving policy using five policy-native signals and dynamically adjusts their sampling weights during training. RefreshGrad focuses optimization on informative, learnable windows without altering the policy architecture, training objective, or inference cost. Across ten LIBERO-Plus manipulation tasks, RefreshGrad boosts average success from 60.7% (Uniform) and 68.2% (FAKTUAL) to 76.1% under visual perturbations, and from 57.4% and 73.3% to 81.3% in clean scenes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Training vision-language-action (VLA) policies from demonstrations typically samples trajectory segments uniformly or relies on static offline data curation before training. However, we find that demonstration data vary sharply in supervision value across temporal windows, and that each window's value to the policy evolves substantially during training rather than being fixed or captured by raw loss alone. Motivated by this finding, we propose RefreshGrad, an online adaptive data-sampling framework that periodically scores temporal windows from the evolving policy using five policy-native signals and dynamically adjusts their sampling weights during training. RefreshGrad focuses optimization on informative, learnable windows without altering the policy architecture, training objective, or inference cost. Across ten LIBERO-Plus manipulation tasks, RefreshGrad boosts average success from 60.7% (Uniform) and 68.2% (FAKTUAL) to 76.1% under visual perturbations, and from 57.4% and 73.3% to 81.3% in clean scenes.

</details>

## 69. Learning to Defer under Expert Drift

**中文标题**: Learning to Defer under Expert Drift

**作者**: Ziyao Mou, Andrea Wynn, Eric Nalisnick

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26440v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26440v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26440v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/ZiyaoMou/L2D-Expert-Drift.

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

The learning to defer (L2D) framework enables safety in machine learning and AI systems by allocating difficult or critical decisions to a human expert. However, most prior work on L2D assumes fixed expert reliability, overlooking temporal fluctuations in human performance due to factors such as fatigue and cognitive biases. Humans commonly exhibit such substantial, systematic performance shifts across a wide range of domains, from high-stakes settings such as radiology, aviation, and judicial decision-making, to everyday tasks such as crowdsourced image annotation. We propose Expert-Drift-Adapted Learning-to-Defer (EDA-L2D), a novel expert-aware L2D framework that explicitly models these temporal shifts by conditioning the deferral head on recent histories of expert outcomes via a sequence model. This history-aware approach enables adaptive deferral policies that anticipate reliability fluctuations and better allocate decisions between human and machine over time. We provide comprehensive experiments across three diverse tasks -- image classification, medical diagnosis, and hate speech detection -- including generalization analyses under realistic deferral conditions and irregular shifts in expert reliability. Our results show that EDA-L2D adapts more effectively than prior L2D methods to diverse temporal patterns of expert reliability, resulting in improved system performance and more effective deferral decisions. Code is available at https://github.com/ZiyaoMou/L2D-Expert-Drift.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, most prior work on L2D assumes fixed expert reliability, overlooking temporal fluctuations in human performance due to factors such as fatigue and cognitive biases. Humans commonly exhibit such substantial, systematic performance shifts across a wide range of domains, from high-stakes settings such as radiology, aviation, and judicial decision-making, to everyday tasks such as crowdsourced image annotation. We propose Expert-Drift-Adapted Learning-to-Defer (EDA-L2D), a novel expert-aware L2D framework that explicitly models these temporal shifts by conditioning the deferral head on recent histories of expert outcomes via a sequence model. This history-aware approach enables adaptive deferral policies that anticipate reliability fluctuations and better allocate decisions between human and machine over time. We provide comprehensive experiments across three diverse tasks -- image classification, medical diagnosis, and hate speech detection -- including generalization analyses under realistic deferral conditions and irregular shifts in expert reliability. Our results show that EDA-L2D adapts more effectively than prior L2D methods to diverse temporal patterns of expert reliability, resulting in improved system performance and more effective deferral decisions. Code is available at https://github.com/ZiyaoMou/L2D-Expert-Drift.

</details>

## 70. TRCF: Temporal-Reference Complementary Forecasting for Irregular Multivariate Time Series

**中文标题**: TRCF: Temporal-Reference Complementary Forecasting for Irregular Multivariate Time Series

**作者**: Xudong Zuo, Shusen Ma, Qirui Chen, Bing Fan, Yun-Bo Zhao

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26351v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26351v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26351v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Irregular multivariate time series forecasting supports healthcare monitoring, environmental analysis, and activity recognition. Channel-independent (CI) approaches capture each variable's temporal patterns but do not explicitly use observations from other variables. Channel-dependent (CD) modeling exploits relationships among variables, yet asynchronous measurements make these relationships difficult to learn. Incorporating cross-variable information while preserving each variable's irregular history therefore remains a key challenge. We propose Temporal-Reference Complementary Forecasting (TRCF), a framework that augments a compact CI forecaster with a complementary CD branch. ANCHOR aggregates observed values and timestamps at shared temporal locations, retaining observation support to form compact CI features. BRIDGE constructs complementary features through history completion. It first estimates each variable's historical values from its own observations, then uses differences between other variables' observed and estimated values at nearby times to correct missing entries. The completed history preserves observed values and is encoded to supply context alongside the direct CI representation. Finally, the two representations are fused, combined with the query-time embedding, and passed to a shallow multilayer perceptron for forecasting. Experiments on four real-world datasets show that TRCF achieves the lowest mean squared error and mean absolute error in the main benchmark comparison, while reducing measured peak inference memory by 17.28% to 87.98% relative to APN.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Irregular multivariate time series forecasting supports healthcare monitoring, environmental analysis, and activity recognition. Channel-independent (CI) approaches capture each variable's temporal patterns but do not explicitly use observations from other variables. Channel-dependent (CD) modeling exploits relationships among variables, yet asynchronous measurements make these relationships difficult to learn. Incorporating cross-variable information while preserving each variable's irregular history therefore remains a key challenge. We propose Temporal-Reference Complementary Forecasting (TRCF), a framework that augments a compact CI forecaster with a complementary CD branch. Experiments on four real-world datasets show that TRCF achieves the lowest mean squared error and mean absolute error in the main benchmark comparison, while reducing measured peak inference memory by 17.28% to 87.98% relative to APN.

</details>

## 71. Time Series Structure Learning with Local Causal Parent Discovery

**中文标题**: Time Series Structure Learning with Local Causal Parent Discovery

**作者**: Jacqueline Maasch, Shu Wan, Hank Zhu, Huan Liu, Zhiguang Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26232v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26232v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26232v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, stat.ML

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning and inference on time series can pose significant statistical challenges, including autocorrelated observations, distribution shift over time, and the curse of dimensionality. These challenges complicate the practical application of causal discovery algorithms, which already suffer from high computational and sample complexity in the general case. To mitigate these issues, this work introduces Iterative Temporal Parent Discovery (ITPD): a sound and complete constraint-based procedure for causal structure learning on multivariate time series with causal nonstationarity. ITPD takes a local-to-global learning approach that discovers the global graph by sequentially discovering the parent set for each variable. ITPD exploits temporal structural priors, graph partitioning, and the local Markov condition to swap higher-order conditional independence tests for lower-order alternatives. With an exact oracle, ITPD performed the least number of tests and used the smallest average conditioning set sizes relative to baselines. In benchmarking simulations, ITPD sat on the Pareto frontier with respect to F1 and wall-clock time and was faster than baselines on large graphs ($\geq$ 1000 nodes). For graphs with 2000 nodes, ITPD ran $2.3\times$, $9.1\times$, and $40.9\times$ faster than iterative IAMB, DF-PC, and PCMCI, respectively.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

These challenges complicate the practical application of causal discovery algorithms, which already suffer from high computational and sample complexity in the general case. ITPD takes a local-to-global learning approach that discovers the global graph by sequentially discovering the parent set for each variable. ITPD exploits temporal structural priors, graph partitioning, and the local Markov condition to swap higher-order conditional independence tests for lower-order alternatives. With an exact oracle, ITPD performed the least number of tests and used the smallest average conditioning set sizes relative to baselines. In benchmarking simulations, ITPD sat on the Pareto frontier with respect to F1 and wall-clock time and was faster than baselines on large graphs ($\geq$ 1000 nodes). For graphs with 2000 nodes, ITPD ran $2.3\times$, $9.1\times$, and $40.9\times$ faster than iterative IAMB, DF-PC, and PCMCI, respectively.

</details>

---

## 72. Phantom of the Masquerade: Graph Neural Network-Based Detection of Masquerade Attacks in Controller Area Networks

**中文标题**: Phantom of the Masquerade: Graph Neural Network-Based Detection of Masquerade Attacks in Controller Area Networks

**作者**: Nehal Ameen, Pablo Moriano, Irfan Ahmed

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.26094v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26094v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.26094v1)

**类别**: cs.CR, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

The lack of built-in security in controller area networks (CANs) exposes modern vehicles and industrial systems to masquerade attacks that can compromise safety and reliability. These attacks inject malicious messages that mimic legitimate communication patterns, making them difficult to detect using existing intrusion detection systems (IDS). Prior graph-based IDS approaches often rely on shallow embeddings that decouple representation learning from classification, which may limit their ability to fully exploit structural and signal-level information. In this paper, we propose PhantoMasq, a graph neural network (GNN)-based framework for detecting masquerade attacks in CAN systems. PhantoMasq models CAN traffic as message sequence graphs enriched with lightweight statistical features derived from signal behavior, enabling joint learning of structural dependencies and temporal characteristics. The detection task is formulated as a graph-level classification problem and solved using graph convolutional networks (GCNs). We evaluate PhantoMasq on the ROAD dataset under a protocol that prevents overlapping windows from spanning training and test data. Across five masquerade attack types, PhantoMasq achieves mean AUC-ROC between 0.831 and 0.999, with performance depending on the transmission rate of the targeted identifier and the magnitude of the induced signal deviation. Against a state-of-the-art graph-embedding baseline evaluated on the same sliding-window configurations, PhantoMasq achieves higher AUC-ROC on all five attack types, by 0.13 to 0.36, and is less sensitive to window configuration. Window-processing latency ranges from 2.33 to 5.94 ms per window on CPU, corresponding to a throughput of 168 to 429 windows per second. We make our implementation and experimental artifacts available to support reproducibility.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The lack of built-in security in controller area networks (CANs) exposes modern vehicles and industrial systems to masquerade attacks that can compromise safety and reliability. These attacks inject malicious messages that mimic legitimate communication patterns, making them difficult to detect using existing intrusion detection systems (IDS). Prior graph-based IDS approaches often rely on shallow embeddings that decouple representation learning from classification, which may limit their ability to fully exploit structural and signal-level information. In this paper, we propose PhantoMasq, a graph neural network (GNN)-based framework for detecting masquerade attacks in CAN systems. PhantoMasq models CAN traffic as message sequence graphs enriched with lightweight statistical features derived from signal behavior, enabling joint learning of structural dependencies and temporal characteristics. The detection task is formulated as a graph-level classification problem and solved using graph convolutional networks (GCNs). We evaluate PhantoMasq on the ROAD dataset under a protocol that prevents overlapping windows from spanning training and test data. Across five masquerade attack types, PhantoMasq achieves mean AUC-ROC between 0.831 and 0.999, with performance depending on the transmission rate of the targeted identifier and the magnitude of the induced signal deviation. Against a state-of-the-art graph-embedding baseline evaluated on the same sliding-window configurations, PhantoMasq achieves higher AUC-ROC on all five attack types, by 0.13 to 0.36, and is less sensitive to window configuration. Window-processing latency ranges from 2.33 to 5.94 ms per window on CPU, corresponding to a throughput of 168 to 429 windows per second. We make our implementation and experimental artifacts available to support reproducibility.

</details>

## 73. Measurable Does Not Imply Transportable: A Prospective Audit of Model Explanations in Multi-Year Field Trials

**中文标题**: Measurable Does Not Imply Transportable: A Prospective Audit of Model Explanations in Multi-Year Field Trials

**作者**: Rayhaneh Shabani Nia, Ali Karkehabadi

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25692v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25692v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25692v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Model explanations are increasingly used as scientific evidence, yet it remains unclear what they license scientists to conclude beyond the data on which they were constructed. When a fine-grained explanation fails to replicate, the failure may be attributed to noisy measurement of its target, raising a fundamental question: does reliable measurement imply a reproducible explanation? We test this directly. We formalize prospective explanation transportability: the extent to which an explanation learned from past data is recovered in a genuinely future context. This property is distinct from feature importance and measurement reliability. Using multi-year Genomes-to-Fields maize trials, where each new year is a real future context rather than a random resample, we evaluate 16 agronomically interpretable environmental concepts across five forward splits and one prospectively locked test year. Coarse environment-level explanations transport reliably (median cosine 0.83); their locked 2024 value, 0.811, fell within a prespecified 80% bootstrap percentile interval. For grain yield, genotype-conditioned explanations have a historical median cosine of 0.30 and range from 0.006 to 0.822 across years. Reliability does not order these outcomes: the year with the highest split-half reliability had the lowest explanation transport. Within-context resampling showed that GxE explanations were reproducible within a fixed context (0.77-0.95) while exhibiting lower prospective alignment across the 2020-2023 transitions (0.01-0.36). A second phenotype transports strongly at both resolutions, and non-temporal validation inflates apparent interaction-level transport by +0.22. These results show that target reliability and within-context explanation reproducibility do not by themselves establish when a model explanation can support a reproducible scientific claim.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Model explanations are increasingly used as scientific evidence, yet it remains unclear what they license scientists to conclude beyond the data on which they were constructed. When a fine-grained explanation fails to replicate, the failure may be attributed to noisy measurement of its target, raising a fundamental question: does reliable measurement imply a reproducible explanation? We test this directly. We formalize prospective explanation transportability: the extent to which an explanation learned from past data is recovered in a genuinely future context. Coarse environment-level explanations transport reliably (median cosine 0.83); their locked 2024 value, 0.811, fell within a prespecified 80% bootstrap percentile interval. For grain yield, genotype-conditioned explanations have a historical median cosine of 0.30 and range from 0.006 to 0.822 across years. Reliability does not order these outcomes: the year with the highest split-half reliability had the lowest explanation transport. Within-context resampling showed that GxE explanations were reproducible within a fixed context (0.77-0.95) while exhibiting lower prospective alignment across the 2020-2023 transitions (0.01-0.36). A second phenotype transports strongly at both resolutions, and non-temporal validation inflates apparent interaction-level transport by +0.22. These results show that target reliability and within-context explanation reproducibility do not by themselves establish when a model explanation can support a reproducible scientific claim.

</details>

## 74. Lightweight Low-Frequency Non-Intrusive Load Monitoring via Karhunen-Loève Feature Distillation and Temporal Convolutional Networks

**中文标题**: Lightweight Low-Frequency Non-Intrusive Load Monitoring via Karhunen-Loève Feature Distillation and Temporal Convolutional Networks

**作者**: Sanka Peeris, Pandula Thennakoon, Praveen Sumanasekara, Roshan Godaliyadda, Parakrama Ekanayake et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25678v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25678v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25678v1)

**类别**: eess.SP, cs.LG, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Non-Intrusive Load Monitoring (NILM) is a critical enabler for demand-side management which allows for smart grid optimization. A deployment-ready NILM solution necessitates three key features: accurate disaggregation, low-frequency sampling, and low computational cost. This paper proposes a lightweight, edge-oriented NILM framework that combines activity-boundary-aware synthetic data augmentation with Karhunen-Loève Transform (KLT) feature extraction and a Temporal Convolutional Network (TCN). The augmentation stage extracts and recombines complete or near-complete appliance activation segments so that the model can be trained without new fully labeled household aggregates. The KLT filter bank extracts decorrelated, appliance-informed temporal features which are then fed into a compact TCN along with the original aggregate for energy disaggregation. Evaluations on two standard NILM datasets, UK-DALE and REDD, at 10-s sampling and with leave-one-house-out testing, demonstrate that the proposed architecture achieves comparable or superior disaggregation accuracy relative to NILMFormer, BERT4NILM, BiLSTM, and BiGRU. The computational footprint of the framework is 31,009 parameters, the fewest of the five models compared, and on a single CPU thread it needs 2.78 ms per 1000 samples, 84-95% less than the baselines. These results demonstrate a favorable accuracy-efficiency tradeoff for low-frequency smart-meter NILM.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

A deployment-ready NILM solution necessitates three key features: accurate disaggregation, low-frequency sampling, and low computational cost. This paper proposes a lightweight, edge-oriented NILM framework that combines activity-boundary-aware synthetic data augmentation with Karhunen-Loève Transform (KLT) feature extraction and a Temporal Convolutional Network (TCN). The augmentation stage extracts and recombines complete or near-complete appliance activation segments so that the model can be trained without new fully labeled household aggregates. The KLT filter bank extracts decorrelated, appliance-informed temporal features which are then fed into a compact TCN along with the original aggregate for energy disaggregation. Evaluations on two standard NILM datasets, UK-DALE and REDD, at 10-s sampling and with leave-one-house-out testing, demonstrate that the proposed architecture achieves comparable or superior disaggregation accuracy relative to NILMFormer, BERT4NILM, BiLSTM, and BiGRU. The computational footprint of the framework is 31,009 parameters, the fewest of the five models compared, and on a single CPU thread it needs 2.78 ms per 1000 samples, 84-95% less than the baselines. These results demonstrate a favorable accuracy-efficiency tradeoff for low-frequency smart-meter NILM.

</details>

## 75. Hierarchical Spatiotemporal World Modeling with Content-Adaptive Spatial Addressing

**中文标题**: Hierarchical Spatiotemporal World Modeling with Content-Adaptive Spatial Addressing

**作者**: Tiantian Li, Junfeng Lu, Yifei Wang, Zhaoyang Zhang, Lei Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25610v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25610v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25610v1)

**类别**: cs.NE, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-horizon world modeling in partially observable environments requires compact memories that preserve spatial structure despite accumulated localization drift. Inspired by hippocampal episodic and spatial memory and the multi-periodic coding of entorhinal grid cells, we propose HiSTFormer, a hierarchical spatiotemporal world model, which adopts a layer-wise organization of local temporal RoPE and multi-periodic spatial RoPE, together with content-conditioned address offsets, to capture recent temporal context and long-range spatial structure. A fixed-capacity memory keeps KV storage and per-step attention cost bounded as the sequence grows. On MemoryMaze and HM3D, HiSTFormer outperforms strong world-model baselines within the training sequence length and generalizes substantially better to prediction horizons far beyond those seen during training, while using a compact model. The hierarchical spatiotemporal design remains effective across deeper Transformer stacks, with consistent gains as depth increases. These results show that hierarchical spatiotemporal organization and content-adaptive spatial addressing provide effective inductive biases for efficient long-horizon world modeling.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-horizon world modeling in partially observable environments requires compact memories that preserve spatial structure despite accumulated localization drift. Inspired by hippocampal episodic and spatial memory and the multi-periodic coding of entorhinal grid cells, we propose HiSTFormer, a hierarchical spatiotemporal world model, which adopts a layer-wise organization of local temporal RoPE and multi-periodic spatial RoPE, together with content-conditioned address offsets, to capture recent temporal context and long-range spatial structure. A fixed-capacity memory keeps KV storage and per-step attention cost bounded as the sequence grows. On MemoryMaze and HM3D, HiSTFormer outperforms strong world-model baselines within the training sequence length and generalizes substantially better to prediction horizons far beyond those seen during training, while using a compact model. The hierarchical spatiotemporal design remains effective across deeper Transformer stacks, with consistent gains as depth increases. These results show that hierarchical spatiotemporal organization and content-adaptive spatial addressing provide effective inductive biases for efficient long-horizon world modeling.

</details>
