## 76. From Data Alignment to Parameter Generation: Few-Shot Cross-City Traffic Prediction with Conditional Diffusion

**中文标题**: From Data Alignment to Parameter Generation: Few-Shot Cross-City Traffic Prediction with Conditional Diffusion

**作者**: Shengchun Wang, Yuhang Lin, Tong Li, Pengyuan Zhou, Andy Chi Lok Tai et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-19

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25592v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25592v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25592v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/LingoodBoy/CDPG.

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Accurate mobile traffic forecasting is crucial for network service management, supporting proactive capacity provisioning, congestion mitigation, and energy-efficient operation. However, many cities and regions are data-scarce (e.g., newly deployed or poorly instrumented areas), thus making traffic prediction a few-shot learning problem and limiting the practicality of standard data-driven models. Existing cross-city solutions primarily rely on data-space alignment to match traffic distributions between data-rich and data-scarce cities, but such alignment is often brittle under real-world distribution shifts, measurement noise, and city-specific bias. To address these limitations, we propose a Conditional Diffusion-based Parameter Generation (CDPG) framework. Instead of aligning raw data, CDPG learns the conditional distribution of high-performing predictor parameters for source cities and directly generates node-specific model parameters for the target city. To support reliable adaptation for network traffic prediction, CDPG conditions parameter generation on multi-modal spatio-temporal context. Specifically, CDPG captures spatial environment cues by combining satellite imagery with large language model (LLM)-derived semantic descriptions, and summarizes complex temporal behaviors via unsupervised pattern clustering. These contextual components guide CDPG in producing parameters tailored to each base station's operational context, thereby improving robustness under cross-city and cross-region shifts. Extensive experiments on three large-scale datasets show that CDPG consistently outperforms state-of-the-art cross-city few-shot baselines by up to 9.72% on standard forecasting metrics and remains robust for few-shot multi-step prediction. The code is available at https://github.com/LingoodBoy/CDPG.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

These contextual components guide CDPG in producing parameters tailored to each base station's operational context, thereby improving robustness under cross-city and cross-region shifts. Extensive experiments on three large-scale datasets show that CDPG consistently outperforms state-of-the-art cross-city few-shot baselines by up to 9.72% on standard forecasting metrics and remains robust for few-shot multi-step prediction. The code is available at https://github.com/LingoodBoy/CDPG.

</details>

## 77. MV-JEPA: Post-Training Video Encoders for Cross-View Understanding

**中文标题**: MV-JEPA: Post-Training Video Encoders for Cross-View Understanding

**作者**: Yangchen Zhao, Ali Payani, Caiwen Ding

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25172v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25172v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25172v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

JEPA-based video encoders learn strong representations for video and image understanding through prediction in latent space. However, their standard pretraining predicts features within individual images or video clips, without explicitly exploiting paired camera views of the same object or moment. Consequently, the learned representations may not reliably support matching across different camera viewpoints. To solve this problem, we introduce multi-view predictive post-training to strengthen cross-view instance and temporal correspondence while retaining independent single-view inference. A shared encoder processes masked context views independently, and a training-time predictor jointly predicts latent tokens for masked context regions and a fully held-out view. We alternate cross-view prediction with single-view masked prediction on general image and video data to help retain the encoder's pretrained capabilities. After only 9,000 post-training updates on a single GPU, MV-JEPA improves cross-view instance retrieval and coarse cross-camera moment retrieval on various datasets, with further gains in zero-shot action-phase retrieval and frozen-feature classification on AE2. Frozen-probe evaluations further indicate that post-training largely preserves motion recognition, image recognition, and dense semantic segmentation performance on the evaluated benchmarks. Together, these results demonstrate that multi-view predictive post-training can strengthen instance and coarse temporal correspondence in JEPA video encoders without modifying the encoder architecture or single-view inference interface.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

JEPA-based video encoders learn strong representations for video and image understanding through prediction in latent space. However, their standard pretraining predicts features within individual images or video clips, without explicitly exploiting paired camera views of the same object or moment. Consequently, the learned representations may not reliably support matching across different camera viewpoints. To solve this problem, we introduce multi-view predictive post-training to strengthen cross-view instance and temporal correspondence while retaining independent single-view inference. A shared encoder processes masked context views independently, and a training-time predictor jointly predicts latent tokens for masked context regions and a fully held-out view. We alternate cross-view prediction with single-view masked prediction on general image and video data to help retain the encoder's pretrained capabilities. After only 9,000 post-training updates on a single GPU, MV-JEPA improves cross-view instance retrieval and coarse cross-camera moment retrieval on various datasets, with further gains in zero-shot action-phase retrieval and frozen-feature classification on AE2. Frozen-probe evaluations further indicate that post-training largely preserves motion recognition, image recognition, and dense semantic segmentation performance on the evaluated benchmarks. Together, these results demonstrate that multi-view predictive post-training can strengthen instance and coarse temporal correspondence in JEPA video encoders without modifying the encoder architecture or single-view inference interface.

</details>

---

## 78. CoRelate: Coordinated Human-Object Motion Synthesis through Interaction Relations

**中文标题**: CoRelate: Coordinated Human-Object Motion Synthesis through Interaction Relations

**作者**: Zhiwen Qiu, Daisy Lin, Diyu Zhou, Jialin Wu, Rosita Ji et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25166v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25166v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25166v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Human-object motion synthesis requires coordinated human participation as objects move toward specified goals. However, reaching object targets does not ensure that contact is established and maintained, while adjustments that improve contact may interfere with task completion. We introduce CoRelate, a diffusion framework that coordinates full-body human-object motion through explicit interaction relations and task feedback for rigid and articulated objects. We represent intended participation through hand-part relations that specify the participating hand, its part-relative reference position, and participation interval. These relations serve both as generation conditions and as references for measuring interaction realization. At each denoising step, a temporal refiner trained jointly with the generator combines relation deviations, task residuals, and motion features from the current prediction to jointly update human and object motion across the sequence. After generation, temporal motion correction addresses remaining contact failures by optimizing local motion segments while preserving object trajectories and protected human motion. Experiments demonstrate improved object-goal satisfaction, contact F1, and complete interaction success over the evaluated baselines. Ablation studies support the benefits of relation conditioning and the complementary roles of task and relation feedback.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Human-object motion synthesis requires coordinated human participation as objects move toward specified goals. However, reaching object targets does not ensure that contact is established and maintained, while adjustments that improve contact may interfere with task completion. We introduce CoRelate, a diffusion framework that coordinates full-body human-object motion through explicit interaction relations and task feedback for rigid and articulated objects. We represent intended participation through hand-part relations that specify the participating hand, its part-relative reference position, and participation interval. These relations serve both as generation conditions and as references for measuring interaction realization. At each denoising step, a temporal refiner trained jointly with the generator combines relation deviations, task residuals, and motion features from the current prediction to jointly update human and object motion across the sequence. After generation, temporal motion correction addresses remaining contact failures by optimizing local motion segments while preserving object trajectories and protected human motion. Experiments demonstrate improved object-goal satisfaction, contact F1, and complete interaction success over the evaluated baselines. Ablation studies support the benefits of relation conditioning and the complementary roles of task and relation feedback.

</details>

## 79. GEMS: Generation and Experience Memory for Subgoal-Guided Long-Horizon World Model Planning

**中文标题**: GEMS: Generation and Experience Memory for Subgoal-Guided Long-Horizon World Model Planning

**作者**: Yangchen Zhao, Ali Payani, Caiwen Ding

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25134v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25134v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25134v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Visual world models enable effective short-horizon goal-conditioned planning, such as reaching nearby targets and pushing objects over short distances, by predicting candidate action outcomes and comparing their representations with a goal representation. However, extending this approach to long-horizon tasks is challenging: prediction errors accumulate over extended rollouts, while latent-space similarity to distant goals provides limited guidance for ranking candidate outcomes. Existing methods address these limitations by introducing intermediate subgoals, either generated by learned models or selected from experience memory. Generated subgoals can contain hallucinations or be inaccurate in aspects such as object positions and timing. Memory-based approaches are constrained by experience coverage and may lack precise guidance for selecting appropriate intermediate states from stored observations. We introduce GEMS, which generates temporally structured subgoals to guide action search and state selection from Latent Experience Memory (LEM). A distance gate determines whether predicted outcomes are evaluated against the generated subgoal or the selected memory state. Experiments across six tasks and task suites spanning manipulation, navigation, and reaching demonstrate the effectiveness of GEMS for long-horizon planning. GEMS outperforms state-of-the-art world-model planning baselines across different manipulation settings, achieving success rates of 92.8% on Cube, 68.4% on Push-T, and 54.1% on Meta-World. It improves over the strongest evaluated baseline on these tasks by 24.0, 7.3, and 22.2 percentage points, respectively. These results demonstrate that generated subgoals and experience memory can jointly strengthen long-horizon planning with a frozen world model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Visual world models enable effective short-horizon goal-conditioned planning, such as reaching nearby targets and pushing objects over short distances, by predicting candidate action outcomes and comparing their representations with a goal representation. However, extending this approach to long-horizon tasks is challenging: prediction errors accumulate over extended rollouts, while latent-space similarity to distant goals provides limited guidance for ranking candidate outcomes. Existing methods address these limitations by introducing intermediate subgoals, either generated by learned models or selected from experience memory. GEMS outperforms state-of-the-art world-model planning baselines across different manipulation settings, achieving success rates of 92.8% on Cube, 68.4% on Push-T, and 54.1% on Meta-World. It improves over the strongest evaluated baseline on these tasks by 24.0, 7.3, and 22.2 percentage points, respectively. These results demonstrate that generated subgoals and experience memory can jointly strengthen long-horizon planning with a frozen world model.

</details>

## 80. Gaspard: Expressive unified speech and gesture synthesis with continuous autoregressive flow-matching and decoupled classifier-free guidance

**中文标题**: Gaspard: Expressive unified speech and gesture synthesis with continuous autoregressive flow-matching and decoupled classifier-free guidance

**作者**: Téo Guichoux, Théodor Lemerle, Catherine Pelachaud, Nicolas Obin

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24862v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24862v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24862v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Human communication involves highly coordinated multimodal signals such as speech and co-speech gestures. Existing approaches for generating both modalities commonly follow a cascaded pipeline, where speech is generated first and subsequently used to drive gesture generation; speech generation is therefore independent of gestures. We introduce Gaspard, a unified speech--gesture generation model based on continuous autoregressive generation with next-step flow-matching predictions, and trained on Seamless Interaction, a large-scale multimodal dataset. The heterogeneous temporal properties of speech and gesture motivate modality-specific prediction heads and decoupled classifier-free guidance over the two generation streams. This guidance rebalances the influence of speech and gesture at inference time, improving expressivity while mitigating dynamics collapse in continuous autoregressive gesture generation. Objective and perceptual evaluations show that Gaspard is a strong gesture generation model against unimodal baselines and that it produces highly synchronous speech and gestures compared to a matched cascaded system.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Human communication involves highly coordinated multimodal signals such as speech and co-speech gestures. Existing approaches for generating both modalities commonly follow a cascaded pipeline, where speech is generated first and subsequently used to drive gesture generation; speech generation is therefore independent of gestures. We introduce Gaspard, a unified speech--gesture generation model based on continuous autoregressive generation with next-step flow-matching predictions, and trained on Seamless Interaction, a large-scale multimodal dataset. The heterogeneous temporal properties of speech and gesture motivate modality-specific prediction heads and decoupled classifier-free guidance over the two generation streams. This guidance rebalances the influence of speech and gesture at inference time, improving expressivity while mitigating dynamics collapse in continuous autoregressive gesture generation. Objective and perceptual evaluations show that Gaspard is a strong gesture generation model against unimodal baselines and that it produces highly synchronous speech and gestures compared to a matched cascaded system.

</details>

## 81. A$^3$Bench: Compositional Reasoning over Aggressive Interactions in Videos

**中文标题**: A$^3$Bench: Compositional Reasoning over Aggressive Interactions in Videos

**作者**: Austin C Baker, Raiyaan Abdullah, Daniel Zeev Yoffe, Shruti Vyas, Yogesh S Rawat

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24762v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24762v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24762v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/austincbaker/A3Bench.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved strong performance on general video understanding benchmarks, yet their ability to reason about participant roles in aggressive interactions remains largely unexplored. Existing violence and crime datasets primarily evaluate whether aggression occurs, without testing whether models can identify who performed the action, who received it, and how participants are related within the scene. To address this gap, we introduce A$^3$Bench, a benchmark to study aggressive action and association in videos. The benchmark evaluates whether models can jointly recognize aggressive actions, distinguish aggressors from victims and bystanders, and correctly bind actions to participants under challenging distractor settings. We benchmark 17 multimodal models and find that they struggle substantially on fine-grained aggressive-scene understanding. Performance degrades consistently as reasoning complexity increases, with models frequently confusing aggressors with victims or bystanders, revealing a systematic failure in role-action association rather than coarse aggression recognition alone. We further observe that increasing model scale or generic reasoning models experience limited improvement. Finally, we explore a simple role-graph prompting strategy that encourages structured reasoning over participant interactions, leading to improvements in compositional reasoning. Our findings highlight that reliable aggressive-scene understanding remains a major challenge for current MLLMs and motivate future research on role-aware video reasoning beyond coarse violence detection. Code and dataset: https://github.com/austincbaker/A3Bench.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved strong performance on general video understanding benchmarks, yet their ability to reason about participant roles in aggressive interactions remains largely unexplored. Existing violence and crime datasets primarily evaluate whether aggression occurs, without testing whether models can identify who performed the action, who received it, and how participants are related within the scene. To address this gap, we introduce A$^3$Bench, a benchmark to study aggressive action and association in videos. The benchmark evaluates whether models can jointly recognize aggressive actions, distinguish aggressors from victims and bystanders, and correctly bind actions to participants under challenging distractor settings. We benchmark 17 multimodal models and find that they struggle substantially on fine-grained aggressive-scene understanding. Performance degrades consistently as reasoning complexity increases, with models frequently confusing aggressors with victims or bystanders, revealing a systematic failure in role-action association rather than coarse aggression recognition alone. We further observe that increasing model scale or generic reasoning models experience limited improvement. Finally, we explore a simple role-graph prompting strategy that encourages structured reasoning over participant interactions, leading to improvements in compositional reasoning. Our findings highlight that reliable aggressive-scene understanding remains a major challenge for current MLLMs and motivate future research on role-aware video reasoning beyond coarse violence detection. Code and dataset: https://github.com/austincbaker/A3Bench.

</details>

## 82. HandWorldBench: A Unified Benchmark for Hand-Centric World Models

**中文标题**: HandWorldBench: A Unified Benchmark for Hand-Centric World Models

**作者**: Zhaolu Wang, Jiacheng Xu, Lihong Wang, Shuqing Wang, Jianjie Fang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24731v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24731v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24731v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in world models are opening new possibilities for fine-grained embodied tasks, with hand-object interaction offering a particularly demanding setting. Modeling these interactions involves capturing how hand actions change objects and how the resulting states persist over time. Yet systematic evaluation tailored to these hand-centric capabilities remains underdeveloped. To address this gap, we introduce HandWorldBench, a unified benchmark that systematically evaluates hand-centric world models across human hands, simulated dexterous hands, and real dexterous hands along six dimensions: visual quality, hand fidelity, appearance consistency, instruction following, physical consistency, and temporal coherence. To construct the benchmark, we develop a data curation pipeline that extracts hand-object interaction clips and screens them for quality, yielding 20,500 high-quality egocentric clips from over 600 hours of footage. We then manually select 500 clips covering seven task families for evaluation. Benchmarking eleven models under a unified protocol reveals that stronger hand-pose control does not consistently translate into coherent object-state evolution and persistence or robust cross-embodiment performance, with several specialized hand-control models underperforming general-purpose text/image-to-video generators on dexterous-hand tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent advances in world models are opening new possibilities for fine-grained embodied tasks, with hand-object interaction offering a particularly demanding setting. Modeling these interactions involves capturing how hand actions change objects and how the resulting states persist over time. Yet systematic evaluation tailored to these hand-centric capabilities remains underdeveloped. To address this gap, we introduce HandWorldBench, a unified benchmark that systematically evaluates hand-centric world models across human hands, simulated dexterous hands, and real dexterous hands along six dimensions: visual quality, hand fidelity, appearance consistency, instruction following, physical consistency, and temporal coherence. To construct the benchmark, we develop a data curation pipeline that extracts hand-object interaction clips and screens them for quality, yielding 20,500 high-quality egocentric clips from over 600 hours of footage. We then manually select 500 clips covering seven task families for evaluation. Benchmarking eleven models under a unified protocol reveals that stronger hand-pose control does not consistently translate into coherent object-state evolution and persistence or robust cross-embodiment performance, with several specialized hand-control models underperforming general-purpose text/image-to-video generators on dexterous-hand tasks.

</details>

## 83. H3Remover: Reinforcing Video Object Removal without Paired Data

**中文标题**: H3Remover: Reinforcing Video Object Removal without Paired Data

**作者**: Kangjie Chen, Xiangyu Li, Jinhao Deng, Dongbin Zhang, Chaoda Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24633v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24633v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24633v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Pretrained video editors possess rich removal and completion priors, yet may retain targets or alter unrelated content. We present H3Remover, which reinforces MiniMax-H3 Base Ref2VA for video object removal without paired object-free training targets. To discourage superficial edits such as recoloring, a target-removal reward favors calibrated depth changes consistent with revealing farther content. To limit unintended scene changes, a background-geometry reward encourages preservation of relative depth ordering. To preserve background appearance without retaining unwanted shadows or reflections, an appearance reward constrains RGB changes in source-derived protected regions while exempting targets and estimated effects. Frozen HPSv3 complements these constraints with perceptual-quality feedback for synthesized content. Evaluation regions remain fixed across candidates, and DiffusionART optimizes LoRA adapters with the backbone frozen. Experiments show substantial reconstruction gains over original H3, with qualitative comparisons demonstrating more effective object removal and better preservation of surrounding scene content.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Pretrained video editors possess rich removal and completion priors, yet may retain targets or alter unrelated content. We present H3Remover, which reinforces MiniMax-H3 Base Ref2VA for video object removal without paired object-free training targets. To discourage superficial edits such as recoloring, a target-removal reward favors calibrated depth changes consistent with revealing farther content. To limit unintended scene changes, a background-geometry reward encourages preservation of relative depth ordering. To preserve background appearance without retaining unwanted shadows or reflections, an appearance reward constrains RGB changes in source-derived protected regions while exempting targets and estimated effects. Frozen HPSv3 complements these constraints with perceptual-quality feedback for synthesized content. Evaluation regions remain fixed across candidates, and DiffusionART optimizes LoRA adapters with the backbone frozen. Experiments show substantial reconstruction gains over original H3, with qualitative comparisons demonstrating more effective object removal and better preservation of surrounding scene content.

</details>

## 84. SCRiD: Spatial and Contextual References for Generalizable AI-Generated Video Detection

**中文标题**: SCRiD: Spatial and Contextual References for Generalizable AI-Generated Video Detection

**作者**: Hao Tan, Zekai Zuo, Boyang Chen, Jinghui Qin

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24524v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24524v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24524v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Generalizable AI-generated video detection remains challenging because natural motion and content variations can resemble synthesis artifacts, making isolated video discrepancies unreliable indicators of authenticity. To address this challenge, we propose Spatial and Contextual Reference-informed Detection (SCRiD), a framework that interprets video variations through complementary spatial and contextual references. Specifically, we introduce Matched-Coordinate Residual Response (MCR) to characterize temporal residual associations under motion and coordinate references with matched subpixel sampling conditions. We further develop Real-Conditioned Score Adaptation (RCSA), which learns a content-conditioned denoising reference from authentic videos and probes its responses across multiple noise scales and quality views to guide feature adaptation. To integrate these complementary sources of evidence, we design Reversible Evidence Exchange (REE) to enable information exchange through reversible symplectic updates. We train SCRiD using synthetic videos from only 4 generators and evaluate it across 88 generator or task subsets spanning 6 benchmarks. Our method achieves average ACC and AP of 72.72% and 74.23%, respectively, outperforming the strongest baseline by 3.24 and 0.81 percentage points.Ablation and robustness experiments further validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and the stability of SCRiD under common post-processing operations.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Generalizable AI-generated video detection remains challenging because natural motion and content variations can resemble synthesis artifacts, making isolated video discrepancies unreliable indicators of authenticity. To address this challenge, we propose Spatial and Contextual Reference-informed Detection (SCRiD), a framework that interprets video variations through complementary spatial and contextual references. Specifically, we introduce Matched-Coordinate Residual Response (MCR) to characterize temporal residual associations under motion and coordinate references with matched subpixel sampling conditions. We further develop Real-Conditioned Score Adaptation (RCSA), which learns a content-conditioned denoising reference from authentic videos and probes its responses across multiple noise scales and quality views to guide feature adaptation. To integrate these complementary sources of evidence, we design Reversible Evidence Exchange (REE) to enable information exchange through reversible symplectic updates. We train SCRiD using synthetic videos from only 4 generators and evaluate it across 88 generator or task subsets spanning 6 benchmarks. Our method achieves average ACC and AP of 72.72% and 74.23%, respectively, outperforming the strongest baseline by 3.24 and 0.81 percentage points.Ablation and robustness experiments further validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and the stability of SCRiD under common post-processing operations.

</details>

## 85. Comparing Each Generation with Independent Siblings Gives Video World Model Post-Training a Proper Objective for Outcome Frequencies

**中文标题**: Comparing Each Generation with Independent Siblings Gives Video World Model Post-Training a Proper Objective for Outcome Frequencies

**作者**: Xin Li, Yiwen Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24512v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24512v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24512v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video world models are used as simulators, so when a first frame leaves several physical outcomes open, repeated generations should produce each outcome as often as it occurs in the data. Physics post-training scores every generation against the single recorded future of its history, which makes single videos more faithful, but the expected score is linear in the model distribution and is maximized by always producing the most likely outcome. The log-likelihood of the recorded outcome is maximized by the data distribution, and its gradient weights agreement with the record by the inverse of how often the model produces the generated outcome, a frequency that other generations of the same history with independent noise, its siblings, can estimate. To train with this weight from a finite group, we propose SibPO, which performs Sibling-paired Policy Optimization: siblings estimate a truncated inverse-frequency weight for agreement with the record, and a second term subtracts the same weight times the agreement with the siblings, which makes the expected objective a strictly proper divergence over outcome cells at every group size. On PisaBench, SibPO lowers the KS distance to simulated landing times by 9.1-10.2% relative to the strongest method outside its reward family on three of four video models without degrading single videos, and it lowers the error of estimated robot policy success rates by 13%.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video world models are used as simulators, so when a first frame leaves several physical outcomes open, repeated generations should produce each outcome as often as it occurs in the data. Physics post-training scores every generation against the single recorded future of its history, which makes single videos more faithful, but the expected score is linear in the model distribution and is maximized by always producing the most likely outcome. The log-likelihood of the recorded outcome is maximized by the data distribution, and its gradient weights agreement with the record by the inverse of how often the model produces the generated outcome, a frequency that other generations of the same history with independent noise, its siblings, can estimate. To train with this weight from a finite group, we propose SibPO, which performs Sibling-paired Policy Optimization: siblings estimate a truncated inverse-frequency weight for agreement with the record, and a second term subtracts the same weight times the agreement with the siblings, which makes the expected objective a strictly proper divergence over outcome cells at every group size. On PisaBench, SibPO lowers the KS distance to simulated landing times by 9.1-10.2% relative to the strongest method outside its reward family on three of four video models without degrading single videos, and it lowers the error of estimated robot policy success rates by 13%.

</details>

## 86. Every Query Routes Itself: Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers with Per-Query Routed Sparse Attention

**中文标题**: Every Query Routes Itself: Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers with Per-Query Routed Sparse Attention

**作者**: Zhen Huang, Shuwei Li, Ruizhe Yao, Xinrui Chen, Danyi Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24479v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24479v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24479v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Self-attention over long token sequences dominates the inference cost of video diffusion transformers. Clustering-based sparse attention clusters both queries and keys and lets all queries in a cluster share one selection of key clusters. We show that this sharing limits accuracy: with key clusters and density fixed, attention grows more accurate as the query clusters are refined, and is most accurate when each query selects on its own, which needs no query clustering at all. We therefore propose Per-Query Routed Sparse Attention (PQRS), a training-free method that clusters only keys, lets each query select its own key clusters, and compensates for the unselected ones with their centroids. Per-query selections leave no query tile with a shared key list, so PQRS traverses key clusters instead of queries, buckets queries with identical selections into dense tasks, and merges their partial results in one pass under a fixed softmax offset carried from the previous denoising step. On four text-to-video and image-to-video models from the Wan and HunyuanVideo families, PQRS is the fastest of all the sparse attention methods we evaluate and the closest to the dense output, reaching $28.9$ dB PSNR on Wan2.1-T2V and $35.4$ dB on HunyuanVideo at $1.66$--$2.10\times$ end-to-end speedup.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Self-attention over long token sequences dominates the inference cost of video diffusion transformers. Clustering-based sparse attention clusters both queries and keys and lets all queries in a cluster share one selection of key clusters. We show that this sharing limits accuracy: with key clusters and density fixed, attention grows more accurate as the query clusters are refined, and is most accurate when each query selects on its own, which needs no query clustering at all. We therefore propose Per-Query Routed Sparse Attention (PQRS), a training-free method that clusters only keys, lets each query select its own key clusters, and compensates for the unselected ones with their centroids. Per-query selections leave no query tile with a shared key list, so PQRS traverses key clusters instead of queries, buckets queries with identical selections into dense tasks, and merges their partial results in one pass under a fixed softmax offset carried from the previous denoising step. On four text-to-video and image-to-video models from the Wan and HunyuanVideo families, PQRS is the fastest of all the sparse attention methods we evaluate and the closest to the dense output, reaching $28.9$ dB PSNR on Wan2.1-T2V and $35.4$ dB on HunyuanVideo at $1.66$--$2.10\times$ end-to-end speedup.

</details>

---

## 87. EgoPass-Bench: Identifying Objects That May Affect the Wearer's Passage in Egocentric Video

**中文标题**: EgoPass-Bench: Identifying Objects That May Affect the Wearer's Passage in Egocentric Video

**作者**: Hongyu Wang, Chuhan Li, Congjin Xu, Xuandong Zhao, Hua Shen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24995v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24995v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24995v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding an egocentric environment requires reasoning about how surrounding entities affect the observer's actions. Existing evaluations of multimodal foundation models (MFMs) examine observer localization, motion trajectories, and action feasibility, but leave the discovery and interpretation of evolving observer-entity interactions insufficiently assessed. We introduce EgoPass-Bench, a benchmark for observer-relative interaction understanding in real-world egocentric video. EgoPass-Bench comprises 284 manually reviewed evaluation clips recorded using Ray-Ban Meta (Gen 2) smart glasses and 2,201 distinct questions across five tasks assessing motion perception, interaction judgments about specified entities, and relevant-entity discovery without target cues. Our evaluation of eight frame-based and six native-video configurations reveals a pronounced discrepancy between motion perception and interaction understanding. The strongest evaluated model, GPT-6 Astra, correctly recognizes object motion in 91.37% of 394 comparisons involving the same targets, but correctly identifies changes in passage constraints in only 66.50%. Across all 574 entity-discovery questions, it trails the two-person human baseline by $23.65 \pm 7.45$ percentage points (paired 95% CI), while exceeding the always-empty baseline (36.76%) by at least 18.9 percentage points. Further analysis reveals frequent inclusion of entities that neither pose a plausible physical conflict nor constrain passage, showing that accurate motion recognition does not ensure reliable relevant-entity discovery.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Understanding an egocentric environment requires reasoning about how surrounding entities affect the observer's actions. Existing evaluations of multimodal foundation models (MFMs) examine observer localization, motion trajectories, and action feasibility, but leave the discovery and interpretation of evolving observer-entity interactions insufficiently assessed. We introduce EgoPass-Bench, a benchmark for observer-relative interaction understanding in real-world egocentric video. EgoPass-Bench comprises 284 manually reviewed evaluation clips recorded using Ray-Ban Meta (Gen 2) smart glasses and 2,201 distinct questions across five tasks assessing motion perception, interaction judgments about specified entities, and relevant-entity discovery without target cues. Our evaluation of eight frame-based and six native-video configurations reveals a pronounced discrepancy between motion perception and interaction understanding. The strongest evaluated model, GPT-6 Astra, correctly recognizes object motion in 91.37% of 394 comparisons involving the same targets, but correctly identifies changes in passage constraints in only 66.50%. Across all 574 entity-discovery questions, it trails the two-person human baseline by $23.65 \pm 7.45$ percentage points (paired 95% CI), while exceeding the always-empty baseline (36.76%) by at least 18.9 percentage points. Further analysis reveals frequent inclusion of entities that neither pose a plausible physical conflict nor constrain passage, showing that accurate motion recognition does not ensure reliable relevant-entity discovery.

</details>

## 88. Anchoring with Visual Evidence: Explicit Semantic Anchors for Joint Change Detection and Captioning

**中文标题**: Anchoring with Visual Evidence: Explicit Semantic Anchors for Joint Change Detection and Captioning

**作者**: Kai Zheng, Hang-Cheng Dong, Puyi Fang, Junjia Pan, Zhenkai Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24462v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24462v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24462v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Joint change detection and captioning seeks to localize changes and describe them from bi-temporal remote sensing images. A key challenge is translating dense visual evidence into the semantic conditions that guide description generation. We propose Explicit Semantic Anchors (ESA), a supervised interface that exposes entity presence, temporal appearance variation, and spatial extent. A prediction-guided readout combines visual features and predicted change probabilities, and three factor heads estimate the corresponding anchor activations. These activations guide generation through two complementary routes. The context route maps the three factor activations to three separate memory tokens, one per factor, and appends them to the dense visual memory, while the lexicon route produces an additive vocabulary bias at each decoding step. Dense visual memory remains available for spatial detail and information beyond the explicit factors. Anchor targets are derived from existing images and change annotations, requiring no additional anchor-level language annotation. At inference, all conditions are computed from the input image pair and the model's own predictions. Experiments on LEVIR-MCI and WHU-CDC show strong joint detection and captioning performance, supporting the use of supervised visual attributes as explicit conditions for change description generation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Joint change detection and captioning seeks to localize changes and describe them from bi-temporal remote sensing images. A key challenge is translating dense visual evidence into the semantic conditions that guide description generation. We propose Explicit Semantic Anchors (ESA), a supervised interface that exposes entity presence, temporal appearance variation, and spatial extent. A prediction-guided readout combines visual features and predicted change probabilities, and three factor heads estimate the corresponding anchor activations. These activations guide generation through two complementary routes. The context route maps the three factor activations to three separate memory tokens, one per factor, and appends them to the dense visual memory, while the lexicon route produces an additive vocabulary bias at each decoding step. Dense visual memory remains available for spatial detail and information beyond the explicit factors. Anchor targets are derived from existing images and change annotations, requiring no additional anchor-level language annotation. Experiments on LEVIR-MCI and WHU-CDC show strong joint detection and captioning performance, supporting the use of supervised visual attributes as explicit conditions for change description generation.

</details>

## 89. Probing Physics in Motion Diffusion Models Against Their Own Estimate

**中文标题**: Probing Physics in Motion Diffusion Models Against Their Own Estimate

**作者**: Saeed Ghorbani

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24423v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24423v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24423v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent studies report that video diffusion models "know more than they show": physical properties decode better from their activations than from their inputs or outputs. But a diffusion model computes its clean-sample estimate from those same activations, so such a comparison measures how easily a readout extracts the information. Whether the model hides anything is a separate question. We set out to find hidden physical knowledge in kinematic text-to-motion diffusion models, where the physics of every intermediate estimate can be computed exactly, and recorded every layer along the sampling trajectories of MDM (two checkpoints) and MotionDiffuse. Against the usual baselines the layers do look ahead of the model's own estimate on balance and flight. With every readout tuned the same way, the estimate closes the gap: for balance and flight, adding a layer gains at most 0.01, a size that a positive control shows we would detect. The fall rate of jumps is the one clear exception. Early in denoising, MDM's layers add about 0.08 in $R^2$ (up to 0.11 in a refit) that the estimate does not yet show, and the gain fades as sampling proceeds; on MotionDiffuse it is small and not robust. The estimate also flags floating samples without training, but aborting them saves under a tenth of the compute. In a video model, plausibility that linear probes miss in Wan 2.1's clean latent becomes readable after one fixed nonlinearity.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent studies report that video diffusion models "know more than they show": physical properties decode better from their activations than from their inputs or outputs. But a diffusion model computes its clean-sample estimate from those same activations, so such a comparison measures how easily a readout extracts the information. Whether the model hides anything is a separate question. We set out to find hidden physical knowledge in kinematic text-to-motion diffusion models, where the physics of every intermediate estimate can be computed exactly, and recorded every layer along the sampling trajectories of MDM (two checkpoints) and MotionDiffuse. Against the usual baselines the layers do look ahead of the model's own estimate on balance and flight. The fall rate of jumps is the one clear exception. Early in denoising, MDM's layers add about 0.08 in $R^2$ (up to 0.11 in a refit) that the estimate does not yet show, and the gain fades as sampling proceeds; on MotionDiffuse it is small and not robust. The estimate also flags floating samples without training, but aborting them saves under a tenth of the compute. In a video model, plausibility that linear probes miss in Wan 2.1's clean latent becomes readable after one fixed nonlinearity.

</details>

## 90. PhysResponse: Probing the Physics of Video Generation with Controlled Interventions

**中文标题**: PhysResponse: Probing the Physics of Video Generation with Controlled Interventions

**作者**: Yuhan Li, Shuran Zhang, Haixin Tian, Baonuo Rao, Weipeng Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24419v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24419v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24419v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generation models are increasingly viewed as world simulators, which requires not only plausible motion but dynamics that respond correctly to specified physical parameters. Existing models, datasets and benchmarks, however, largely target physical plausibility, leaving this physical parameter responsiveness insufficiently explored. We introduce PhysResponse, a benchmark that measures it at two levels: scene-conditioned simulator inversion recovers the effective gravity, friction and initial speed of generated motion, and event evaluation tests outcomes near and across critical transitions. Evaluating ten image-to-video models, we find that even the lowest parameter errors exceed those of physically correct videos by over an order of magnitude, that models rarely reproduce both outcomes of a transition pair, and that the two levels expose complementary failures that visual metrics such as PSNR miss. To supply the missing training signal, we construct 72,000 simulated videos of 20 multi-object interaction types with controlled parameters; fine-tuning on them lowers parameter errors, even on held-out interaction types, and transfers to real videos and an external physics benchmark. Continued training with a numerical encoder further lowers all three errors on held-out types. PhysResponse provides a foundation for world simulators that follow physical conditions, not just appearances.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video generation models are increasingly viewed as world simulators, which requires not only plausible motion but dynamics that respond correctly to specified physical parameters. Existing models, datasets and benchmarks, however, largely target physical plausibility, leaving this physical parameter responsiveness insufficiently explored. We introduce PhysResponse, a benchmark that measures it at two levels: scene-conditioned simulator inversion recovers the effective gravity, friction and initial speed of generated motion, and event evaluation tests outcomes near and across critical transitions. Evaluating ten image-to-video models, we find that even the lowest parameter errors exceed those of physically correct videos by over an order of magnitude, that models rarely reproduce both outcomes of a transition pair, and that the two levels expose complementary failures that visual metrics such as PSNR miss. To supply the missing training signal, we construct 72,000 simulated videos of 20 multi-object interaction types with controlled parameters; fine-tuning on them lowers parameter errors, even on held-out interaction types, and transfers to real videos and an external physics benchmark. Continued training with a numerical encoder further lowers all three errors on held-out types. PhysResponse provides a foundation for world simulators that follow physical conditions, not just appearances.

</details>

## 91. Semantic Guidance for Joint Infrared-Visible Video Fusion and Frame Interpolation

**中文标题**: Semantic Guidance for Joint Infrared-Visible Video Fusion and Frame Interpolation

**作者**: Yushen Xu, Xiaosong Li, Huaming Wu, Dongwei Ren

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24277v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24277v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24277v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/XylonXu01/SAFI.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Infrared and visible video fusion at high frame rates requires recovering missing observations when camera rates differ or fall below the desired output rate. Cascading interpolation and fusion can accumulate errors and cause brightness fluctuations. Motion and occlusion further complicate content selection, particularly when missing visible frames also remove direct semantic guidance. We propose SAFI, which uses semantics at the target time to guide joint interpolation and fusion. When the visible frame rate exceeds the infrared rate, available visible frames provide semantic context directly. At times without visible observations, motion guides local matching, and propagation recovers semantics from neighboring frames. Semantic context complements aligned image features at the target time, while matching confidence helps select reliable infrared and visible information. Reconstruction and fusion share source selection within each scale, allowing supervision on missing frames to guide fusion. To preserve visible appearance and reduce flicker, we bound thermal enhancement and penalize brightness inconsistencies across frames while accounting for changes in thermal contrast. Experiments on three datasets show improved visual quality with less computation than cascaded baselines and consistent temporal gains when input rates differ. SAFI maintains strong downstream tracking performance despite missing frames. The code is available at https://github.com/XylonXu01/SAFI.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Infrared and visible video fusion at high frame rates requires recovering missing observations when camera rates differ or fall below the desired output rate. Cascading interpolation and fusion can accumulate errors and cause brightness fluctuations. Motion and occlusion further complicate content selection, particularly when missing visible frames also remove direct semantic guidance. We propose SAFI, which uses semantics at the target time to guide joint interpolation and fusion. When the visible frame rate exceeds the infrared rate, available visible frames provide semantic context directly. At times without visible observations, motion guides local matching, and propagation recovers semantics from neighboring frames. Semantic context complements aligned image features at the target time, while matching confidence helps select reliable infrared and visible information. Reconstruction and fusion share source selection within each scale, allowing supervision on missing frames to guide fusion. To preserve visible appearance and reduce flicker, we bound thermal enhancement and penalize brightness inconsistencies across frames while accounting for changes in thermal contrast. Experiments on three datasets show improved visual quality with less computation than cascaded baselines and consistent temporal gains when input rates differ. SAFI maintains strong downstream tracking performance despite missing frames. The code is available at https://github.com/XylonXu01/SAFI.

</details>

---

## 92. Look Closer, Look Back, or Wait for Agentic Streaming Video Understanding

**中文标题**: Look Closer, Look Back, or Wait for Agentic Streaming Video Understanding

**作者**: Pengyu Li, Zhitao Gao, Lingling Zhang, Wenxuan Huang, YiCheng Xiao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24267v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24267v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24267v2)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Streaming video models must answer questions about a live video under strict memory and latency limits. Feeding only the latest few frames keeps present perception sharp, whereas adding past frames dilutes attention on the current scene and triggers hallucinations. Recent cascade methods retrieve past frames when uncertain, but they still fail when current frames are blurry or when decisive future clues have not appeared yet. We introduce ActiveForager, a streaming video agent that maintains a clean short working window and gathers extra evidence only on demand. For each query, the agent either answers directly, zooms into high-resolution recent frames, recalls past events from memory, or waits for upcoming frames. When recalling history, a shared-weight sub-agent verifies candidate clips in isolation and returns a compact evidence summary. A stream-decoupled attention mask then keeps historical evidence from interfering with present visual tokens. We train this policy using counterfactual information gain, which rewards a tool call only when the gathered evidence makes the true answer more likely and prevents tool collapse across all actions. Across Qwen2.5-VL-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B on OVO-Bench, ActiveForager improves real-time perception, historical recall, and proactive response at once, lifting overall accuracy on Qwen3-VL-8B from 47.6% to 65.9%. It also doubles zero-shot accuracy on the multi-hop Video-Holmes benchmark while adding minimal visual tokens per query.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Streaming video models must answer questions about a live video under strict memory and latency limits. Feeding only the latest few frames keeps present perception sharp, whereas adding past frames dilutes attention on the current scene and triggers hallucinations. A stream-decoupled attention mask then keeps historical evidence from interfering with present visual tokens. We train this policy using counterfactual information gain, which rewards a tool call only when the gathered evidence makes the true answer more likely and prevents tool collapse across all actions. Across Qwen2.5-VL-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B on OVO-Bench, ActiveForager improves real-time perception, historical recall, and proactive response at once, lifting overall accuracy on Qwen3-VL-8B from 47.6% to 65.9%. It also doubles zero-shot accuracy on the multi-hop Video-Holmes benchmark while adding minimal visual tokens per query.

</details>

## 93. Do Thinking VLMs Need to Keep Looking at Long Videos? Write It Down, Then Look Away

**中文标题**: Do Thinking VLMs Need to Keep Looking at Long Videos? Write It Down, Then Look Away

**作者**: Pengyu Li, Zhitao Gao, Lingling Zhang, Heng Wang, Chengqian Ma et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24259v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24259v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24259v2)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language models (VLMs) reason about long videos over hundreds to thousands of tokens. Every decoding step re-reads the key-value (KV) cache of the entire video. Must the model keep looking while it reasons? In LookAway, the model first writes timestamped notes on what the question needs and then puts the video away. Once the notes close, the inference engine hides the video's KV cache for the rest of the response. Across both hybrid and pure-attention VLMs, dependence on the video is concentrated early in the response. On Qwen3.6-27B, writing notes preserves accuracy better than free reasoning at the same withdrawal step. Replacing them with another video's notes harms accuracy, whereas keeping the video visible alongside the model's own notes adds no significant benefit. Without additional training, LookAway nearly matches full-video thinking accuracy at 0.12-0.13 times its modeled decode cost. To shorten this initial look, we fine-tune the model on its own correct LookAway responses. Reinforcement learning then rewards correct answers while penalizing KV reads. The trained model puts the video away after a median of 66-68 tokens. Across three held-out benchmarks, it writes more tokens than an identically trained full-video model yet reads only 27-35% as many KV positions at matched accuracy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language models (VLMs) reason about long videos over hundreds to thousands of tokens. Every decoding step re-reads the key-value (KV) cache of the entire video. Must the model keep looking while it reasons? In LookAway, the model first writes timestamped notes on what the question needs and then puts the video away. Once the notes close, the inference engine hides the video's KV cache for the rest of the response. Across both hybrid and pure-attention VLMs, dependence on the video is concentrated early in the response. On Qwen3.6-27B, writing notes preserves accuracy better than free reasoning at the same withdrawal step. Replacing them with another video's notes harms accuracy, whereas keeping the video visible alongside the model's own notes adds no significant benefit. Without additional training, LookAway nearly matches full-video thinking accuracy at 0.12-0.13 times its modeled decode cost. To shorten this initial look, we fine-tune the model on its own correct LookAway responses. Reinforcement learning then rewards correct answers while penalizing KV reads. The trained model puts the video away after a median of 66-68 tokens. Across three held-out benchmarks, it writes more tokens than an identically trained full-video model yet reads only 27-35% as many KV positions at matched accuracy.

</details>

## 94. Spark-H3: Adaptive Block Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion Models

**中文标题**: Spark-H3: Adaptive Block Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion Models

**作者**: Zecheng Tang, Jiaye Fu, Youtian Lin, Xinyao Rao, Liuxuan Sun et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24256v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24256v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24256v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long video sequences make attention a substantial cost in video diffusion models. Block sparse attention (BSA) reduces this cost by computing selected blocks exactly while compressing the remaining blocks. However, fixed token grouping can place tokens with dissimilar attention preferences in the same group, while uniform key averaging can underestimate the attention mass of compressed blocks. We introduce Spark-Attn, a training-free BSA method comprising Spark-Reblock and Spark-Reweight. Spark-Reblock recursively groups tokens by attention preference while preserving regular block shapes for efficient GPU execution. Spark-Reweight uses the global mean query as an anchor to construct attention-weighted key-value representations and logit biases for the compressed blocks, calibrating their attention mass. We instantiate Spark-Attn in MiniMax-H3 as Spark-H3. At 10% exact-block density, Spark-H3 delivers a 2.49$\times$ DiT speedup and reaches 24.81 dB PSNR against the corresponding dense outputs on MiniMax-H3 (20 denoising steps, 345 frames, 1344$\times$768). Increasing the density to 20% further improves PSNR to 26.76 dB while still achieving a 2.26$\times$ DiT speedup. These results demonstrate that Spark-H3 achieves a state-of-the-art quality-efficiency trade-off for training-free BSA on video diffusion models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long video sequences make attention a substantial cost in video diffusion models. Block sparse attention (BSA) reduces this cost by computing selected blocks exactly while compressing the remaining blocks. However, fixed token grouping can place tokens with dissimilar attention preferences in the same group, while uniform key averaging can underestimate the attention mass of compressed blocks. We introduce Spark-Attn, a training-free BSA method comprising Spark-Reblock and Spark-Reweight. Spark-Reblock recursively groups tokens by attention preference while preserving regular block shapes for efficient GPU execution. Spark-Reweight uses the global mean query as an anchor to construct attention-weighted key-value representations and logit biases for the compressed blocks, calibrating their attention mass. We instantiate Spark-Attn in MiniMax-H3 as Spark-H3. At 10% exact-block density, Spark-H3 delivers a 2.49$\times$ DiT speedup and reaches 24.81 dB PSNR against the corresponding dense outputs on MiniMax-H3 (20 denoising steps, 345 frames, 1344$\times$768). Increasing the density to 20% further improves PSNR to 26.76 dB while still achieving a 2.26$\times$ DiT speedup. These results demonstrate that Spark-H3 achieves a state-of-the-art quality-efficiency trade-off for training-free BSA on video diffusion models.

</details>

## 95. One Shot, Any View: High-Fidelity Video Reshooting with Geometry Guidance and Visual References

**中文标题**: One Shot, Any View: High-Fidelity Video Reshooting with Geometry Guidance and Visual References

**作者**: Kai Huang, Runze Xu, Hui Ren, Taiyi Wu, Xinyuan Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24253v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24253v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24253v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Filming a scene from an additional angle after production usually requires a costly reshoot. We present One Shot Any View, a system that takes a recorded clip and generates a reshot video of the same performance along a user-specified camera trajectory. As in recent geometry-grounded methods, the system conditions generation on the source video and an explicit 3D prior, namely a point video rendered along the requested trajectory from a feed-forward reconstruction of the source video. We target human-centric cinematic videos, which are often shot with long lenses, shallow depth of field, and low-key lighting, all of which degrade feed-forward reconstruction. Existing geometry-grounded methods tend to inherit the degraded point video, and their output quality drops visibly. Two designs make such point videos usable. First, in addition to rendered videos of 3D assets, we train on real-world multi-camera videos, whose point videos are produced by the same reconstruction pipeline as at inference. This training-inference consistency enables the generator to take only the composition of people and scene elements from the point video, and their appearance from the source video. Second, a geometry-anchored RoPE offset keeps the generated video temporally synchronized with the source while largely preventing the output from copying the degraded appearance of the point video. Meanwhile, since the generator is a pretrained reference-to-video (R2V) model, the system also accepts optional character and scene reference images, which supply the appearance of regions that the source never shows. In both objective and human evaluations, One Shot Any View outperforms existing reshooting methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Filming a scene from an additional angle after production usually requires a costly reshoot. We present One Shot Any View, a system that takes a recorded clip and generates a reshot video of the same performance along a user-specified camera trajectory. As in recent geometry-grounded methods, the system conditions generation on the source video and an explicit 3D prior, namely a point video rendered along the requested trajectory from a feed-forward reconstruction of the source video. We target human-centric cinematic videos, which are often shot with long lenses, shallow depth of field, and low-key lighting, all of which degrade feed-forward reconstruction. Existing geometry-grounded methods tend to inherit the degraded point video, and their output quality drops visibly. Two designs make such point videos usable. First, in addition to rendered videos of 3D assets, we train on real-world multi-camera videos, whose point videos are produced by the same reconstruction pipeline as at inference. This training-inference consistency enables the generator to take only the composition of people and scene elements from the point video, and their appearance from the source video. Second, a geometry-anchored RoPE offset keeps the generated video temporally synchronized with the source while largely preventing the output from copying the degraded appearance of the point video. Meanwhile, since the generator is a pretrained reference-to-video (R2V) model, the system also accepts optional character and scene reference images, which supply the appearance of regions that the source never shows. In both objective and human evaluations, One Shot Any View outperforms existing reshooting methods.

</details>

## 96. End-to-End Landsat Land Surface Temperature Reconstruction with AlphaEarth Embeddings

**中文标题**: End-to-End Landsat Land Surface Temperature Reconstruction with AlphaEarth Embeddings

**作者**: Shengjie Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24243v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24243v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24243v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Land surface temperature (LST) data at Landsat's 30 m resolution is important for many applications, but cloud contamination prevents its spatially continuous observations. Here, we propose A$^2$E$^2$, an end-to-end U-Net that integrates annual temperature cycle (ATC), coarse LST from Earth system modeling, and AlphaEarth embeddings for Landsat LST reconstruction. The ATC supplies a physics-guided thermal prior, the coarse LST provides a temporal background, and the U-Net converts the embedding field into day-specific fine-scale correction using observed pixels. Experimental results on four seasonally distributed Landsat 8/9 scenes over New York City in 2022 show that A$^2$E$^2$ achieves 0.78 K mean absolute error and 1.01 K root mean squared error, reducing error by 60.1% relative to baseline method without spatial correction and by 3.7% relative to the same U-Net guided by annual mean spectral reflectance. Sensitivity analysis shows stable performance regardless of distance to clear pixels and percentage of blocked pixels. Independent validation with ECOSTRESS LST shows that Earth foundation model embeddings have better performance than annual mean spectral bands for LST reconstruction.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Land surface temperature (LST) data at Landsat's 30 m resolution is important for many applications, but cloud contamination prevents its spatially continuous observations. Here, we propose A$^2$E$^2$, an end-to-end U-Net that integrates annual temperature cycle (ATC), coarse LST from Earth system modeling, and AlphaEarth embeddings for Landsat LST reconstruction. The ATC supplies a physics-guided thermal prior, the coarse LST provides a temporal background, and the U-Net converts the embedding field into day-specific fine-scale correction using observed pixels. Experimental results on four seasonally distributed Landsat 8/9 scenes over New York City in 2022 show that A$^2$E$^2$ achieves 0.78 K mean absolute error and 1.01 K root mean squared error, reducing error by 60.1% relative to baseline method without spatial correction and by 3.7% relative to the same U-Net guided by annual mean spectral reflectance. Sensitivity analysis shows stable performance regardless of distance to clear pixels and percentage of blocked pixels. Independent validation with ECOSTRESS LST shows that Earth foundation model embeddings have better performance than annual mean spectral bands for LST reconstruction.

</details>

## 97. Some Memories Must Fade: Persistence and Decay of User Memories in Model Parameters

**中文标题**: Some Memories Must Fade: Persistence and Decay of User Memories in Model Parameters

**作者**: Sanidhya Vijayvargiya, Rahul Lokesh

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25235v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25235v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25235v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Parametric memory allows Large Language Models to internalize knowledge directly into their weights, offering a potentially powerful alternative to retrieval-based memory (RAG). However, existing approaches to parametric memory are difficult to deploy as they largely mirror the Continual Learning (CL) objective of retaining information indefinitely. This is neither sustainable nor sufficient for a memory system, which must govern knowledge across its lifecycle by retaining stable facts, reinforcing recurring observations, updating changed beliefs, and allowing transient information to fade. We propose a system for parametric memory management consisting of a memory substrate (handling information storage and forgetting) and a learned policy (what to remember, how deeply, and for how long). Our substrate is a fixed-capacity LoRA adapter that consists of k-hot rank combinations which store facts without catastrophic interference and maintain an independently controlled temporal decay, enabling outdated information to fade while long-term facts persist. On this substrate, we train a lightweight controller that governs each incoming user event to ensure the memory system reflects the user's true, updated belief state. To evaluate how memory systems manage user memory, we introduce a controlled dataset of long-horizon user trajectories with varying persistence of facts based on changing belief states and user-behavioral shifts, and probe queries that test recall while information is valid and abstention after it expires. Our policy learns to effectively manage user memory over its entire lifecycle, balancing recall and abstention to achieve 86% of oracle performance, unlike CL and RAG baselines that primarily default to one of the two behaviors. By providing the first system to manage memory directly in model weights, this work establishes a foundation for long-horizon parametric memory.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Parametric memory allows Large Language Models to internalize knowledge directly into their weights, offering a potentially powerful alternative to retrieval-based memory (RAG). However, existing approaches to parametric memory are difficult to deploy as they largely mirror the Continual Learning (CL) objective of retaining information indefinitely. This is neither sustainable nor sufficient for a memory system, which must govern knowledge across its lifecycle by retaining stable facts, reinforcing recurring observations, updating changed beliefs, and allowing transient information to fade. We propose a system for parametric memory management consisting of a memory substrate (handling information storage and forgetting) and a learned policy (what to remember, how deeply, and for how long). Our substrate is a fixed-capacity LoRA adapter that consists of k-hot rank combinations which store facts without catastrophic interference and maintain an independently controlled temporal decay, enabling outdated information to fade while long-term facts persist. On this substrate, we train a lightweight controller that governs each incoming user event to ensure the memory system reflects the user's true, updated belief state. To evaluate how memory systems manage user memory, we introduce a controlled dataset of long-horizon user trajectories with varying persistence of facts based on changing belief states and user-behavioral shifts, and probe queries that test recall while information is valid and abstention after it expires. Our policy learns to effectively manage user memory over its entire lifecycle, balancing recall and abstention to achieve 86% of oracle performance, unlike CL and RAG baselines that primarily default to one of the two behaviors. By providing the first system to manage memory directly in model weights, this work establishes a foundation for long-horizon parametric memory.

</details>

## 98. WorldRuler: Interactive World Modeling under Configurable Rules

**中文标题**: WorldRuler: Interactive World Modeling under Configurable Rules

**作者**: Mingliang Zhai, Chuanhao Li, Jiaming Tan, Zhen Li, Yuwei Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24869v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24869v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24869v2)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Rules govern how player actions change interactive worlds. Different rules create distinct gameplay experiences within similar scenes and action spaces. For customizable gameplay, generative world models must follow user-defined rules beyond real-world behaviors. However, realizing user-defined rules presents two challenges: Rule-State Persistence and Rule-to-Visual Realization. The former requires preserving rule definitions, dependencies, and world states across saving and loading, so that restored worlds follow the established rules. The latter requires accurately realizing rule-induced changes on designated objects and attributes, particularly when affected objects are unseen. In this paper, we propose WorldRuler, which organizes rule definitions, dependencies, and object states in an executable 3D rule graph and connects rule execution, video generation, and perceptual feedback in a closed loop. The graph supports joint saving and restoration of rules and world states, while dynamic object binding grounds rule-specified entities in visual instances and uses stored states to constrain generation from their first appearance. We further introduce object attribute state replacement and counterfactual reconstruction training, enabling accurate attribute control and compatible appearance and motion changes while limiting interference with unrelated content. We develop an automated data collection and annotation pipeline using GTA-V and Habitat to construct RuleVerse, a dataset of rule-driven interactions with detailed state and event annotations, including cases where rules take effect before affected objects become visible. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of WorldRuler in preserving rule-state consistency across saving and loading and accurately realizing rule outcomes on designated objects and attributes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Rules govern how player actions change interactive worlds. Different rules create distinct gameplay experiences within similar scenes and action spaces. For customizable gameplay, generative world models must follow user-defined rules beyond real-world behaviors. However, realizing user-defined rules presents two challenges: Rule-State Persistence and Rule-to-Visual Realization. The former requires preserving rule definitions, dependencies, and world states across saving and loading, so that restored worlds follow the established rules. The latter requires accurately realizing rule-induced changes on designated objects and attributes, particularly when affected objects are unseen. In this paper, we propose WorldRuler, which organizes rule definitions, dependencies, and object states in an executable 3D rule graph and connects rule execution, video generation, and perceptual feedback in a closed loop. The graph supports joint saving and restoration of rules and world states, while dynamic object binding grounds rule-specified entities in visual instances and uses stored states to constrain generation from their first appearance. We further introduce object attribute state replacement and counterfactual reconstruction training, enabling accurate attribute control and compatible appearance and motion changes while limiting interference with unrelated content. We develop an automated data collection and annotation pipeline using GTA-V and Habitat to construct RuleVerse, a dataset of rule-driven interactions with detailed state and event annotations, including cases where rules take effect before affected objects become visible. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of WorldRuler in preserving rule-state consistency across saving and loading and accurately realizing rule outcomes on designated objects and attributes.

</details>
