## 99. Vibe-Trading: Towards Agent-Native Trading

**中文标题**: Vibe-Trading: Towards Agent-Native Trading

**作者**: Haozhe Wu, Zhonghang Li, Chao Huang

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24845v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24845v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24845v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CL, cs.MA, q-fin.CP, q-fin.TR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Existing AI agents can retrieve information, call tools, run experiments, and produce multi-step outputs. Yet most are built around relatively static workflows and struggle to preserve reliable state as the environment changes. Financial trading makes this limitation especially consequential: prices, news, market conditions, portfolio positions, and available capital evolve continuously. A result is useful only when it remains tied to the correct time, data snapshot, and execution context. We present Vibe-Trading, a comprehensive agent-native trading framework and all-in-one workspace for building, evaluating, and evolving agents for financial trading. It unifies market research, financial data, strategy generation, backtesting, portfolio analysis, paper trading, multi-agent collaboration, and optional broker execution. Its central contribution is an architecture that keeps results, evidence, research history, and trading authority connected throughout the research-to-action process. Each result retains its source, temporal scope, configuration, and producing run. Shared evidence grounding enables verification, versioned research records support reproducible continuation, and an explicit authority gate separates strategy research from live execution. Together, these designs provide a practical foundation for trading agents whose results can be traced, reproduced, and acted on only under explicit authority.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing AI agents can retrieve information, call tools, run experiments, and produce multi-step outputs. Yet most are built around relatively static workflows and struggle to preserve reliable state as the environment changes. Financial trading makes this limitation especially consequential: prices, news, market conditions, portfolio positions, and available capital evolve continuously. A result is useful only when it remains tied to the correct time, data snapshot, and execution context. We present Vibe-Trading, a comprehensive agent-native trading framework and all-in-one workspace for building, evaluating, and evolving agents for financial trading. It unifies market research, financial data, strategy generation, backtesting, portfolio analysis, paper trading, multi-agent collaboration, and optional broker execution. Its central contribution is an architecture that keeps results, evidence, research history, and trading authority connected throughout the research-to-action process. Each result retains its source, temporal scope, configuration, and producing run. Shared evidence grounding enables verification, versioned research records support reproducible continuation, and an explicit authority gate separates strategy research from live execution. Together, these designs provide a practical foundation for trading agents whose results can be traced, reproduced, and acted on only under explicit authority.

</details>

## 100. Horizontal Flow Matching for Single-Cell Spatiotemporal Dynamics

**中文标题**: Horizontal Flow Matching for Single-Cell Spatiotemporal Dynamics

**作者**: Zihan Wang, Jianzhe Li, Zhenyi Zhang, Peijie Zhou

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24764v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24764v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24764v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI, q-bio.QM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning the spatiotemporal dynamics of cells from spatial transcriptomics requires connecting snapshots of different cells acquired in different coordinate frames. A rotation or translation of a section can appear as cell movement and distort the inferred correspondence. We introduce horizontal flow matching, which learns changes in tissue organization, molecular state, and cell abundance up to shared rigid motion. The dynamics follow a continuity--reaction equation whose action assigns no cost to shared rigid motion, and the resulting horizontal projector removes this component from spatial prediction errors during learning and from predicted velocities during generation. An equivalent endpoint problem jointly estimates correspondence and relative pose in Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao geometry and yields analytic paths whose velocity and growth serve as regression targets. Regressing these targets recovers the horizontal dynamics under ideal population conditions. Our model, HORIZON, uses an equivariant transformer to predict velocity and growth from the current tissue, so that a tissue can be generated continuously from a single initial snapshot. On a simulated tissue observed in perturbed coordinate frames, alignment improves reconstruction and projection reduces rigid drift. On four spatial transcriptomics time courses, HORIZON reconstructs tissue geometry, gene expression, and cell abundance more accurately than existing spatial and unbalanced trajectory methods, and interpolates held-out developmental stages.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Learning the spatiotemporal dynamics of cells from spatial transcriptomics requires connecting snapshots of different cells acquired in different coordinate frames. A rotation or translation of a section can appear as cell movement and distort the inferred correspondence. We introduce horizontal flow matching, which learns changes in tissue organization, molecular state, and cell abundance up to shared rigid motion. The dynamics follow a continuity--reaction equation whose action assigns no cost to shared rigid motion, and the resulting horizontal projector removes this component from spatial prediction errors during learning and from predicted velocities during generation. An equivalent endpoint problem jointly estimates correspondence and relative pose in Wasserstein--Fisher--Rao geometry and yields analytic paths whose velocity and growth serve as regression targets. Regressing these targets recovers the horizontal dynamics under ideal population conditions. Our model, HORIZON, uses an equivariant transformer to predict velocity and growth from the current tissue, so that a tissue can be generated continuously from a single initial snapshot. On a simulated tissue observed in perturbed coordinate frames, alignment improves reconstruction and projection reduces rigid drift. On four spatial transcriptomics time courses, HORIZON reconstructs tissue geometry, gene expression, and cell abundance more accurately than existing spatial and unbalanced trajectory methods, and interpolates held-out developmental stages.

</details>

## 101. The Largest Tractable Subclass of Multi-Scale Allen Temporal Reasoning

**中文标题**: The Largest Tractable Subclass of Multi-Scale Allen Temporal Reasoning

**作者**: Quentin Cohen-Solal

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24498v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24498v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24498v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In this paper, we propose a novel model for temporal reasoning over multi-scale intervals. We show that this model yields a complete reasoning framework and exhibits other particularly interesting properties. Furthermore, we identify the maximal tractable subalgebra for this model, as well as for the other complete model, in the qualitative scales context, which exhibits weaker properties but whose results can be derived by transfer from our new model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In this paper, we propose a novel model for temporal reasoning over multi-scale intervals. We show that this model yields a complete reasoning framework and exhibits other particularly interesting properties. Furthermore, we identify the maximal tractable subalgebra for this model, as well as for the other complete model, in the qualitative scales context, which exhibits weaker properties but whose results can be derived by transfer from our new model.

</details>

## 102. MINT: Meeting-time Indicators for Truncation in Multi-Step Off-Policy RL

**中文标题**: MINT: Meeting-time Indicators for Truncation in Multi-Step Off-Policy RL

**作者**: Seungyub Han, Taehyun Cho, Dohyeong Kim, Kyungjae Lee, Jungwoo Lee

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24413v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24413v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24413v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Many multi-step off-policy temporal-difference methods handle policy mismatch through products of per-step trace coefficients, leading to a trade-off between variance control and long-horizon signal. The maximum trace length consequently becomes a sensitive hyperparameter. We propose MINT (Meeting-time INdicators for Truncation), which replaces these products with a binary stopping indicator: one until coupled behavior and target trajectories first meet, zero thereafter. At the true value function, the indicator's idempotence yields a variance bound governed by meeting-time survival. For practical implementation, a single sampled action provides a model-free surrogate for the stopping rule, requiring no transition model or mixing-time estimate. We derive separate sufficient conditions for contraction of the state-coupled operator and its practical surrogate under fixed-policy evaluation. On MuJoCo and DeepMind Control tasks, MINT outperforms the reported multi-step baselines; the main MuJoCo comparison uses a common cap without per-task cap-length tuning. We also demonstrate the effectiveness of MINT on Atari 100k, RWRL, and OGBench experiments; MINT adapts the backup horizon to reduce target variability relative to long fixed-horizon backups across tasks and dynamics.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The maximum trace length consequently becomes a sensitive hyperparameter. We propose MINT (Meeting-time INdicators for Truncation), which replaces these products with a binary stopping indicator: one until coupled behavior and target trajectories first meet, zero thereafter. At the true value function, the indicator's idempotence yields a variance bound governed by meeting-time survival. For practical implementation, a single sampled action provides a model-free surrogate for the stopping rule, requiring no transition model or mixing-time estimate. We derive separate sufficient conditions for contraction of the state-coupled operator and its practical surrogate under fixed-policy evaluation. On MuJoCo and DeepMind Control tasks, MINT outperforms the reported multi-step baselines; the main MuJoCo comparison uses a common cap without per-task cap-length tuning. We also demonstrate the effectiveness of MINT on Atari 100k, RWRL, and OGBench experiments; MINT adapts the backup horizon to reduce target variability relative to long fixed-horizon backups across tasks and dynamics.

</details>

## 103. VideoACE: Benchmarking Agentic Video Understanding through High-Frame-Rate Actions

**中文标题**: VideoACE: Benchmarking Agentic Video Understanding through High-Frame-Rate Actions

**作者**: Luoyuan Zhang, Xuanyu Zheng, Yancheng Long, Haoyang Xu, Bin Wen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24184v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24184v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24184v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Agentic video understanding requires models to actively interact with video environments to gather evidence and form judgments. Evaluating this capability therefore requires assessing whether models can translate opportunities for interaction into better understanding. We introduce VideoACE, a benchmark for assessing video understanding under conditions that support sustained interaction. We focus on high-frame-rate action videos, where rapid, subtle changes make targeted observation especially valuable. The benchmark comprises 1,000 multiple-choice questions spanning six categories of fine-grained action understanding, accompanied by temporal evidence annotations and user hints screened for answer leakage. Its evaluation environment combines video inspection tools with a user simulator, allowing models to adjust temporal coverage, sampling density, and spatial detail, and to revisit evidence and revise their judgments under simulated user feedback. We evaluate single-pass observation, autonomous tool use, and tool use combined with user feedback to examine how effectively models exploit these opportunities. Evaluation of five models reveals distinct patterns in how they acquire evidence and respond to opportunities for further interaction. These findings establish VideoACE as a testbed for examining agentic video understanding beyond performance under a fixed observation setting.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Agentic video understanding requires models to actively interact with video environments to gather evidence and form judgments. Evaluating this capability therefore requires assessing whether models can translate opportunities for interaction into better understanding. We introduce VideoACE, a benchmark for assessing video understanding under conditions that support sustained interaction. We focus on high-frame-rate action videos, where rapid, subtle changes make targeted observation especially valuable. The benchmark comprises 1,000 multiple-choice questions spanning six categories of fine-grained action understanding, accompanied by temporal evidence annotations and user hints screened for answer leakage. Its evaluation environment combines video inspection tools with a user simulator, allowing models to adjust temporal coverage, sampling density, and spatial detail, and to revisit evidence and revise their judgments under simulated user feedback. We evaluate single-pass observation, autonomous tool use, and tool use combined with user feedback to examine how effectively models exploit these opportunities. Evaluation of five models reveals distinct patterns in how they acquire evidence and respond to opportunities for further interaction. These findings establish VideoACE as a testbed for examining agentic video understanding beyond performance under a fixed observation setting.

</details>

## 104. A Synthetic Multi-Modal Testbed for Security Stress Testing of Agentic Counter-UAS Systems

**中文标题**: A Synthetic Multi-Modal Testbed for Security Stress Testing of Agentic Counter-UAS Systems

**作者**: Ivan A. Fernandez, Jingdao Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25249v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25249v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25249v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Agentic cyber-physical systems rely on perception and state estimation to support autonomous decisions. Degraded, inconsistent, or manipulated sensor observations can therefore propagate through an autonomous pipeline and create security-relevant failures. This work presents a synthetic Counter-Unmanned Aircraft System (C-UAS) testbed developed in NVIDIA Isaac Sim for repeatable stress testing of perception and tracking algorithms supporting agentic decision-making. A simplified quadrotor follows scripted trajectories while co-located virtual sensors generate synchronized RGB, RTX LiDAR, and illustrative IR-like observations. The testbed varies target range, motion, clutter, occlusion, and sensor availability, with future stress cases including sensor dropout, temporal misalignment, cross-modal disagreement, and false observations. As an initial exemplar, a nonlinear coordinated-turn Extended Kalman Filter (EKF) is evaluated over a 20-s maneuvering trajectory using LiDAR target-centroid measurements. Position error and uncertainty are compared as LiDAR observations degrade and are lost. The framework provides a reproducible environment for studying how sensing degradation affects downstream autonomous estimation and resilience in agentic C-UAS systems.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Agentic cyber-physical systems rely on perception and state estimation to support autonomous decisions. Degraded, inconsistent, or manipulated sensor observations can therefore propagate through an autonomous pipeline and create security-relevant failures. This work presents a synthetic Counter-Unmanned Aircraft System (C-UAS) testbed developed in NVIDIA Isaac Sim for repeatable stress testing of perception and tracking algorithms supporting agentic decision-making. A simplified quadrotor follows scripted trajectories while co-located virtual sensors generate synchronized RGB, RTX LiDAR, and illustrative IR-like observations. The testbed varies target range, motion, clutter, occlusion, and sensor availability, with future stress cases including sensor dropout, temporal misalignment, cross-modal disagreement, and false observations. As an initial exemplar, a nonlinear coordinated-turn Extended Kalman Filter (EKF) is evaluated over a 20-s maneuvering trajectory using LiDAR target-centroid measurements. Position error and uncertainty are compared as LiDAR observations degrade and are lost. The framework provides a reproducible environment for studying how sensing degradation affects downstream autonomous estimation and resilience in agentic C-UAS systems.

</details>

## 105. Verify2Act: Critic-Guided Latent World Models for Verifying Language-Conditioned Manipulation Plans

**中文标题**: Verify2Act: Critic-Guided Latent World Models for Verifying Language-Conditioned Manipulation Plans

**作者**: Chrisantus Eze, Christopher Crick

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25243v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25243v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25243v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) can propose multi-step manipulation plans, but they have no grounded model of how a scene will change, and the pixel-level world models that could check those plans are slow and drift over long horizons. We present Verify2Act, a modular verification framework that separates semantic action proposal from latent physical verification. A VLM proposes candidate plans, a latent world model (V2A-WM) imagines each of them in the DINOv2 feature space, and a dual-head critic checks the imagined rollouts for temporal consistency and goal satisfaction before anything is executed. V2A-WM extends recent flow-matching world models with cross-attention action grounding and a causal temporal history window. We deploy the full system on a low-cost robot arm, training the world model and critic only on data generated in a digital twin. Across six language-conditioned tasks, Verify2Act reaches 83.3\% task success, compared with 61.7\% for a latent world model without our architectural changes, 53.3\% for the VLM alone, and 46.7\% for a pixel-diffusion world model. It verified every plan it executed and incurred no planning-induced failures: all of its failed episodes were caused by low-level grasp or placement slips. In simulation, Verify2Act obtains the highest five-task chain success on CALVIN among the compared baselines with 30$\times$ fewer reflective VLM calls than pixel-diffusion verification, and the highest success on cluttered nut assembly.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) can propose multi-step manipulation plans, but they have no grounded model of how a scene will change, and the pixel-level world models that could check those plans are slow and drift over long horizons. We present Verify2Act, a modular verification framework that separates semantic action proposal from latent physical verification. A VLM proposes candidate plans, a latent world model (V2A-WM) imagines each of them in the DINOv2 feature space, and a dual-head critic checks the imagined rollouts for temporal consistency and goal satisfaction before anything is executed. V2A-WM extends recent flow-matching world models with cross-attention action grounding and a causal temporal history window. We deploy the full system on a low-cost robot arm, training the world model and critic only on data generated in a digital twin. Across six language-conditioned tasks, Verify2Act reaches 83.3\% task success, compared with 61.7\% for a latent world model without our architectural changes, 53.3\% for the VLM alone, and 46.7\% for a pixel-diffusion world model. It verified every plan it executed and incurred no planning-induced failures: all of its failed episodes were caused by low-level grasp or placement slips. In simulation, Verify2Act obtains the highest five-task chain success on CALVIN among the compared baselines with 30$\times$ fewer reflective VLM calls than pixel-diffusion verification, and the highest success on cluttered nut assembly.

</details>

## 106. Phoenix: Learning Scene Structure Representations for Generalizable World-Action Models

**中文标题**: Phoenix: Learning Scene Structure Representations for Generalizable World-Action Models

**作者**: Yupeng Zheng, Chaoyue Li, Linbo Wang, Xiang Li, Yuhang Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24938v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24938v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24938v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World-action models jointly predict future observations and actions, yet reconstruction-oriented visual representations do not explicitly emphasize the spatial relationships and object-state changes needed for manipulation. We introduce Phoenix, which learns representations of scene structure for world-action modeling. Specifically, Phoenix Encoder enriches DINOv3's general visual semantics through dual-teacher distillation of geometric and spatiotemporal semantic knowledge. Next, CS-VAE causally aggregates and compresses Phoenix Encoder features into generative latents while preserving scene information and spatial layout. Finally, Phoenix jointly predicts these latents, complementary appearance latents, and robot actions, linking scene evolution with action generation. To study pretraining data efficiency, we curate approximately 39,000 hours of egocentric and robot manipulation data for representation learning and use the robot demonstration subset for WAM pretraining. Following benchmark-specific post-training, Phoenix achieves state-of-the-art performance across simulation and real-robot benchmarks spanning single-arm, bimanual, and dexterous manipulation. It demonstrates strong robustness to perturbations, achieving success rates of 94.8% on LIBERO-Plus and 72.4% on RoboTwin 2.0 C2R, alongside strong cross-embodiment transfer and real-world manipulation performance on Bench2Dex and RoboDojo-RealWorld, respectively. Pretraining-scale analysis shows larger performance gains from data scaling than a video-VAE-only baseline, indicating stronger scaling benefits and more effective data utilization. Moreover, Phoenix maintains high success rates when adapted with limited downstream demonstrations, demonstrating strong few-shot adaptation. Our code, pretraining data, and models are available on the project page to help the community reproduce our results.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce Phoenix, which learns representations of scene structure for world-action modeling. Following benchmark-specific post-training, Phoenix achieves state-of-the-art performance across simulation and real-robot benchmarks spanning single-arm, bimanual, and dexterous manipulation. It demonstrates strong robustness to perturbations, achieving success rates of 94.8% on LIBERO-Plus and 72.4% on RoboTwin 2.0 C2R, alongside strong cross-embodiment transfer and real-world manipulation performance on Bench2Dex and RoboDojo-RealWorld, respectively. Pretraining-scale analysis shows larger performance gains from data scaling than a video-VAE-only baseline, indicating stronger scaling benefits and more effective data utilization. Moreover, Phoenix maintains high success rates when adapted with limited downstream demonstrations, demonstrating strong few-shot adaptation. Our code, pretraining data, and models are available on the project page to help the community reproduce our results.

</details>

## 107. Single-Controller Teleoperation and Multimodal Dataset Generation for a Mobile Manipulator

**中文标题**: Single-Controller Teleoperation and Multimodal Dataset Generation for a Mobile Manipulator

**作者**: Fardien Azizi, Sitor Sla, Christiaan Peppelman, Dion van Santen, Andrei-Carlo Papuc et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24499v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24499v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24499v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robot learning systems require synchronized, high-quality demonstrations that combine visual, proprioceptive, and action data. However, collecting such datasets with mobile manipulators is often slowed down by fragmented teleoperation interfaces and manual post-processing workflows. This paper presents an integrated framework for teleoperated multimodal data collection on a Clearpath Dingo-O mobile base equipped with a Kinova Gen3 Lite manipulator and an Intel RealSense RGB-D camera. The framework combines single-controller operation of the base, arm, and recording process with an automated ROS-based pipeline for data capture, image-quality filtering, temporal synchronization, visualization, and export to machine-learning-ready Zarr datasets. We validate the system on a pick-and-place mobile-manipulation task in which the robot transports a bottle between two surfaces. The results show that the unified controller supports complete task execution and recording without external commands, while the data pipeline produces synchronized datasets for short demonstrations and identifies corrupted visual frames. The proposed framework provides a practical basis for producing structured demonstrations for robot-learning research.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robot learning systems require synchronized, high-quality demonstrations that combine visual, proprioceptive, and action data. However, collecting such datasets with mobile manipulators is often slowed down by fragmented teleoperation interfaces and manual post-processing workflows. This paper presents an integrated framework for teleoperated multimodal data collection on a Clearpath Dingo-O mobile base equipped with a Kinova Gen3 Lite manipulator and an Intel RealSense RGB-D camera. The framework combines single-controller operation of the base, arm, and recording process with an automated ROS-based pipeline for data capture, image-quality filtering, temporal synchronization, visualization, and export to machine-learning-ready Zarr datasets. We validate the system on a pick-and-place mobile-manipulation task in which the robot transports a bottle between two surfaces. The results show that the unified controller supports complete task execution and recording without external commands, while the data pipeline produces synchronized datasets for short demonstrations and identifies corrupted visual frames. The proposed framework provides a practical basis for producing structured demonstrations for robot-learning research.

</details>

## 108. Remote Teleoperation of Endovascular Robots: A Network Emulation Framework for Systematic Performance Evaluation

**中文标题**: Remote Teleoperation of Endovascular Robots: A Network Emulation Framework for Systematic Performance Evaluation

**作者**: Xingyu Chen, Nikola Fischer, Harry Robertshaw, Weiyuan Deng, Christos Bergeles et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24280v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24280v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24280v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Remote endovascular teleoperation requires network performance to be evaluated in relation to physical navigation and operator experience, while the effects of network degradation on the usability of remote endovascular systems remain insufficiently characterized from the perspective of clinical users. We developed a network emulation and benchmarking framework that combines an endovascular robot with data streaming. We conducted a user study involving eight clinicians from interventional neuroradiology and general interventional radiology and eight engineering students, who performed 480 navigation attempts across 15 network conditions. Communication performance and received video quality were assessed alongside navigation outcomes, confidence, workload and clinician rated acceptability. Under the most impaired combined condition, success was 78.1\%, compared with 93.8\% under the reference condition, and mean attempt time increased from 63.5 to 94.3\,s. At 5\% configured packet loss, 87.5\% of attempts succeeded, although all eight clinicians rated the condition unacceptable. Perceived video quality was associated with clinician confidence and acceptability, with mean within participant Spearman correlations of 0.72 and 0.78, respectively. Framework integration on a second robotic platform additionally supported successful remote navigation over a real network. Our framework provides a reusable basis that can help identify communication bottlenecks and assess their effects on navigation and clinician acceptance for endovascular intervention telesurgery tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 109. Assessing Vision-Language Models for Early Prediction of Unsafe Situations in Robotics

**中文标题**: Assessing Vision-Language Models for Early Prediction of Unsafe Situations in Robotics

**作者**: Muhammad Farooq, Aitor Arrieta, Paolo Arcaini, Shaukat Ali, Thomas Peyrucain

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24146v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24146v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24146v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.SE

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) process visual information and textual instructions and, by using their ability to understand and reason about context, are well-suited for predicting robot failures before they occur. However, evidence supporting this remains limited. To address this, we present an empirical evaluation of VLMs for predicting robot failures. In particular, we experiment with Qwen, InternVL, and LLaVA-OneVision across three robotic case studies (HuRoN, Pal Robotics, and CrowdBot), analyzing both video and odometry data for early failure warning (i.e., before an unsafe situation occurs). Specifically, we evaluate their performance in classifying situations as safe, potentially unsafe, and unsafe, together with uncertainty quantified using various metrics, and study the relationship between uncertainty and performance. Our results showed that Qwen provides the strongest overall classification and prediction-reliability performance, while InternVL shows stronger early-warning capability in several configurations. The results further show that the effect of robot-state information and temporal history depends on the model and evaluation objective. Overall, our findings indicate that general-purpose VLMs can support early robot-safety monitoring, but the most suitable configuration depends on whether the priority is accurate assessment of safety-state, reliable confidence and uncertainty, or timely warning before unsafe events.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-Language Models (VLMs) process visual information and textual instructions and, by using their ability to understand and reason about context, are well-suited for predicting robot failures before they occur. However, evidence supporting this remains limited. To address this, we present an empirical evaluation of VLMs for predicting robot failures. In particular, we experiment with Qwen, InternVL, and LLaVA-OneVision across three robotic case studies (HuRoN, Pal Robotics, and CrowdBot), analyzing both video and odometry data for early failure warning (i.e., before an unsafe situation occurs). Specifically, we evaluate their performance in classifying situations as safe, potentially unsafe, and unsafe, together with uncertainty quantified using various metrics, and study the relationship between uncertainty and performance. Our results showed that Qwen provides the strongest overall classification and prediction-reliability performance, while InternVL shows stronger early-warning capability in several configurations. The results further show that the effect of robot-state information and temporal history depends on the model and evaluation objective. Overall, our findings indicate that general-purpose VLMs can support early robot-safety monitoring, but the most suitable configuration depends on whether the priority is accurate assessment of safety-state, reliable confidence and uncertainty, or timely warning before unsafe events.

</details>

## 110. SemPhys: Semantic-Physical Co-Learning for Humanoid Motion Generation via Differentiable World Models

**中文标题**: SemPhys: Semantic-Physical Co-Learning for Humanoid Motion Generation via Differentiable World Models

**作者**: Zejian Kang, Yeqiang Wang, Zekai Chen, Boyang Shen, Mingyu Ma et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24074v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24074v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24074v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In human-robot collaboration, natural language lets people direct a humanoid's whole-body movements without programming joint trajectories. A common approach generates a kinematic reference from an instruction and relies on a tracking controller to execute it. However, as the controller maintains balance under contact constraints, the executed motion can depart from the reference in ways that change the action it conveys. Our central insight is that semantic alignment must be evaluated directly on the complete motion the robot physically executes. Building on this insight, we introduce \textbf{SemPhys}, a framework that trains motion generators using semantic feedback from simulated executions. A differentiable world model and a fixed tracking controller predict a short segment of a recorded execution. SemPhys inserts this prediction at its original position, scores the complete trajectory against the instruction, and backpropagates through the predicted segment. A paired anchoring loss stabilizes adaptation by keeping each generated reference close to its counterpart from the initial generator. On BONES-SEED, SemPhys largely preserves instruction retrieval accuracy from reference to execution and achieves the highest retrieval accuracy on executed motions among the evaluated methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In human-robot collaboration, natural language lets people direct a humanoid's whole-body movements without programming joint trajectories. A common approach generates a kinematic reference from an instruction and relies on a tracking controller to execute it. However, as the controller maintains balance under contact constraints, the executed motion can depart from the reference in ways that change the action it conveys. Our central insight is that semantic alignment must be evaluated directly on the complete motion the robot physically executes. Building on this insight, we introduce \textbf{SemPhys}, a framework that trains motion generators using semantic feedback from simulated executions. A differentiable world model and a fixed tracking controller predict a short segment of a recorded execution. SemPhys inserts this prediction at its original position, scores the complete trajectory against the instruction, and backpropagates through the predicted segment. On BONES-SEED, SemPhys largely preserves instruction retrieval accuracy from reference to execution and achieves the highest retrieval accuracy on executed motions among the evaluated methods.

</details>

## 111. LaST1.0: A Unified Latent World-Action Model with Control-Centric SpatioTemporal Reasoning

**中文标题**: LaST1.0: A Unified Latent World-Action Model with Control-Centric SpatioTemporal Reasoning

**作者**: Hao Chen, Xuheng Zhang, Zhuangzhe Wu, Jiaming Liu, Shaobo Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.23974v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23974v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23974v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World-Action Models couple action learning with future prediction, enabling robotic policies to capture environmental dynamics for robust control. However, prevailing methods take a reconstruction-oriented approach to model future videos, allocating substantial capacity to appearance details that are incidental to control. In this work, we reformulate world modeling for manipulation as control-centric future prediction in latent space, and present LaST1.0, a unified latent World-Action Model. Rather than modeling an observation-complete video future, LaST1.0 predicts compact, geometry-aware latent targets derived from future wrist views, focusing the supervision on the evolving robot-object interactions required for fine-grained manipulation. To better support this control-centric paradigm, LaST1.0 revisits the visual input interface and attention mechanism of its unified architecture. It adopts a vision-encoder-free design with a lightweight patch embedder, exposing fine-grained visual cues directly to the backbone, and introduces LaST-Attention for structured spatiotemporal interactions across modalities. Evaluations across two robot embodiments, five challenging manipulation tasks, and three simulation benchmarks---RLBench, LIBERO, and LIBERO-Plus---demonstrate that LaST1.0 achieves state-of-the-art success rates and generalizes to novel scenarios. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of control-centric latent world modeling for robust manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World-Action Models couple action learning with future prediction, enabling robotic policies to capture environmental dynamics for robust control. However, prevailing methods take a reconstruction-oriented approach to model future videos, allocating substantial capacity to appearance details that are incidental to control. In this work, we reformulate world modeling for manipulation as control-centric future prediction in latent space, and present LaST1.0, a unified latent World-Action Model. Rather than modeling an observation-complete video future, LaST1.0 predicts compact, geometry-aware latent targets derived from future wrist views, focusing the supervision on the evolving robot-object interactions required for fine-grained manipulation. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of control-centric latent world modeling for robust manipulation.

</details>

## 112. TacPilot: Does Touch Need to Be Everywhere?

**中文标题**: TacPilot: Does Touch Need to Be Everywhere?

**作者**: Yunzhen Zhang, Yuxiao Zhu, Jie Yang, Yufeng Miao, Bing Luo

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.23804v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23804v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23804v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Tactile feedback helps visual manipulation policies respond to local contact, but changes in tactile observations do not always require corrective motion, and sustained corrections do not always require new tactile features. We introduce TacPilot, a tactile residual framework that separates representation updates from action intervention around a frozen visual policy. TacPilot uses a perception gate to determine when tactile features need refreshing and updates only affected regions while reusing unchanged features. A separate intervention gate determines whether bounded Cartesian and gripper residuals modify the nominal visual action. This separation allows tactile perception and action correction to follow different spatial and temporal demands during manipulation. In offline expert-trajectory replay, local feature reuse reduces encoded regions by 56.4% relative to periodic full encoding, with essentially unchanged action predictions. In an exploratory simulation evaluation spanning two cylinder sizes, TacPilot succeeds in 26 of 30 trials (86.7%). We further validate TacPilot on OpenArm v2 equipped with Paxini tactile sensors across four contact-rich manipulation tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce TacPilot, a tactile residual framework that separates representation updates from action intervention around a frozen visual policy. TacPilot uses a perception gate to determine when tactile features need refreshing and updates only affected regions while reusing unchanged features. A separate intervention gate determines whether bounded Cartesian and gripper residuals modify the nominal visual action. This separation allows tactile perception and action correction to follow different spatial and temporal demands during manipulation. In offline expert-trajectory replay, local feature reuse reduces encoded regions by 56.4% relative to periodic full encoding, with essentially unchanged action predictions. In an exploratory simulation evaluation spanning two cylinder sizes, TacPilot succeeds in 26 of 30 trials (86.7%). We further validate TacPilot on OpenArm v2 equipped with Paxini tactile sensors across four contact-rich manipulation tasks.

</details>

## 113. ZERITH V2.5: From Predictive Power to Policy Learnability

**中文标题**: ZERITH V2.5: From Predictive Power to Policy Learnability

**作者**: Chenxi Han,  Ruby,  Epoch,  Orion,  Luke et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.23746v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23746v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23746v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

When humans learn to manipulate an unfamiliar object, they may already be able to anticipate how its state will change while still being unable to control it skillfully. This gap between prediction and control motivates us to study how the priors acquired by video generation models can be used effectively by an action policy. We present ZERITH V2.5, a video-action model (VAM) in which the action policy reads intermediate features of a video generation model, and use it to study, under fixed demonstrations and a fixed total number of updates, what kind of predictive training improves subsequent action learning.   We find that the way video predictive training is supervised affects subsequent action learning. In our experiments, adapting at many noise levels yields a large gain, while adapting only at the noise level used at deployment yields none. Weight-transfer experiments further show that the gain stays with the adapted weights when they remain in the video generation model and disappears when they are used only to initialize the action policy. Initial loss and gradient measurements at the start of joint training indicate that target-domain predictive training gives the video model a better starting state, making subsequent joint learning of prediction and action easier. Under matched data and training budgets, predictive adaptation improves control. We evaluate the complete policy on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, Meta-World MT50, and real-robot tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

When humans learn to manipulate an unfamiliar object, they may already be able to anticipate how its state will change while still being unable to control it skillfully. This gap between prediction and control motivates us to study how the priors acquired by video generation models can be used effectively by an action policy. We present ZERITH V2.5, a video-action model (VAM) in which the action policy reads intermediate features of a video generation model, and use it to study, under fixed demonstrations and a fixed total number of updates, what kind of predictive training improves subsequent action learning.   We find that the way video predictive training is supervised affects subsequent action learning. In our experiments, adapting at many noise levels yields a large gain, while adapting only at the noise level used at deployment yields none. Weight-transfer experiments further show that the gain stays with the adapted weights when they remain in the video generation model and disappears when they are used only to initialize the action policy. Initial loss and gradient measurements at the start of joint training indicate that target-domain predictive training gives the video model a better starting state, making subsequent joint learning of prediction and action easier. Under matched data and training budgets, predictive adaptation improves control. We evaluate the complete policy on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, Meta-World MT50, and real-robot tasks.

</details>

## 114. Temporal Extrapolation of Elastic Seismic Wave Propagation in Layered Media Using Neural Operators

**中文标题**: Temporal Extrapolation of Elastic Seismic Wave Propagation in Layered Media Using Neural Operators

**作者**: Dibyajyoti Nayak, Yuze Pu, Kami Mohammadi, Somdatta Goswami

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25130v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25130v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25130v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, physics.geo-ph

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Temporal prediction of seismic velocity fields beyond the time interval represented in training remains challenging for neural operator models. This study develops a coupled neural operator framework for stress-conditioned temporal prediction in layered elastic media. A time-integrator-embedded Deep Operator Network (TI-DeepONet) advances the velocity field through an embedded fourth-order Runge-Kutta (RK4) scheme, while a Fourier Neural Operator (FNO) learns a residual correction to the predicted spatial field. The two components are trained jointly in a predictor-corrector configuration. Prediction is initialized with reference velocity fields at the first two time levels and proceeds autoregressively, with reference stress fields and prescribed excitation supplied throughout rollout, including beyond the training interval. Four cases consider horizontally polarized shear (SH) waves in four-layer soil columns with fixed total depths of 50 and 280 m, excited by third-order Gaussian-derivative (DOG3) wavelets with central frequencies between 0.5 and 6 Hz. Layer thicknesses and shear-wave velocities vary across profiles. Evaluations on test profiles extend beyond the training interval by up to twice its duration. The predictions reproduce principal velocity arrivals and depth-dependent response patterns, with accuracy varying across profiles. Across the four cases, mean peak-normalized root-mean-square errors (NRMSEs) range from 0.53% to 2.31% over the full evaluation windows and from 0.61% to 2.82% over the extrapolation intervals. Both metrics use each profile's peak absolute reference velocity over the full evaluation window as the normalizer. These results demonstrate temporal velocity prediction under reference-stress conditioning for the tested configurations; autonomous continuation of the coupled velocity-stress wavefield remains to be established.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The predictions reproduce principal velocity arrivals and depth-dependent response patterns, with accuracy varying across profiles. Across the four cases, mean peak-normalized root-mean-square errors (NRMSEs) range from 0.53% to 2.31% over the full evaluation windows and from 0.61% to 2.82% over the extrapolation intervals. Both metrics use each profile's peak absolute reference velocity over the full evaluation window as the normalizer. These results demonstrate temporal velocity prediction under reference-stress conditioning for the tested configurations; autonomous continuation of the coupled velocity-stress wavefield remains to be established.

</details>

## 115. Empirical Variational Bayes Factorization for Longitudinal Matrix Data

**中文标题**: Empirical Variational Bayes Factorization for Longitudinal Matrix Data

**作者**: Jiakun Li, Eric F. Lock

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.25065v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.25065v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.25065v1)

**类别**: stat.ML, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Collecting high-dimensional data over time for multiple individuals is increasingly common in biomedical research, particularly in studies of disease progression or molecular time-course experiments. Often data are missing or the timepoints are irregularly spaced across individuals. There is a lack of flexible methodology to explore latent patterns of change over time and account for missing data. We propose an empirical variational Bayes (EVB) approach to factorize high-dimensional longitudinal data, in which the underlying factors have temporal correlation over time. The EVB approach is computationally efficient and accommodates flexible covariance structures over time with automatic determination of all hyper-parameters (including the underlying rank, i.e., number of factors). Simulations demonstrate that the approach captures the underlying signal more precisely than other strategies, and performs well with respect to missing data imputation under different scenarios. An application to a gene time-course data for multiple sclerosis treatment provides a factorization with interpretable components over time and improved imputation accuracy for missing data.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Collecting high-dimensional data over time for multiple individuals is increasingly common in biomedical research, particularly in studies of disease progression or molecular time-course experiments. Often data are missing or the timepoints are irregularly spaced across individuals. There is a lack of flexible methodology to explore latent patterns of change over time and account for missing data. We propose an empirical variational Bayes (EVB) approach to factorize high-dimensional longitudinal data, in which the underlying factors have temporal correlation over time. The EVB approach is computationally efficient and accommodates flexible covariance structures over time with automatic determination of all hyper-parameters (including the underlying rank, i.e., number of factors). Simulations demonstrate that the approach captures the underlying signal more precisely than other strategies, and performs well with respect to missing data imputation under different scenarios. An application to a gene time-course data for multiple sclerosis treatment provides a factorization with interpretable components over time and improved imputation accuracy for missing data.

</details>

## 116. Cap-and-Track: Optimizing LLM Inference Request Dispatch Across Shifting Capacity

**中文标题**: Cap-and-Track: Optimizing LLM Inference Request Dispatch Across Shifting Capacity

**作者**: Benjamin Novotny, Doug Szeto, Chang Wang, Shicheng Chen, George Zhihua Yuan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24891v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24891v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24891v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A fixed dispatch rate for LLM inference becomes stale as serving fleets resize and request sizes change. Generative output length is unknown at admission, and shared provider pools can lose capacity. We present Cap-and-Track, a design for asynchronous inference across provider capacity and internal GPUs. Meta's traffic shaper implements Cap-and-Track for post and live-video understanding. Refusals signal the backend's current admission ceiling. Durable priority queues hold excess work outside execution, including refused requests for retry. Cap is a fail-fast admission check at the saturated resource that refuses work before a long wait. Using only those refusals, the Track dispatcher probes upward and backs off. It follows the ceiling without output-length prediction or a fleet-level rate to retune. In a concurrent same-traffic production A/B, the shaper ran per-replica Cap plus Track. It served a classifier on 43% fewer H100s than its queued, fixed-rate precursor, though lower-priority work waited longer at backfill peaks. In steady-capacity real-engine replay, Track ran within 1% of the goodput of a fixed rate tuned above measured capacity, with about a quarter of that rate's refusal share. The shaper carries more than 300 million online requests and hundreds of terabytes of media a day. During a backfill, it handled 1.1 billion requests in its busiest 24 hours. Lessons include keeping queues inside inference engines just long enough to fill batches, steering dispatch by capacity refusals alone, keeping dispatch stateful, and sizing fleets by how long low-priority work can wait.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

A fixed dispatch rate for LLM inference becomes stale as serving fleets resize and request sizes change. Generative output length is unknown at admission, and shared provider pools can lose capacity. We present Cap-and-Track, a design for asynchronous inference across provider capacity and internal GPUs. Meta's traffic shaper implements Cap-and-Track for post and live-video understanding. Refusals signal the backend's current admission ceiling. Durable priority queues hold excess work outside execution, including refused requests for retry. Cap is a fail-fast admission check at the saturated resource that refuses work before a long wait. Using only those refusals, the Track dispatcher probes upward and backs off. It follows the ceiling without output-length prediction or a fleet-level rate to retune. In a concurrent same-traffic production A/B, the shaper ran per-replica Cap plus Track. It served a classifier on 43% fewer H100s than its queued, fixed-rate precursor, though lower-priority work waited longer at backfill peaks. In steady-capacity real-engine replay, Track ran within 1% of the goodput of a fixed rate tuned above measured capacity, with about a quarter of that rate's refusal share. The shaper carries more than 300 million online requests and hundreds of terabytes of media a day. During a backfill, it handled 1.1 billion requests in its busiest 24 hours. Lessons include keeping queues inside inference engines just long enough to fill batches, steering dispatch by capacity refusals alone, keeping dispatch stateful, and sizing fleets by how long low-priority work can wait.

</details>

## 117. Reconstruction vs. Prediction Pre-Training of Discrete Video Representations for Continuous Control

**中文标题**: Reconstruction vs. Prediction Pre-Training of Discrete Video Representations for Continuous Control

**作者**: Ron Kosova, Francesco Croce

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24781v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24781v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24781v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Self-supervised video encoders are commonly trained to either reconstruct pixels or predict latent features, yet it remains unclear how these objectives shape representations for downstream dynamics. We present a capacity-matched study isolating this choice: two identical video encoders with a shared FSQ bottleneck are trained under autoencoding and V-JEPA objectives on a continuous control environment. We introduce a simple asymmetric discretization scheme to apply the same bottleneck to the predictive model. The resulting representations exhibit a clear trade-off: the autoencoder preserves substantially more physical state information and achieves slightly better one-step latent prediction, while the predictive encoder accumulates less error under autoregressive rollout. Structural probes suggest that this advantage is associated with greater robustness to low-level visual perturbations and stronger dependence on temporal context. These results indicate that predictive and reconstructive objectives induce distinct representations of dynamics, trading state fidelity for properties that may benefit long-horizon prediction.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Self-supervised video encoders are commonly trained to either reconstruct pixels or predict latent features, yet it remains unclear how these objectives shape representations for downstream dynamics. We present a capacity-matched study isolating this choice: two identical video encoders with a shared FSQ bottleneck are trained under autoencoding and V-JEPA objectives on a continuous control environment. We introduce a simple asymmetric discretization scheme to apply the same bottleneck to the predictive model. The resulting representations exhibit a clear trade-off: the autoencoder preserves substantially more physical state information and achieves slightly better one-step latent prediction, while the predictive encoder accumulates less error under autoregressive rollout. Structural probes suggest that this advantage is associated with greater robustness to low-level visual perturbations and stronger dependence on temporal context. These results indicate that predictive and reconstructive objectives induce distinct representations of dynamics, trading state fidelity for properties that may benefit long-horizon prediction.

</details>

## 118. DyNA-Mixer: Decoupling Time and Topology for Scalable Dynamic Node Affinity Prediction

**中文标题**: DyNA-Mixer: Decoupling Time and Topology for Scalable Dynamic Node Affinity Prediction

**作者**: Yuanxing Zheng, Liping Wang, Xuemin Lin

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24426v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24426v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24426v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Continuous-time dynamic graphs preserve the ordering, timing, and evolving strength of interactions that static snapshots discard, making dynamic graph mining essential for evolving systems such as recommender systems, trade networks, and transaction platforms. Research in this area has centered on dynamic link prediction, which forecasts only whether a future interaction will occur, whereas many applications also need its intensity. The dynamic node affinity prediction (DNAP) problem generalizes this task to continuous targets and full-candidate ranking, making it more broadly applicable but substantially harder. This raises two coupled difficulties: ranking requires a predictor expressive enough to capture both multi-scale temporal evolution and graph-wide collaborative patterns, yet running such a predictor over every source--destination pair is computationally prohibitive. We propose DyNA-Mixer, which decouples temporal forecasting from collaborative structure modeling. It adopts a multi-scale temporal predictor for pair-specific affinity evolution, constructs a Virtual Collaborative Graph that infers query-time relationships and aggregates trends from related interactions, and fuses the two through a gated residual mechanism. A sparsity-aware evaluation strategy further restricts expensive forecasting to pairs with recent non-zero affinities. Experiments on four Temporal Graph Benchmark datasets for the DNAP problem show that DyNA-Mixer achieves new state-of-the-art NDCG@10 on every dataset, including a 17.8\% relative test improvement over the strongest baseline on tgbn-token, the largest dataset.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Continuous-time dynamic graphs preserve the ordering, timing, and evolving strength of interactions that static snapshots discard, making dynamic graph mining essential for evolving systems such as recommender systems, trade networks, and transaction platforms. Research in this area has centered on dynamic link prediction, which forecasts only whether a future interaction will occur, whereas many applications also need its intensity. The dynamic node affinity prediction (DNAP) problem generalizes this task to continuous targets and full-candidate ranking, making it more broadly applicable but substantially harder. This raises two coupled difficulties: ranking requires a predictor expressive enough to capture both multi-scale temporal evolution and graph-wide collaborative patterns, yet running such a predictor over every source--destination pair is computationally prohibitive. We propose DyNA-Mixer, which decouples temporal forecasting from collaborative structure modeling. It adopts a multi-scale temporal predictor for pair-specific affinity evolution, constructs a Virtual Collaborative Graph that infers query-time relationships and aggregates trends from related interactions, and fuses the two through a gated residual mechanism. A sparsity-aware evaluation strategy further restricts expensive forecasting to pairs with recent non-zero affinities. Experiments on four Temporal Graph Benchmark datasets for the DNAP problem show that DyNA-Mixer achieves new state-of-the-art NDCG@10 on every dataset, including a 17.8\% relative test improvement over the strongest baseline on tgbn-token, the largest dataset.

</details>

## 119. Temporal Rank in Recurrent Neural Networks

**中文标题**: Temporal Rank in Recurrent Neural Networks

**作者**: Leo Dobson, Anton Ragni

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24394v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24394v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24394v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) are a natural framework for temporal modelling, yet are often dismissed due to their sequential, non-parallelisable computation and difficulty in capturing long-range dependencies, typically attributed to vanishing and exploding gradients. We identify a distinct and under-explored limitation: an information bottleneck induced by rank-constrained hidden state dynamics, which limits the number of independent input directions that can be preserved over time, as reflected in the rank of the input-to-state mapping. We show that this constraint restricts temporal capacity independently of gradient stability. To address this, we introduce structured high-dimensional state dynamics that preserve temporal separability and prevent saturation of the input-to-state Jacobian over time. On controlled sequence tasks, the resulting model exhibits rapid learning despite remaining strictly within a standard RNN formulation. Our results demonstrate that RNN performance is governed by the evolution and preservation of temporal rank, and suggest that mitigating gradient pathologies alone is insufficient without explicitly maintaining temporal capacity.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recurrent Neural Networks (RNNs) are a natural framework for temporal modelling, yet are often dismissed due to their sequential, non-parallelisable computation and difficulty in capturing long-range dependencies, typically attributed to vanishing and exploding gradients. We identify a distinct and under-explored limitation: an information bottleneck induced by rank-constrained hidden state dynamics, which limits the number of independent input directions that can be preserved over time, as reflected in the rank of the input-to-state mapping. We show that this constraint restricts temporal capacity independently of gradient stability. To address this, we introduce structured high-dimensional state dynamics that preserve temporal separability and prevent saturation of the input-to-state Jacobian over time. On controlled sequence tasks, the resulting model exhibits rapid learning despite remaining strictly within a standard RNN formulation. Our results demonstrate that RNN performance is governed by the evolution and preservation of temporal rank, and suggest that mitigating gradient pathologies alone is insufficient without explicitly maintaining temporal capacity.

</details>

## 120. Exploring Daily Emotional Well-Being through Personalized Data Analysis

**中文标题**: Exploring Daily Emotional Well-Being through Personalized Data Analysis

**作者**: Adriana Rodríguez Rodríguez

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24342v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24342v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24342v1)

**类别**: cs.HC, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This project analysed daily emotional well-being from a personalized perspective using records of emotional states and contextual information derived from daily life. It was based on the idea that well-being is not a fixed value, but varies from day to day, and can be better understood in relation to individual context and behaviour. The work is framed within the field of Data Science and focuses on emotional states at an individual level. The project focused on constructing an integrated daily dataset from multiple personal data sources and analysing emotional states. The distributions of emotional variables, their temporal evolution and the differences between users were studied, together with their variability over time. Personality and well-being questionnaires were also incorporated as stable references to better contextualize daily emotional results. A simple personalized recommendation approach was then developed by comparing similar days within each user, exploring how past contextual conditions relate to differences in the user's emotional state. Finally, machine learning techniques were used to evaluate whether daily contextual information contains signals that help distinguish between different types of days relative to a person's usual well-being. The project adopted an applied approach, with an exploratory scope focused on understanding patterns rather than building a fully predictive system. The results showed that emotional well-being is very personal and changes from day to day. General models had limited value, while personalized approaches captured these differences much better, highlighting the importance of analysing each person on their own terms.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

It was based on the idea that well-being is not a fixed value, but varies from day to day, and can be better understood in relation to individual context and behaviour. ## 121. Distribution-Free Uncertainty Quantification for Weather Forecasting: Conformal Prediction Under Spatiotemporal Dependence

**中文标题**: Distribution-Free Uncertainty Quantification for Weather Forecasting: Conformal Prediction Under Spatiotemporal Dependence

**作者**:  Mariya

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24329v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24329v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24329v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Conformal prediction gives finite-sample, distribution-free marginal coverage and has begun to be applied to gridded weather forecasts. Marginal coverage concerns the long-run fraction of covered points at a location, not how coverage behaves jointly across space and time on a given day. I benchmark eight interval-construction procedures (Gaussian, pooled and per-cell split conformal, per-season, adaptive conformal inference and rolling windows) for gridded 2 m temperature forecasts at eight lead times from 6 hours to 10 days, using ERA5 at 5.625 degree resolution, a deliberately modest convolutional forecaster and ECMWF's operational HRES, with calibration in 2017-2018 and testing in 2019-2021. Alongside coverage, width and interval score, I report dependence diagnostics: a spatial overdispersion index, quantiles of daily spatial coverage and miss persistence relative to a shuffled-days null. Per-cell methods reach near-nominal marginal coverage, yet the day-to-day variance of the fraction of cells covered is roughly 3 to 10 times what independent cells would give, and misses cluster in time; both effects grow with lead time for the CNN, also appear for HRES, and are shared by all methods. An oracle that divides each day's normalised errors by that day's mean removes 81 to 92 percent of the excess dispersion for the CNN and 93 to 98 percent for HRES, so days of large errors across much of the grid account for most of it. A day-scale predicted from seasonal terms and already-verified errors lowers the overdispersion index without widening intervals but improves interval score by at most 0.6 percent; on HRES from 12 hours to 3 days it has the lowest overdispersion index while ACI or season-conditional calibration has the best interval score, so no method is best on both. The study covers one variable, one coarse grid, a reanalysis target and two forecasters.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Conformal prediction gives finite-sample, distribution-free marginal coverage and has begun to be applied to gridded weather forecasts. Marginal coverage concerns the long-run fraction of covered points at a location, not how coverage behaves jointly across space and time on a given day. ## 122. Stability-compatible kernels: beyond Gaussianity for impulse response estimation

**中文标题**: Stability-compatible kernels: beyond Gaussianity for impulse response estimation

**作者**: Gianluigi Pillonetto, Mauro Bisiacco

**发布日期**: 2026-11-18

**arXiv ID**: [2611.24272v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.24272v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.24272v1)

**类别**: stat.ML, cs.LG, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Kernel-based regularization for linear system identification uses positive semidefinite kernels both to define reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces (RKHSs) and as covariance models for stochastic impulse responses. Sharp kernel conditions are known for RKHS stability, while almost-sure BIBO stability under Gaussian priors requires an even more restrictive covariance condition. These restrictions, however, need not persist beyond Gaussianity, where the covariance no longer determines the full probability law. We introduce the notion of a stability-compatible kernel, namely a covariance kernel admitting at least one stochastic realization with almost-surely absolutely summable sample paths. We show that stability-compatibility holds if and only if the stable elements are dense in the associated RKHS, a condition strictly weaker than RKHS stability, and derive practical sufficient conditions, including a spectral criterion for stationary kernels. We then construct non-Gaussian priors that preserve a prescribed covariance while enforcing almost-sure stability. In particular, this yields a stable prior based on the stationary radial-basis-function (RBF) kernel, separating covariance structure from the mechanism enforcing stability. Numerical experiments show substantial estimation gains for several important classes of impulse responses, with particularly pronounced advantages when relevant dynamics occur at late lags or in separated temporal regions.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Kernel-based regularization for linear system identification uses positive semidefinite kernels both to define reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces (RKHSs) and as covariance models for stochastic impulse responses. Sharp kernel conditions are known for RKHS stability, while almost-sure BIBO stability under Gaussian priors requires an even more restrictive covariance condition. These restrictions, however, need not persist beyond Gaussianity, where the covariance no longer determines the full probability law. We introduce the notion of a stability-compatible kernel, namely a covariance kernel admitting at least one stochastic realization with almost-surely absolutely summable sample paths. We show that stability-compatibility holds if and only if the stable elements are dense in the associated RKHS, a condition strictly weaker than RKHS stability, and derive practical sufficient conditions, including a spectral criterion for stationary kernels. We then construct non-Gaussian priors that preserve a prescribed covariance while enforcing almost-sure stability. In particular, this yields a stable prior based on the stationary radial-basis-function (RBF) kernel, separating covariance structure from the mechanism enforcing stability. Numerical experiments show substantial estimation gains for several important classes of impulse responses, with particularly pronounced advantages when relevant dynamics occur at late lags or in separated temporal regions.

</details>

---

## 123. Streaming Generative Video Compression with Embedding Alignment and In-Context Propagation

**中文标题**: Streaming Generative Video Compression with Embedding Alignment and In-Context Propagation

**作者**: Zhitao Wang, Hengyu Man, Han Liu, Wenrui Li, Debin Zhao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-11-17

**arXiv ID**: [2611.23352v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23352v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23352v1)

**项目主页**: https://chigland.github.io/SGVC/

**类别**: eess.IV, cs.CV, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video compression at ultra-low bitrates presents substantial challenges in preserving perceptual quality. Although pretrained video diffusion transformers (DiTs) provide powerful generative priors, existing generative video compression (GVC) methods built on them typically rely on computationally expensive VAE decoders to reconstruct video frames. Moreover, jointly refining multiple frames to exploit the inherent spatiotemporal modeling capabilities of video DiTs introduces additional buffering delay. To address these challenges, we propose SGVC, a streaming generative video compression framework that adapts a pretrained latent video DiT into a causal pixel-space refiner. Specifically, SGVC projects decoded features from a contextual pixel codec directly into the DiT input embedding space. To bridge the representation gap between codec-derived embeddings and pretrained DiT embeddings, we introduce Embedding Alignment, which aligns the projected embeddings with input embeddings extracted from the original frames during training. The refined DiT hidden states are then mapped directly to the pixel domain through a lightweight reconstruction path, bypassing the pretrained VAE decoder. To further exploit the spatiotemporal priors of the video DiT while maintaining causal decoding, we introduce In-Context Propagation, which reuses layer-wise K/V states across frames to exploit historical generative context, improving temporal consistency without requiring clip-wise buffering. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SGVC achieves competitive rate-perception performance, with average BD-rate savings of 71.97% over S$^2$VC in terms of DISTS. Project Page: https://chigland.github.io/SGVC/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video compression at ultra-low bitrates presents substantial challenges in preserving perceptual quality. Although pretrained video diffusion transformers (DiTs) provide powerful generative priors, existing generative video compression (GVC) methods built on them typically rely on computationally expensive VAE decoders to reconstruct video frames. Moreover, jointly refining multiple frames to exploit the inherent spatiotemporal modeling capabilities of video DiTs introduces additional buffering delay. To address these challenges, we propose SGVC, a streaming generative video compression framework that adapts a pretrained latent video DiT into a causal pixel-space refiner. Specifically, SGVC projects decoded features from a contextual pixel codec directly into the DiT input embedding space. To bridge the representation gap between codec-derived embeddings and pretrained DiT embeddings, we introduce Embedding Alignment, which aligns the projected embeddings with input embeddings extracted from the original frames during training. The refined DiT hidden states are then mapped directly to the pixel domain through a lightweight reconstruction path, bypassing the pretrained VAE decoder. To further exploit the spatiotemporal priors of the video DiT while maintaining causal decoding, we introduce In-Context Propagation, which reuses layer-wise K/V states across frames to exploit historical generative context, improving temporal consistency without requiring clip-wise buffering. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SGVC achieves competitive rate-perception performance, with average BD-rate savings of 71.97% over S$^2$VC in terms of DISTS. Project Page: https://chigland.github.io/SGVC/

</details>

## 124. Satisfiability Modulo Theories for Robust Synthesis of Signal Temporal Logic Specifications

**中文标题**: Satisfiability Modulo Theories for Robust Synthesis of Signal Temporal Logic Specifications

**作者**: Philip Smith, Kevin Leahy

**发布日期**: 2026-11-17

**arXiv ID**: [2611.23021v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23021v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2611.23021v1)

**类别**: eess.SY, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Signal Temporal Logic is a popular framework for describing a robotic system's goals. Once described using a temporal logic framework, synthesis turns the goals into actual steps for the robots to follow. Currently, the state of the art for sound and complete synthesis is using Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP), which struggles on long time horizons and large environments, preventing the construction of guaranteed safe plans for long deployments. This paper introduces a new synthesis method using Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT) which scales better for long deployments and adapts to environmental changes more quickly. We do this by introducing an encoding method for STL into SMT constraints, and an encoding method for discrete-time dynamical systems modeled as a transition system. We then conduct an empirical comparison of our SMT based methods against the state of the art MILP based methods. Results show that for this class of problems, SMT is an effective encoding strategy that performs the best on long time horizon tasks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Signal Temporal Logic is a popular framework for describing a robotic system's goals. Once described using a temporal logic framework, synthesis turns the goals into actual steps for the robots to follow. Currently, the state of the art for sound and complete synthesis is using Mixed-Integer Linear Programming (MILP), which struggles on long time horizons and large environments, preventing the construction of guaranteed safe plans for long deployments. This paper introduces a new synthesis method using Satisfiability Modulo Theories (SMT) which scales better for long deployments and adapts to environmental changes more quickly. We do this by introducing an encoding method for STL into SMT constraints, and an encoding method for discrete-time dynamical systems modeled as a transition system. We then conduct an empirical comparison of our SMT based methods against the state of the art MILP based methods. Results show that for this class of problems, SMT is an effective encoding strategy that performs the best on long time horizon tasks.

</details>
